Implement the following plan:

# Benchmark: Session Start Hook Performance

## Context

A customer reports that the `entire hooks claude-code session-start` hook significantly slows down Claude Code startup. We need a benchmark that reproduces the customer-facing latency (full process spawn) to establish a baseline for optimization.

## Approach

### End-to-End Subprocess Benchmark

**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/hook_bench_test.go`
**Build tag:** `//go:build integration`

Uses the existing integration test infrastructure (`getTestBinary()`, `TestMain` binary build, `benchutil.NewBenchRepo`) to measure real-world latency of the session-start hook.

**Scenarios:**
- `SessionStart/NoSessions` — Clean repo, no prior sessions (best case)
- `SessionStart/1Session` — 1 existing session state file
- `SessionStart/5Sessions` — 5 session state files (typical user)
- `SessionStart/20Sessions` — 20 session state files (heavy user, worst case)

Each benchmark:
1. Creates a `BenchRepo` via `benchutil.NewBenchRepo()`
2. Creates N session state files via `benchutil.CreateSessionState()`
3. Pipes `{"session_id":"bench-session","transcript_path":""}` to stdin
4. Invokes `getTestBinary() hooks claude-code session-start` as a subprocess
5. Measures wall-clock time (what the customer experiences)

**Key patterns to reuse:**
- `benchutil.NewBenchRepo()` from `cmd/entire/cli/benchutil/benchutil.go` — creates isolated repos
- `benchutil.CreateSessionState()` — creates session state files
- `getTestBinary()` from `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/testenv.go` — shared pre-built binary
- `HookRunner.runHookWithOutput()` pattern from `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/hooks.go` — subprocess invocation with stdin

### File to Create

`cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/hook_bench_test.go`

### How to Run

```bash
# End-to-end subprocess benchmarks (build binary + measure real latency)
go test -tags=integration -bench=BenchmarkHookSessionStart -benchtime=10x -run='^$' -timeout=10m ./cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/...
```

### Verification

1. `mise run fmt && mise run lint` — passes lint
2. `mise run test:ci` — no regressions
3. Run benchmarks and verify output shows timing data per scenario

    32→// testBinaryPath holds the path to the CLI binary built once in TestMain.
    33→// All tests share this binary to avoid repeated builds.
    34→var testBinaryPath string
    35→
    36→// getTestBinary returns the path to the shared test binary.
    37→// It panics if TestMain hasn't run (testBinaryPath is empty).
    38→func getTestBinary() string {
    39→	if testBinaryPath == "" {
    40→		panic("testBinaryPath not set - TestMain must run before tests")
    41→	}
    42→	return testBinaryPath
    43→}
    44→
    45→// TestEnv manages an isolated test environment for integration tests.
    46→type TestEnv struct {
    47→	T                  *testing.T
    48→	RepoDir            string
    49→	ClaudeProjectDir   string
    50→	GeminiProjectDir   string
    51→	OpenCodeProjectDir string
    52→	SessionCounter     int
    53→}
    54→
    55→// NewTestEnv creates a new isolated test environment.
    56→// It creates temp directories for the git repo and agent project files.
    57→// Note: Does NOT change working directory to allow parallel test execution.
    58→// Note: Does NOT use t.Setenv to allow parallel test execution - CLI commands
    59→// receive the env var via cmd.Env instead.
    60→func NewTestEnv(t *testing.T) *TestEnv {
    61→	t.Helper()
    62→
    63→	// Resolve symlinks on macOS where /var -> /private/var
    64→	// This ensures the CLI subprocess and test use consistent paths
    65→	repoDir := t.TempDir()
    66→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(repoDir); err == nil {
    67→		repoDir = resolved
    68→	}
    69→	claudeProjectDir := t.TempDir()
    70→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(claudeProjectDir); err == nil {
    71→		claudeProjectDir = resolved
    72→	}
    73→	geminiProjectDir := t.TempDir()
    74→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(geminiProjectDir); err == nil {
    75→		geminiProjectDir = resolved
    76→	}
    77→	openCodeProjectDir := t.TempDir()
    78→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(openCodeProjectDir); err == nil {
    79→		openCodeProjectDir = resolved
    80→	}
    81→
    82→	env := &TestEnv{
    83→		T:                  t,
    84→		RepoDir:            repoDir,
    85→		ClaudeProjectDir:   claudeProjectDir,
    86→		GeminiProjectDir:   geminiProjectDir,
    87→		OpenCodeProjectDir: openCodeProjectDir,
    88→	}
    89→
    90→	// Note: Don't use t.Setenv here - it's incompatible with t.Parallel()
    91→	// CLI commands receive ENTIRE_TEST_*_PROJECT_DIR via cmd.Env instead
    92→
    93→	return env
    94→}
    95→
    96→// Cleanup is a no-op retained for backwards compatibility.
    97→//
    98→// Previously this method restored the working directory after NewTestEnv changed it.
    99→// With the refactor to remove os.Chdir from NewTestEnv:
   100→// - Temp directories are now cleaned up automatically by t.TempDir()
   101→// - Working directory is never changed, so no restoration is needed
   102→//
   103→// This method is kept to avoid breaking existing tests that call defer env.Cleanup().
   104→// New tests should not call this method as it serves no purpose.
   105→//
   106→// Deprecated: This method is a no-op and will be removed in a future version.
   107→func (env *TestEnv) Cleanup() {
   108→	// No-op - temp dirs are cleaned up by t.TempDir()
   109→}

   162→// NewRepoEnv creates a TestEnv with an initialized git repo and Entire.
   163→// This is a convenience factory for tests that need a basic repo setup.
   164→func NewRepoEnv(t *testing.T, strategy string) *TestEnv {

   172→// NewRepoWithCommit creates a TestEnv with a git repo, Entire, and an initial commit.
   173→// The initial commit contains a README.md and .gitignore (excluding .entire/).
   174→func NewRepoWithCommit(t *testing.T, strategy string) *TestEnv {
   175→	t.Helper()
   176→	env := NewRepoEnv(t, strategy)
   177→	env.WriteFile(".gitignore", ".entire/\n")
   178→	env.WriteFile("README.md", "# Test Repository")
   179→	env.GitAdd(".gitignore")

   185→// NewFeatureBranchEnv creates a TestEnv ready for session testing.
   186→// It initializes the repo, creates an initial commit on main,
   187→// and checks out a feature branch. This is the most common setup
   188→// for session and rewind tests since Entire tracking skips main/master.
   189→func NewFeatureBranchEnv(t *testing.T, strategyName string) *TestEnv {
   190→	t.Helper()
   191→	env := NewRepoWithCommit(t, strategyName)
   192→	env.GitCheckoutNewBranch("feature/test-branch")
   193→	return env
   194→}

   196→// AllStrategies returns all strategy names for parameterized tests.
   197→func AllStrategies() []string {
   198→	return []string{
   199→		strategy.StrategyNameAutoCommit,
   200→		strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit,
   201→	}
   202→}
   203→
   204→// RunForAllStrategies runs a test function for each strategy in parallel.
   205→// This reduces boilerplate for tests that need to verify behavior across all strategies.
   206→// Each subtest gets its own TestEnv with a feature branch ready for testing.
   207→func RunForAllStrategies(t *testing.T, testFn func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string)) {
   208→	t.Helper()
   209→	for _, strat := range AllStrategies() {
   210→		strat := strat // capture for parallel
   211→		t.Run(strat, func(t *testing.T) {
   212→			t.Parallel()
   213→			env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, strat)
   214→			testFn(t, env, strat)
   215→		})
   216→	}
   217→}
   218→
   219→// RunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv runs a test function for each strategy in parallel,
   220→// using NewRepoWithCommit instead of NewFeatureBranchEnv. Use this for tests
   221→// that need to test behavior on the main branch.
   222→func RunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv(t *testing.T, testFn func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string)) {
   223→	t.Helper()
   224→	for _, strat := range AllStrategies() {
   225→		strat := strat // capture for parallel
   226→		t.Run(strat, func(t *testing.T) {
   227→			t.Parallel()
   228→			env := NewRepoWithCommit(t, strat)
   229→			testFn(t, env, strat)
   230→		})
   231→	}
   232→}
   233→
   234→// RunForAllStrategiesWithBasicEnv runs a test function for each strategy in parallel,
   235→// using NewRepoEnv (git repo + entire init, no commits). Use this for tests
   236→// that need to verify basic initialization behavior.
   237→func RunForAllStrategiesWithBasicEnv(t *testing.T, testFn func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string)) {
   238→	t.Helper()
   239→	for _, strat := range AllStrategies() {
   240→		strat := strat // capture for parallel
   241→		t.Run(strat, func(t *testing.T) {
   242→			t.Parallel()
   243→			env := NewRepoEnv(t, strat)
   244→			testFn(t, env, strat)
   245→		})
   246→	}
   247→}
   248→
   249→// RunForStrategiesSequential runs a test function for specific strategies sequentially.
   250→// Use this for tests that cannot be parallelized (e.g., tests using os.Chdir).
   251→// The strategies parameter allows testing a subset of strategies.
   252→func RunForStrategiesSequential(t *testing.T, strategies []string, testFn func(t *testing.T, strategyName string)) {
   253→	t.Helper()
   254→	for _, strat := range strategies {
   255→		t.Run(strat, func(t *testing.T) {
   256→			testFn(t, strat)
   257→		})
   258→	}
   259→}

   261→// InitRepo initializes a git repository in the test environment.
   262→func (env *TestEnv) InitRepo() {
   263→	env.T.Helper()
   264→
   265→	repo, err := git.PlainInit(env.RepoDir, false)
   266→	if err != nil {
   267→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to init git repo: %v", err)
   268→	}
   269→
   270→	// Configure git user for commits
   271→	cfg, err := repo.Config()
   272→	if err != nil {
   273→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get repo config: %v", err)
   274→	}
   275→	cfg.User.Name = "Test User"
   276→	cfg.User.Email = "test@example.com"
   277→
   278→	// Disable GPG signing for test commits (prevents failures if user has commit.gpgsign=true globally)
   279→	if cfg.Raw == nil {
   280→		cfg.Raw = config.New()
   281→	}
   282→	cfg.Raw.Section("commit").SetOption("gpgsign", "false")
   283→
   284→	if err := repo.SetConfig(cfg); err != nil {
   285→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to set repo config: %v", err)
   286→	}
   287→}

   313→
   314→// initEntireInternal is the common implementation for InitEntire variants.
   315→func (env *TestEnv) initEntireInternal(strategyName string, strategyOptions map[string]any) {
   316→	env.T.Helper()
   317→
   318→	// Create .entire directory structure
   319→	entireDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".entire")
   320→	if err := os.MkdirAll(entireDir, 0o755); err != nil {
   321→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to create .entire directory: %v", err)
   322→	}
   323→
   324→	// Create tmp directory
   325→	tmpDir := filepath.Join(entireDir, "tmp")
   326→	if err := os.MkdirAll(tmpDir, 0o755); err != nil {
   327→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to create .entire/tmp directory: %v", err)
   328→	}
   329→
   330→	// Write settings.json
   331→	// Note: The agent name is NOT stored in settings.json — the CLI determines
   332→	// the agent from installed hooks (detect presence) or checkpoint metadata.
   333→	// The settings parser uses DisallowUnknownFields(), so only recognized fields are allowed.
   334→	settings := map[string]any{
   335→		"strategy":  strategyName,
   336→		"local_dev": true, // Note: git-triggered hooks won't work (path is relative); tests call hooks via getTestBinary() instead
   337→	}
   338→	if strategyOptions != nil {
   339→		settings["strategy_options"] = strategyOptions
   340→	}
   341→	data, err := jsonutil.MarshalIndentWithNewline(settings, "", "  ")
   342→	if err != nil {
   343→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to marshal settings: %v", err)
   344→	}
   345→	settingsPath := filepath.Join(entireDir, paths.SettingsFileName)
   346→	if err := os.WriteFile(settingsPath, data, 0o644); err != nil {
   347→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write %s: %v", paths.SettingsFileName, err)
   348→	}
   349→}
   350→
   351→// WriteFile creates a file with the given content in the test repo.
   352→// It creates parent directories as needed.
   353→func (env *TestEnv) WriteFile(path, content string) {
   354→	env.T.Helper()
   355→
   356→	fullPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, path)
   357→
   358→	// Create parent directories
   359→	dir := filepath.Dir(fullPath)
   360→	if err := os.MkdirAll(dir, 0o755); err != nil {
   361→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to create directory %s: %v", dir, err)
   362→	}
   363→
   364→	if err := os.WriteFile(fullPath, []byte(content), 0o644); err != nil {
   365→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write file %s: %v", path, err)
   366→	}
   367→}

   401→// GitAdd stages files for commit.

   422→// GitCommit creates a commit with all staged files.

   478→// GitCommitWithCheckpointID creates a commit with Entire-Checkpoint trailer.
   479→// This simulates commits created by the auto-commit strategy.
   480→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithCheckpointID(message, checkpointID string) {
   481→	env.T.Helper()
   482→
   483→	// Format message with checkpoint trailer
   484→	fullMessage := message + "\n\nEntire-Checkpoint: " + checkpointID + "\n"
   485→
   486→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   487→	if err != nil {
   488→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   489→	}
   490→
   491→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   492→	if err != nil {
   493→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
   494→	}
   495→
   496→	_, err = worktree.Commit(fullMessage, &git.CommitOptions{
   497→		Author: &object.Signature{
   498→			Name:  "Test User",
   499→			Email: "test@example.com",
   500→			When:  time.Now(),
   501→		},
   502→	})
   503→	if err != nil {
   504→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
   505→	}
   506→}
   507→
   508→// GitCommitWithMultipleSessions creates a commit with multiple Entire-Session trailers.
   509→// This simulates merge commits that combine work from multiple sessions.
   510→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithMultipleSessions(message string, sessionIDs []string) {
   511→	env.T.Helper()
   512→
   513→	// Format message with multiple session trailers
   514→	fullMessage := message + "\n\n"
   515→	var fullMessageSb404 strings.Builder
   516→	for _, sessionID := range sessionIDs {
   517→		fullMessageSb404.WriteString("Entire-Session: " + sessionID + "\n")
   518→	}
   519→	fullMessage += fullMessageSb404.String()
   520→
   521→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   522→	if err != nil {
   523→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   524→	}
   525→
   526→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   527→	if err != nil {
   528→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
   529→	}
   530→
   531→	_, err = worktree.Commit(fullMessage, &git.CommitOptions{
   532→		Author: &object.Signature{
   533→			Name:  "Test User",
   534→			Email: "test@example.com",
   535→			When:  time.Now(),
   536→		},
   537→	})
   538→	if err != nil {
   539→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
   540→	}
   541→}

   585→// GetGitLog returns a list of commit hashes from HEAD.
   586→func (env *TestEnv) GetGitLog() []string {
   587→	env.T.Helper()
   588→
   589→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   590→	if err != nil {
   591→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   592→	}
   593→
   594→	head, err := repo.Head()
   595→	if err != nil {
   596→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
   597→	}
   598→
   599→	commitIter, err := repo.Log(&git.LogOptions{From: head.Hash()})
   600→	if err != nil {
   601→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get log: %v", err)
   602→	}
   603→
   604→	var commits []string
   605→	err = commitIter.ForEach(func(c *object.Commit) error {
   606→		commits = append(commits, c.Hash.String())
   607→		return nil
   608→	})
   609→	if err != nil {
   610→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to iterate commits: %v", err)
   611→	}
   612→
   613→	return commits
   614→}

   616→// GitCheckoutNewBranch creates and checks out a new branch.
   617→// Uses git CLI instead of go-git to work around go-git v5 bug where Checkout
   618→// deletes untracked files (see https://github.com/go-git/go-git/issues/970).
   619→func (env *TestEnv) GitCheckoutNewBranch(branchName string) {
   620→	env.T.Helper()
   621→
   622→	cmd := exec.Command("git", "checkout", "-b", branchName)
   623→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   624→	if output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   625→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to checkout new branch %s: %v\nOutput: %s", branchName, err, output)
   626→	}
   627→}

   629→// GetCurrentBranch returns the current branch name.
   630→func (env *TestEnv) GetCurrentBranch() string {
   631→	env.T.Helper()
   632→
   633→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   634→	if err != nil {
   635→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   636→	}
   637→
   638→	head, err := repo.Head()
   639→	if err != nil {
   640→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
   641→	}
   642→
   643→	if !head.Name().IsBranch() {
   644→		return "" // Detached HEAD
   645→	}
   646→
   647→	return head.Name().Short()
   648→}

   650→// RewindPoint mirrors strategy.RewindPoint for test assertions.
   651→type RewindPoint struct {
   652→	ID               string
   653→	Message          string
   654→	MetadataDir      string
   655→	Date             time.Time
   656→	IsTaskCheckpoint bool
   657→	ToolUseID        string
   658→	IsLogsOnly       bool
   659→	CondensationID   string
   660→}
   661→
   662→// GetRewindPoints returns available rewind points using the CLI.
   663→func (env *TestEnv) GetRewindPoints() []RewindPoint {
   664→	env.T.Helper()
   665→
   666→	// Run rewind --list using the shared binary
   667→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "rewind", "--list")
   668→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   669→	cmd.Env = env.cliEnv()
   670→
   671→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   672→	if err != nil {
   673→		env.T.Fatalf("rewind --list failed: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   674→	}
   675→
   676→	// Parse JSON output
   677→	var jsonPoints []struct {
   678→		ID               string `json:"id"`
   679→		Message          string `json:"message"`
   680→		MetadataDir      string `json:"metadata_dir"`
   681→		Date             string `json:"date"`
   682→		IsTaskCheckpoint bool   `json:"is_task_checkpoint"`
   683→		ToolUseID        string `json:"tool_use_id"`
   684→		IsLogsOnly       bool   `json:"is_logs_only"`
   685→		CondensationID   string `json:"condensation_id"`
   686→	}
   687→
   688→	if err := json.Unmarshal(output, &jsonPoints); err != nil {
   689→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to parse rewind points: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   690→	}
   691→
   692→	points := make([]RewindPoint, len(jsonPoints))
   693→	for i, jp := range jsonPoints {
   694→		date, _ := time.Parse(time.RFC3339, jp.Date)
   695→		points[i] = RewindPoint{
   696→			ID:               jp.ID,
   697→			Message:          jp.Message,
   698→			MetadataDir:      jp.MetadataDir,
   699→			Date:             date,
   700→			IsTaskCheckpoint: jp.IsTaskCheckpoint,
   701→			ToolUseID:        jp.ToolUseID,
   702→			IsLogsOnly:       jp.IsLogsOnly,
   703→			CondensationID:   jp.CondensationID,
   704→		}
   705→	}
   706→
   707→	return points
   708→}
   709→
   710→// Rewind performs a rewind to the specified commit ID using the CLI.
   711→func (env *TestEnv) Rewind(commitID string) error {

   716→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   717→	cmd.Env = env.cliEnv()
   718→
   719→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   720→	if err != nil {
   721→		return errors.New("rewind failed: " + string(output))

   723→
   724→	env.T.Logf("Rewind output: %s", output)
   725→	return nil
   726→}

   766→// BranchExists checks if a branch exists in the repository.
   767→func (env *TestEnv) BranchExists(branchName string) bool {
   768→	env.T.Helper()
   769→
   770→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   771→	if err != nil {
   772→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   773→	}
   774→
   775→	refs, err := repo.References()
   776→	if err != nil {
   777→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get references: %v", err)
   778→	}
   779→
   780→	found := false
   781→	_ = refs.ForEach(func(ref *plumbing.Reference) error {
   782→		if ref.Name().Short() == branchName {
   783→			found = true
   784→		}
   785→		return nil
   786→	})
   787→
   788→	return found
   789→}

   808→
   809→// FileExistsInBranch checks if a file exists in a specific branch's tree.
   810→func (env *TestEnv) FileExistsInBranch(branchName, filePath string) bool {
   811→	env.T.Helper()
   812→
   813→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   814→	if err != nil {
   815→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   816→	}
   817→
   818→	// Get the branch reference
   819→	ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchName), true)
   820→	if err != nil {
   821→		// Try as a remote-style ref
   822→		ref, err = repo.Reference(plumbing.ReferenceName("refs/heads/"+branchName), true)
   823→		if err != nil {
   824→			return false
   825→		}
   826→	}
   827→
   828→	// Get the commit
   829→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   830→	if err != nil {
   831→		return false
   832→	}
   833→
   834→	// Get the tree
   835→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   836→	if err != nil {
   837→		return false
   838→	}
   839→
   840→	// Check if file exists
   841→	_, err = tree.File(filePath)
   842→	return err == nil
   843→}
   844→
   845→// ReadFileFromBranch reads a file's content from a specific branch's tree.
   846→// Returns the content and true if found, empty string and false if not found.
   847→func (env *TestEnv) ReadFileFromBranch(branchName, filePath string) (string, bool) {
   848→	env.T.Helper()
   849→
   850→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   851→	if err != nil {
   852→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   853→	}
   854→
   855→	// Get the branch reference
   856→	ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchName), true)
   857→	if err != nil {
   858→		// Try as a remote-style ref
   859→		ref, err = repo.Reference(plumbing.ReferenceName("refs/heads/"+branchName), true)
   860→		if err != nil {
   861→			return "", false
   862→		}
   863→	}
   864→
   865→	// Get the commit
   866→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   867→	if err != nil {
   868→		return "", false
   869→	}
   870→
   871→	// Get the tree
   872→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   873→	if err != nil {
   874→		return "", false
   875→	}
   876→
   877→	// Get the file
   878→	file, err := tree.File(filePath)
   879→	if err != nil {
   880→		return "", false
   881→	}
   882→
   883→	// Get the content
   884→	content, err := file.Contents()
   885→	if err != nil {
   886→		return "", false
   887→	}
   888→
   889→	return content, true
   890→}

   892→// GetLatestCommitMessageOnBranch returns the commit message of the latest commit on the given branch.

   915→
   916→// GitCommitWithShadowHooks stages and commits files, simulating the prepare-commit-msg
   917→// and post-commit hooks as a human (with TTY). This is the default for tests.
   918→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithShadowHooks(message string, files ...string) {
   919→	env.T.Helper()
   920→	env.gitCommitWithShadowHooks(message, true, files...)
   921→}
   922→
   923→// GitCommitWithShadowHooksAsAgent is like GitCommitWithShadowHooks but simulates
   924→// an agent commit (no TTY). This triggers the fast path in PrepareCommitMsg that
   925→// skips content detection and interactive prompts for ACTIVE sessions.
   926→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithShadowHooksAsAgent(message string, files ...string) {
   927→	env.T.Helper()
   928→	env.gitCommitWithShadowHooks(message, false, files...)
   929→}
   930→
   931→// gitCommitWithShadowHooks is the shared implementation for committing with shadow hooks.
   932→// When simulateTTY is true, sets ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 to simulate a human at the terminal.
   933→// When false, filters it out to simulate an agent subprocess (no controlling terminal).
   934→func (env *TestEnv) gitCommitWithShadowHooks(message string, simulateTTY bool, files ...string) {
   935→	env.T.Helper()
   936→
   937→	// Stage files using go-git
   938→	for _, file := range files {
   939→		env.GitAdd(file)
   940→	}
   941→
   942→	// Create a temp file for the commit message (prepare-commit-msg hook modifies this)
   943→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
   944→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
   945→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
   946→	}
   947→
   948→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook using the shared binary.
   949→	// Pass source="message" to match real `git commit -m` behavior.
   950→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile, "message")
   951→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   952→	if simulateTTY {
   953→		// Simulate human at terminal: ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 makes hasTTY() return true
   954→		// and askConfirmTTY() return defaultYes without reading from /dev/tty.
   955→		prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1")
   956→	} else {
   957→		// Simulate agent: ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=0 makes hasTTY() return false,
   958→		// triggering the fast path that adds trailers for ACTIVE sessions.
   959→		prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=0")
   960→	}
   961→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   962→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg output: %s", output)
   963→		// Don't fail - hook may silently succeed
   964→	}
   965→
   966→	// Read the modified message
   967→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
   968→	if err != nil {
   969→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
   970→	}
   971→
   972→	// Create the commit using go-git with the modified message
   973→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   974→	if err != nil {
   975→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   976→	}
   977→
   978→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   979→	if err != nil {
   980→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
   981→	}
   982→
   983→	_, err = worktree.Commit(string(modifiedMsg), &git.CommitOptions{
   984→		Author: &object.Signature{
   985→			Name:  "Test User",
   986→			Email: "test@example.com",
   987→			When:  time.Now(),
   988→		},
   989→	})
   990→	if err != nil {
   991→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
   992→	}
   993→
   994→	// Run post-commit hook using the shared binary
   995→	// This triggers condensation if the commit has an Entire-Checkpoint trailer
   996→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
   997→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   998→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   999→		env.T.Logf("post-commit output: %s", output)
  1000→		// Don't fail - hook may silently succeed
  1001→	}
  1002→}
  1003→
  1004→// GitCommitAmendWithShadowHooks amends the last commit with shadow hooks.
  1005→// This simulates `git commit --amend` with the prepare-commit-msg and post-commit hooks.
  1006→// The prepare-commit-msg hook is called with "commit" source to indicate an amend.
  1007→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitAmendWithShadowHooks(message string, files ...string) {
  1008→	env.T.Helper()
  1009→
  1010→	// Stage any additional files
  1011→	for _, file := range files {
  1012→		env.GitAdd(file)
  1013→	}
  1014→
  1015→	// Write commit message to temp file
  1016→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
  1017→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
  1018→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
  1019→	}
  1020→
  1021→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook with "commit" source (indicates amend).
  1022→	// Set ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 to simulate human (amend is always a human operation).
  1023→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile, "commit")
  1024→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1025→	prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1")
  1026→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1027→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg (amend) output: %s", output)
  1028→	}
  1029→
  1030→	// Read the modified message
  1031→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
  1032→	if err != nil {
  1033→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
  1034→	}
  1035→
  1036→	// Amend the commit using go-git
  1037→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1038→	if err != nil {
  1039→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1040→	}
  1041→
  1042→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
  1043→	if err != nil {
  1044→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
  1045→	}
  1046→
  1047→	_, err = worktree.Commit(string(modifiedMsg), &git.CommitOptions{
  1048→		Author: &object.Signature{
  1049→			Name:  "Test User",
  1050→			Email: "test@example.com",
  1051→			When:  time.Now(),
  1052→		},
  1053→		Amend: true,
  1054→	})
  1055→	if err != nil {
  1056→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to amend commit: %v", err)
  1057→	}
  1058→
  1059→	// Run post-commit hook
  1060→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
  1061→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1062→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1063→		env.T.Logf("post-commit (amend) output: %s", output)
  1064→	}
  1065→}
  1066→
  1067→// GitCommitWithTrailerRemoved stages and commits files, simulating what happens when
  1068→// a user removes the Entire-Checkpoint trailer during commit message editing.
  1069→// This tests the opt-out behavior where removing the trailer skips condensation.
  1070→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithTrailerRemoved(message string, files ...string) {
  1071→	env.T.Helper()
  1072→
  1073→	// Stage files using go-git
  1074→	for _, file := range files {
  1075→		env.GitAdd(file)
  1076→	}
  1077→
  1078→	// Create a temp file for the commit message (prepare-commit-msg hook modifies this)
  1079→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
  1080→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
  1081→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
  1082→	}
  1083→
  1084→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook using the shared binary.
  1085→	// Set ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 to simulate human (this tests the editor flow where
  1086→	// the user removes the trailer before committing).
  1087→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile)
  1088→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1089→	prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1")
  1090→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1091→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg output: %s", output)
  1092→	}
  1093→
  1094→	// Read the modified message (with trailer added by hook)
  1095→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
  1096→	if err != nil {
  1097→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
  1098→	}
  1099→
  1100→	// REMOVE the Entire-Checkpoint trailer (simulating user editing the message)
  1101→	lines := strings.Split(string(modifiedMsg), "\n")
  1102→	var cleanedLines []string
  1103→	for _, line := range lines {
  1104→		// Skip the trailer and the comments about it
  1105→		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:") {
  1106→			continue
  1107→		}
  1108→		if strings.Contains(line, "Remove the Entire-Checkpoint trailer") {
  1109→			continue
  1110→		}
  1111→		if strings.Contains(line, "trailer will be added to your next commit") {
  1112→			continue
  1113→		}
  1114→		cleanedLines = append(cleanedLines, line)
  1115→	}
  1116→	cleanedMsg := strings.TrimRight(strings.Join(cleanedLines, "\n"), "\n") + "\n"
  1117→
  1118→	// Create the commit using go-git with the cleaned message (no trailer)
  1119→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1120→	if err != nil {
  1121→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1122→	}
  1123→
  1124→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
  1125→	if err != nil {
  1126→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
  1127→	}
  1128→
  1129→	_, err = worktree.Commit(cleanedMsg, &git.CommitOptions{
  1130→		Author: &object.Signature{
  1131→			Name:  "Test User",
  1132→			Email: "test@example.com",
  1133→			When:  time.Now(),
  1134→		},
  1135→	})
  1136→	if err != nil {
  1137→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
  1138→	}
  1139→
  1140→	// Run post-commit hook - since trailer was removed, no condensation should happen
  1141→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
  1142→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1143→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1144→		env.T.Logf("post-commit output: %s", output)
  1145→	}
  1146→}
  1147→
  1148→// GitRm stages file deletions using git rm.
  1149→func (env *TestEnv) GitRm(paths ...string) {
  1150→	env.T.Helper()
  1151→
  1152→	args := append([]string{"rm", "--"}, paths...)
  1153→	cmd := exec.Command("git", args...)

  1155→	if output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1156→		env.T.Fatalf("git rm failed: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)

  1160→// GitCommitStagedWithShadowHooks commits whatever is already staged (without adding files first),
  1161→// running the prepare-commit-msg and post-commit hooks like a real workflow.
  1162→// Use this after GitRm or when files are already staged.
  1163→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitStagedWithShadowHooks(message string) {

  1167→
  1168→// gitCommitStagedWithShadowHooks is the shared implementation for committing staged changes with hooks.
  1169→func (env *TestEnv) gitCommitStagedWithShadowHooks(message string, simulateTTY bool) {
  1170→	env.T.Helper()
  1171→
  1172→	// Create a temp file for the commit message (prepare-commit-msg hook modifies this)
  1173→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
  1174→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
  1175→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
  1176→	}
  1177→
  1178→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook using the shared binary.
  1179→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile, "message")
  1180→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1181→	if simulateTTY {
  1182→		prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1")
  1183→	} else {
  1184→		prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=0")
  1185→	}
  1186→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1187→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg output: %s", output)
  1188→	}
  1189→
  1190→	// Read the modified message
  1191→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
  1192→	if err != nil {
  1193→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
  1194→	}
  1195→
  1196→	// Create the commit using go-git with the modified message
  1197→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1198→	if err != nil {
  1199→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1200→	}
  1201→
  1202→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
  1203→	if err != nil {
  1204→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
  1205→	}
  1206→
  1207→	_, err = worktree.Commit(string(modifiedMsg), &git.CommitOptions{
  1208→		Author: &object.Signature{
  1209→			Name:  "Test User",
  1210→			Email: "test@example.com",
  1211→			When:  time.Now(),
  1212→		},
  1213→	})
  1214→	if err != nil {
  1215→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
  1216→	}
  1217→
  1218→	// Run post-commit hook
  1219→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
  1220→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1221→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1222→		env.T.Logf("post-commit output: %s", output)
  1223→	}
  1224→}

  1226→// ListBranchesWithPrefix returns all branches that start with the given prefix.
  1227→func (env *TestEnv) ListBranchesWithPrefix(prefix string) []string {
  1228→	env.T.Helper()
  1229→
  1230→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1231→	if err != nil {
  1232→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1233→	}
  1234→
  1235→	refs, err := repo.References()
  1236→	if err != nil {
  1237→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get references: %v", err)
  1238→	}
  1239→
  1240→	var branches []string
  1241→	_ = refs.ForEach(func(ref *plumbing.Reference) error {
  1242→		name := ref.Name().Short()
  1243→		if len(name) >= len(prefix) && name[:len(prefix)] == prefix {
  1244→			branches = append(branches, name)
  1245→		}
  1246→		return nil
  1247→	})
  1248→
  1249→	return branches
  1250→}
  1251→
  1252→// GetLatestCheckpointID returns the most recent checkpoint ID from the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch.
  1253→// This is used by tests that previously extracted the checkpoint ID from commit message trailers.
  1254→// Now that active branch commits are clean (no trailers), we get the ID from the sessions branch.
  1255→// Fatals if the checkpoint ID cannot be found, with detailed context about what was found.
  1256→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCheckpointID() string {
  1257→	env.T.Helper()
  1258→
  1259→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1260→	if err != nil {
  1261→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1262→	}
  1263→
  1264→	// Get the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch
  1265→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName)
  1266→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
  1267→	if err != nil {
  1268→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get %s branch: %v", paths.MetadataBranchName, err)
  1269→	}
  1270→
  1271→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
  1272→	if err != nil {
  1273→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get commit: %v", err)
  1274→	}
  1275→
  1276→	// Extract checkpoint ID from commit message
  1277→	// Format: "Checkpoint: <12-hex-char-id>\n\nSession: ...\nStrategy: ..."
  1278→	for _, line := range strings.Split(commit.Message, "\n") {
  1279→		line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
  1280→		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Checkpoint: ") {
  1281→			return strings.TrimPrefix(line, "Checkpoint: ")
  1282→		}
  1283→	}
  1284→
  1285→	env.T.Fatalf("could not find checkpoint ID in %s branch commit message:\n%s",
  1286→		paths.MetadataBranchName, commit.Message)
  1287→	return ""
  1288→}
  1289→
  1290→// TryGetLatestCheckpointID returns the most recent checkpoint ID from the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch.
  1291→// Returns empty string if the branch doesn't exist or has no checkpoint commits yet.
  1292→// Use this when you need to check if a checkpoint exists without failing the test.
  1293→func (env *TestEnv) TryGetLatestCheckpointID() string {
  1294→	env.T.Helper()
  1295→
  1296→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1297→	if err != nil {
  1298→		return ""
  1299→	}
  1300→
  1301→	// Get the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch
  1302→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName)
  1303→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
  1304→	if err != nil {
  1305→		return ""
  1306→	}
  1307→
  1308→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
  1309→	if err != nil {
  1310→		return ""
  1311→	}
  1312→
  1313→	// Extract checkpoint ID from commit message
  1314→	// Format: "Checkpoint: <12-hex-char-id>\n\nSession: ...\nStrategy: ..."
  1315→	for _, line := range strings.Split(commit.Message, "\n") {
  1316→		line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
  1317→		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Checkpoint: ") {
  1318→			return strings.TrimPrefix(line, "Checkpoint: ")
  1319→		}
  1320→	}
  1321→
  1322→	return ""
  1323→}
  1324→
  1325→// GetLatestCondensationID is an alias for GetLatestCheckpointID for backwards compatibility.
  1326→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCondensationID() string {
  1327→	return env.GetLatestCheckpointID()
  1328→}
  1329→
  1330→// GetCheckpointIDFromCommitMessage extracts the Entire-Checkpoint trailer from a commit message.
  1331→// Returns empty string if no trailer found.
  1332→func (env *TestEnv) GetCheckpointIDFromCommitMessage(commitSHA string) string {
  1333→	env.T.Helper()
  1334→
  1335→	msg := env.GetCommitMessage(commitSHA)
  1336→	cpID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(msg)
  1337→	if !found {
  1338→		return ""
  1339→	}
  1340→	return cpID.String()
  1341→}
  1342→
  1343→// GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory walks backwards from HEAD on the active branch
  1344→// and returns the checkpoint ID from the first commit that has an Entire-Checkpoint trailer.
  1345→// This verifies that condensation actually happened (commit has trailer) without relying
  1346→// on timestamp-based matching.
  1347→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory() string {
  1348→	env.T.Helper()
  1349→
  1350→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1351→	if err != nil {
  1352→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1353→	}
  1354→
  1355→	head, err := repo.Head()
  1356→	if err != nil {
  1357→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
  1358→	}
  1359→
  1360→	commitIter, err := repo.Log(&git.LogOptions{From: head.Hash()})
  1361→	if err != nil {
  1362→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to iterate commits: %v", err)
  1363→	}
  1364→
  1365→	var checkpointID string
  1366→	//nolint:errcheck // ForEach callback handles errors
  1367→	commitIter.ForEach(func(c *object.Commit) error {
  1368→		if cpID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(c.Message); found {
  1369→			checkpointID = cpID.String()
  1370→			return errors.New("stop iteration") // Found it, stop
  1371→		}
  1372→		return nil
  1373→	})
  1374→
  1375→	if checkpointID == "" {
  1376→		env.T.Fatalf("no commit with Entire-Checkpoint trailer found in history")
  1377→	}
  1378→
  1379→	return checkpointID
  1380→}
  1381→
  1382→// ShardedCheckpointPath returns the sharded path for a checkpoint ID.
  1383→// Format: <id[:2]>/<id[2:]>
  1384→// Delegates to id.CheckpointID.Path() for consistency.
  1385→func ShardedCheckpointPath(checkpointID string) string {
  1386→	return id.CheckpointID(checkpointID).Path()
  1387→}
  1388→
  1389→// SessionFilePath returns the path to a session file within a checkpoint.
  1390→// Session files are stored in numbered subdirectories using 0-based indexing (e.g., 0/full.jsonl).
  1391→// This function constructs the path for the first (default) session.
  1392→func SessionFilePath(checkpointID string, fileName string) string {
  1393→	return id.CheckpointID(checkpointID).Path() + "/0/" + fileName
  1394→}
  1395→
  1396→// CheckpointSummaryPath returns the path to the root metadata.json (CheckpointSummary) for a checkpoint.
  1397→func CheckpointSummaryPath(checkpointID string) string {
  1398→	return id.CheckpointID(checkpointID).Path() + "/" + paths.MetadataFileName
  1399→}
  1400→
  1401→// SessionMetadataPath returns the path to the session-level metadata.json for a checkpoint.
  1402→func SessionMetadataPath(checkpointID string) string {
  1403→	return SessionFilePath(checkpointID, paths.MetadataFileName)
  1404→}
  1405→
  1406→// CheckpointValidation contains expected values for checkpoint validation.
  1407→type CheckpointValidation struct {
  1408→	// CheckpointID is the expected checkpoint ID
  1409→	CheckpointID string
  1410→
  1411→	// SessionID is the expected session ID
  1412→	SessionID string
  1413→
  1414→	// Strategy is the expected strategy name
  1415→	Strategy string
  1416→
  1417→	// FilesTouched are the expected files in files_touched
  1418→	FilesTouched []string
  1419→
  1420→	// ExpectedPrompts are strings that should appear in prompt.txt
  1421→	ExpectedPrompts []string
  1422→
  1423→	// ExpectedTranscriptContent are strings that should appear in full.jsonl
  1424→	ExpectedTranscriptContent []string
  1425→
  1426→	// CheckpointsCount is the expected checkpoint count (0 means don't validate)
  1427→	CheckpointsCount int
  1428→}
  1429→
  1430→// ValidateCheckpoint performs comprehensive validation of a checkpoint on the metadata branch.
  1431→// It validates:
  1432→// - Root metadata.json (CheckpointSummary) structure and expected fields
  1433→// - Session metadata.json (CommittedMetadata) structure and expected fields
  1434→// - Transcript file (full.jsonl) is valid JSONL and contains expected content
  1435→// - Content hash file (content_hash.txt) matches SHA256 of transcript
  1436→// - Prompt file (prompt.txt) contains expected prompts
  1437→func (env *TestEnv) ValidateCheckpoint(v CheckpointValidation) {
  1438→	env.T.Helper()
  1439→
  1440→	// Validate root metadata.json (CheckpointSummary)
  1441→	env.validateCheckpointSummary(v)
  1442→
  1443→	// Validate session metadata.json (CommittedMetadata)
  1444→	env.validateSessionMetadata(v)
  1445→
  1446→	// Validate transcript is valid JSONL
  1447→	env.validateTranscriptJSONL(v.CheckpointID, v.ExpectedTranscriptContent)
  1448→
  1449→	// Validate content hash matches transcript
  1450→	env.validateContentHash(v.CheckpointID)
  1451→
  1452→	// Validate prompt.txt contains expected prompts
  1453→	if len(v.ExpectedPrompts) > 0 {
  1454→		env.validatePromptContent(v.CheckpointID, v.ExpectedPrompts)
  1455→	}
  1456→}
  1457→
  1458→// validateCheckpointSummary validates the root metadata.json (CheckpointSummary).
  1459→func (env *TestEnv) validateCheckpointSummary(v CheckpointValidation) {
  1460→	env.T.Helper()
  1461→
  1462→	summaryPath := CheckpointSummaryPath(v.CheckpointID)
  1463→	content, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(paths.MetadataBranchName, summaryPath)
  1464→	if !found {
  1465→		env.T.Fatalf("CheckpointSummary not found at %s", summaryPath)
  1466→	}
  1467→
  1468→	var summary checkpoint.CheckpointSummary
  1469→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(content), &summary); err != nil {
  1470→		env.T.Fatalf("Failed to parse CheckpointSummary: %v\nContent: %s", err, content)
  1471→	}
  1472→
  1473→	// Validate checkpoint_id
  1474→	if summary.CheckpointID.String() != v.CheckpointID {
  1475→		env.T.Errorf("CheckpointSummary.CheckpointID = %q, want %q", summary.CheckpointID, v.CheckpointID)
  1476→	}
  1477→
  1478→	// Validate strategy
  1479→	if v.Strategy != "" && summary.Strategy != v.Strategy {
  1480→		env.T.Errorf("CheckpointSummary.Strategy = %q, want %q", summary.Strategy, v.Strategy)
  1481→	}
  1482→
  1483→	// Validate sessions array is populated
  1484→	if len(summary.Sessions) == 0 {
  1485→		env.T.Error("CheckpointSummary.Sessions should have at least one entry")
  1486→	}
  1487→
  1488→	// Validate files_touched
  1489→	if len(v.FilesTouched) > 0 {
  1490→		touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
  1491→		for _, f := range summary.FilesTouched {
  1492→			touchedSet[f] = true
  1493→		}
  1494→		for _, expected := range v.FilesTouched {
  1495→			if !touchedSet[expected] {
  1496→				env.T.Errorf("CheckpointSummary.FilesTouched missing %q, got %v", expected, summary.FilesTouched)
  1497→			}
  1498→		}
  1499→	}
  1500→
  1501→	// Validate checkpoints_count
  1502→	if v.CheckpointsCount > 0 && summary.CheckpointsCount != v.CheckpointsCount {
  1503→		env.T.Errorf("CheckpointSummary.CheckpointsCount = %d, want %d", summary.CheckpointsCount, v.CheckpointsCount)
  1504→	}
  1505→}
  1506→
  1507→// validateSessionMetadata validates the session-level metadata.json (CommittedMetadata).
  1508→func (env *TestEnv) validateSessionMetadata(v CheckpointValidation) {
  1509→	env.T.Helper()
  1510→
  1511→	metadataPath := SessionMetadataPath(v.CheckpointID)
  1512→	content, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(paths.MetadataBranchName, metadataPath)
  1513→	if !found {
  1514→		env.T.Fatalf("Session metadata not found at %s", metadataPath)
  1515→	}
  1516→
  1517→	var metadata checkpoint.CommittedMetadata
  1518→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(content), &metadata); err != nil {
  1519→		env.T.Fatalf("Failed to parse CommittedMetadata: %v\nContent: %s", err, content)
  1520→	}
  1521→
  1522→	// Validate checkpoint_id
  1523→	if metadata.CheckpointID.String() != v.CheckpointID {
  1524→		env.T.Errorf("CommittedMetadata.CheckpointID = %q, want %q", metadata.CheckpointID, v.CheckpointID)
  1525→	}
  1526→
  1527→	// Validate session_id
  1528→	if v.SessionID != "" && metadata.SessionID != v.SessionID {
  1529→		env.T.Errorf("CommittedMetadata.SessionID = %q, want %q", metadata.SessionID, v.SessionID)
  1530→	}
  1531→
  1532→	// Validate strategy
  1533→	if v.Strategy != "" && metadata.Strategy != v.Strategy {
  1534→		env.T.Errorf("CommittedMetadata.Strategy = %q, want %q", metadata.Strategy, v.Strategy)
  1535→	}
  1536→
  1537→	// Validate created_at is not zero
  1538→	if metadata.CreatedAt.IsZero() {
  1539→		env.T.Error("CommittedMetadata.CreatedAt should not be zero")
  1540→	}
  1541→
  1542→	// Validate files_touched
  1543→	if len(v.FilesTouched) > 0 {
  1544→		touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
  1545→		for _, f := range metadata.FilesTouched {
  1546→			touchedSet[f] = true
  1547→		}
  1548→		for _, expected := range v.FilesTouched {
  1549→			if !touchedSet[expected] {
  1550→				env.T.Errorf("CommittedMetadata.FilesTouched missing %q, got %v", expected, metadata.FilesTouched)
  1551→			}
  1552→		}
  1553→	}
  1554→
  1555→	// Validate checkpoints_count
  1556→	if v.CheckpointsCount > 0 && metadata.CheckpointsCount != v.CheckpointsCount {
  1557→		env.T.Errorf("CommittedMetadata.CheckpointsCount = %d, want %d", metadata.CheckpointsCount, v.CheckpointsCount)
  1558→	}
  1559→}
  1560→
  1561→// validateTranscriptJSONL validates that full.jsonl exists and is valid JSONL.
  1562→func (env *TestEnv) validateTranscriptJSONL(checkpointID string, expectedContent []string) {
  1563→	env.T.Helper()
  1564→
  1565→	transcriptPath := SessionFilePath(checkpointID, paths.TranscriptFileName)
  1566→	content, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(paths.MetadataBranchName, transcriptPath)
  1567→	if !found {
  1568→		env.T.Fatalf("Transcript not found at %s", transcriptPath)
  1569→	}
  1570→
  1571→	// Validate it's valid JSONL (each non-empty line should be valid JSON)
  1572→	lines := strings.Split(content, "\n")
  1573→	validLines := 0
  1574→	for i, line := range lines {
  1575→		line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
  1576→		if line == "" {
  1577→			continue
  1578→		}
  1579→		validLines++
  1580→		var obj map[string]any
  1581→		if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(line), &obj); err != nil {
  1582→			env.T.Errorf("Transcript line %d is not valid JSON: %v\nLine: %s", i+1, err, line)
  1583→		}
  1584→	}
  1585→
  1586→	if validLines == 0 {
  1587→		env.T.Error("Transcript is empty (no valid JSONL lines)")
  1588→	}
  1589→
  1590→	// Validate expected content appears in transcript
  1591→	for _, expected := range expectedContent {
  1592→		if !strings.Contains(content, expected) {
  1593→			env.T.Errorf("Transcript should contain %q", expected)
  1594→		}
  1595→	}
  1596→}
  1597→
  1598→// validateContentHash validates that content_hash.txt matches the SHA256 of the transcript.
  1599→func (env *TestEnv) validateContentHash(checkpointID string) {
  1600→	env.T.Helper()
  1601→
  1602→	// Read transcript
  1603→	transcriptPath := SessionFilePath(checkpointID, paths.TranscriptFileName)
  1604→	transcript, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(paths.MetadataBranchName, transcriptPath)
  1605→	if !found {
  1606→		env.T.Fatalf("Transcript not found at %s", transcriptPath)
  1607→	}
  1608→
  1609→	// Read content hash
  1610→	hashPath := SessionFilePath(checkpointID, "content_hash.txt")
  1611→	storedHash, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(paths.MetadataBranchName, hashPath)
  1612→	if !found {
  1613→		env.T.Fatalf("Content hash not found at %s", hashPath)
  1614→	}
  1615→	storedHash = strings.TrimSpace(storedHash)
  1616→
  1617→	// Calculate expected hash with sha256: prefix (matches format in committed.go)
  1618→	hash := sha256.Sum256([]byte(transcript))
  1619→	expectedHash := "sha256:" + hex.EncodeToString(hash[:])
  1620→
  1621→	if storedHash != expectedHash {
  1622→		env.T.Errorf("Content hash mismatch:\n  stored:   %s\n  expected: %s", storedHash, expectedHash)
  1623→	}
  1624→}
  1625→
  1626→// validatePromptContent validates that prompt.txt contains the expected prompts.
  1627→func (env *TestEnv) validatePromptContent(checkpointID string, expectedPrompts []string) {
  1628→	env.T.Helper()
  1629→
  1630→	promptPath := SessionFilePath(checkpointID, paths.PromptFileName)
  1631→	content, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(paths.MetadataBranchName, promptPath)
  1632→	if !found {
  1633→		env.T.Fatalf("Prompt file not found at %s", promptPath)
  1634→	}
  1635→
  1636→	for _, expected := range expectedPrompts {
  1637→		if !strings.Contains(content, expected) {
  1638→			env.T.Errorf("Prompt file should contain %q\nContent: %s", expected, content)
  1639→		}
  1640→	}
  1641→}
  1642→
  1643→func findModuleRoot() string {
  1644→	// Start from this source file's location and walk up to find go.mod
  1645→	_, thisFile, _, ok := runtime.Caller(0)
  1646→	if !ok {
  1647→		panic("failed to get current file path via runtime.Caller")
  1648→	}
  1649→	dir := filepath.Dir(thisFile)
  1650→
  1651→	for {
  1652→		if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(dir, "go.mod")); err == nil {
  1653→			return dir
  1654→		}
  1655→		parent := filepath.Dir(dir)
  1656→		if parent == dir {
  1657→			panic("could not find go.mod starting from " + thisFile)
  1658→		}
  1659→		dir = parent
  1660→	}
  1661→}
  1662→

   196→func (r *HookRunner) runHookWithInput(flag string, input interface{}) error {

   200→	if err != nil {

   206→
   207→func (r *HookRunner) runHookInRepoDir(hookName string, inputJSON []byte) error {
   208→	// Run using the shared test binary
   209→	// Command structure: entire hooks claude-code <hook-name>
   210→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "claude-code", hookName)
   211→	cmd.Dir = r.RepoDir
   212→	cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(inputJSON)
   213→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   214→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+r.ClaudeProjectDir,
   215→	)
   216→
   217→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   218→	if err != nil {
   219→		return fmt.Errorf("hook %s failed: %w\nInput: %s\nOutput: %s",
   220→			hookName, err, inputJSON, output)
   221→	}
   222→
   223→	r.T.Logf("Hook %s output: %s", hookName, output)
   224→	return nil
   225→}

   227→// Session represents a simulated Claude Code session.
   228→type Session struct {
   229→	ID                string // Raw model session ID (e.g., "test-session-1")
   230→	TranscriptPath    string
   231→	TranscriptBuilder *TranscriptBuilder
   232→	env               *TestEnv

   235→// FileChange represents a file modification in a session.
   236→type FileChange struct {

   238→	Content string
   239→}
   240→
   241→// NewSession creates a new simulated session.
   242→func (env *TestEnv) NewSession() *Session {
   243→	env.T.Helper()
   244→
   245→	env.SessionCounter++
   246→	sessionID := fmt.Sprintf("test-session-%d", env.SessionCounter)
   247→
   248→	transcriptPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".entire", "tmp", sessionID+".jsonl")
   249→
   250→	return &Session{
   251→		ID:                sessionID,
   252→		TranscriptPath:    transcriptPath,
   253→		TranscriptBuilder: NewTranscriptBuilder(),
   254→		env:               env,
   255→	}
   256→}
   257→
   258→// CreateTranscript builds and writes a transcript for the session.
   259→func (s *Session) CreateTranscript(prompt string, changes []FileChange) string {
   260→	s.TranscriptBuilder.AddUserMessage(prompt)
   261→	s.TranscriptBuilder.AddAssistantMessage("I'll help you with that.")
   262→
   263→	for _, change := range changes {
   264→		toolID := s.TranscriptBuilder.AddToolUse("mcp__acp__Write", change.Path, change.Content)
   265→		s.TranscriptBuilder.AddToolResult(toolID)
   266→	}
   267→
   268→	s.TranscriptBuilder.AddAssistantMessage("Done!")
   269→
   270→	if err := s.TranscriptBuilder.WriteToFile(s.TranscriptPath); err != nil {
   271→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to write transcript: %v", err)
   272→	}
   273→
   274→	return s.TranscriptPath
   275→}

   367→// ClearSessionState removes the session state file for the given session ID.
   368→// This simulates what happens when a user commits their changes (session is "completed").
   369→// Used in tests to allow sequential sessions to run without triggering concurrent session warnings.
   370→func (env *TestEnv) ClearSessionState(sessionID string) error {
   371→	env.T.Helper()
   372→
   373→	// Session state is stored in .git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json
   374→	stateFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "entire-sessions", sessionID+".json")
   375→

   382→// HookOutput contains the result of running a hook.

   389→// runHookWithOutput runs a hook and returns both stdout and stderr separately.
   390→func (r *HookRunner) runHookWithOutput(hookName string, inputJSON []byte) HookOutput {
   391→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "claude-code", hookName)
   392→	cmd.Dir = r.RepoDir
   393→	cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(inputJSON)
   394→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   395→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+r.ClaudeProjectDir,
   396→	)
   397→
   398→	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
   399→	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
   400→	cmd.Stderr = &stderr
   401→
   402→	err := cmd.Run()
   403→	return HookOutput{
   404→		Stdout: stdout.Bytes(),
   405→		Stderr: stderr.Bytes(),
   406→		Err:    err,
   407→	}
   408→}

   458→// GetSessionState reads and returns the session state for the given session ID.
   459→func (env *TestEnv) GetSessionState(sessionID string) (*strategy.SessionState, error) {
   460→	env.T.Helper()
   461→
   462→	stateFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "entire-sessions", sessionID+".json")
   463→
   464→	data, err := os.ReadFile(stateFile)
   465→	if os.IsNotExist(err) {
   466→		return nil, nil
   467→	}
   468→	if err != nil {
   469→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read session state: %w", err)
   470→	}
   471→
   472→	var state strategy.SessionState
   473→	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &state); err != nil {
   474→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse session state: %w", err)
   475→	}
   476→	return &state, nil
   477→}
   478→
   479→// WriteSessionState writes the session state for the given session ID.
   480→// This is useful for tests that need to manipulate session state directly.
   481→func (env *TestEnv) WriteSessionState(sessionID string, state *strategy.SessionState) error {
   482→	env.T.Helper()
   483→
   484→	stateDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "entire-sessions")
   485→	if err := os.MkdirAll(stateDir, 0o755); err != nil {
   486→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create session state dir: %w", err)
   487→	}
   488→
   489→	stateFile := filepath.Join(stateDir, sessionID+".json")
   490→
   491→	data, err := json.MarshalIndent(state, "", "  ")
   492→	if err != nil {
   493→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal session state: %w", err)
   494→	}
   495→
   496→	if err := os.WriteFile(stateFile, data, 0o644); err != nil {
   497→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to write session state: %w", err)
   498→	}
   499→	return nil
   500→}

   526→// runGeminiHookWithInput runs a Gemini hook with the given input.
   527→func (r *GeminiHookRunner) runGeminiHookWithInput(hookName string, input interface{}) error {
   528→	r.T.Helper()
   529→
   530→	inputJSON, err := json.Marshal(input)
   531→	if err != nil {
   532→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal hook input: %w", err)

   537→
   538→func (r *GeminiHookRunner) runGeminiHookInRepoDir(hookName string, inputJSON []byte) error {
   539→	// Run using the shared test binary
   540→	// Command structure: entire hooks gemini <hook-name>
   541→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "gemini", hookName)
   542→	cmd.Dir = r.RepoDir
   543→	cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(inputJSON)
   544→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   545→		"ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR="+r.GeminiProjectDir,
   546→	)
   547→
   548→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   549→	if err != nil {
   550→		return fmt.Errorf("hook %s failed: %w\nInput: %s\nOutput: %s",
   551→			hookName, err, inputJSON, output)
   552→	}
   553→
   554→	r.T.Logf("Gemini hook %s output: %s", hookName, output)
   555→	return nil
   556→}
   557→
   558→// runGeminiHookWithOutput runs a Gemini hook and returns both stdout and stderr separately.
   559→func (r *GeminiHookRunner) runGeminiHookWithOutput(hookName string, inputJSON []byte) HookOutput {
   560→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "gemini", hookName)
   561→	cmd.Dir = r.RepoDir
   562→	cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(inputJSON)
   563→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   564→		"ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR="+r.GeminiProjectDir,
   565→	)
   566→
   567→	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
   568→	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
   569→	cmd.Stderr = &stderr
   570→
   571→	err := cmd.Run()
   572→	return HookOutput{
   573→		Stdout: stdout.Bytes(),
   574→		Stderr: stderr.Bytes(),
   575→		Err:    err,
   576→	}
   577→}

   657→// NewGeminiSession creates a new simulated Gemini session.
   658→func (env *TestEnv) NewGeminiSession() *GeminiSession {
   659→	env.T.Helper()
   660→
   661→	env.SessionCounter++
   662→	sessionID := fmt.Sprintf("gemini-session-%d", env.SessionCounter)

   672→// CreateGeminiTranscript creates a Gemini JSON transcript file for the session.
   673→func (s *GeminiSession) CreateGeminiTranscript(prompt string, changes []FileChange) string {
   674→	// Build Gemini-format transcript (JSON, not JSONL)
   675→	messages := []map[string]interface{}{
   676→		{
   677→			"type":    "user",
   678→			"content": prompt,
   679→		},
   680→		{
   681→			"type":    "assistant",
   682→			"content": "I'll help you with that.",
   683→		},
   684→	}
   685→
   686→	for _, change := range changes {
   687→		messages = append(messages, map[string]interface{}{
   688→			"type": "tool_use",
   689→			"name": "write_file",
   690→			"input": map[string]string{
   691→				"path":    change.Path,
   692→				"content": change.Content,
   693→			},
   694→		})
   695→		messages = append(messages, map[string]interface{}{
   696→			"type":   "tool_result",
   697→			"output": "File written successfully",
   698→		})
   699→	}
   700→
   701→	messages = append(messages, map[string]interface{}{
   702→		"type":    "assistant",
   703→		"content": "Done!",
   704→	})
   705→
   706→	transcript := map[string]interface{}{
   707→		"sessionId": s.ID,
   708→		"messages":  messages,
   709→	}
   710→
   711→	// Ensure directory exists
   712→	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(s.TranscriptPath), 0o755); err != nil {
   713→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to create transcript dir: %v", err)
   714→	}
   715→
   716→	// Write transcript
   717→	data, err := json.MarshalIndent(transcript, "", "  ")
   718→	if err != nil {
   719→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to marshal transcript: %v", err)
   720→	}
   721→	if err := os.WriteFile(s.TranscriptPath, data, 0o644); err != nil {
   722→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to write transcript: %v", err)
   723→	}
   724→
   725→	return s.TranscriptPath
   726→}

   871→// NewOpenCodeSession creates a new simulated OpenCode session.
   872→func (env *TestEnv) NewOpenCodeSession() *OpenCodeSession {
   873→	env.T.Helper()
   874→
   875→	env.SessionCounter++
   876→	sessionID := fmt.Sprintf("opencode-session-%d", env.SessionCounter)
   877→	transcriptPath := filepath.Join(env.OpenCodeProjectDir, sessionID+".jsonl")
   878→

   885→
   886→// CreateOpenCodeTranscript creates an OpenCode JSONL transcript file for the session.
   887→// Each line is a JSON message in OpenCode's format (id, role, content, time, tokens, parts).
   888→func (s *OpenCodeSession) CreateOpenCodeTranscript(prompt string, changes []FileChange) string {
   889→	var lines []string
   890→
   891→	// User message
   892→	s.msgCounter++
   893→	userMsg := map[string]interface{}{
   894→		"id":      fmt.Sprintf("msg-%d", s.msgCounter),
   895→		"role":    "user",
   896→		"content": prompt,
   897→		"time":    map[string]interface{}{"created": 1708300000 + s.msgCounter},
   898→	}
   899→	userJSON, _ := json.Marshal(userMsg)
   900→	lines = append(lines, string(userJSON))
   901→
   902→	// Assistant message with tool calls for file changes
   903→	s.msgCounter++
   904→	var parts []map[string]interface{}
   905→	parts = append(parts, map[string]interface{}{
   906→		"type": "text",
   907→		"text": "I'll help you with that.",
   908→	})
   909→	for i, change := range changes {
   910→		parts = append(parts, map[string]interface{}{
   911→			"type":   "tool",
   912→			"tool":   "write",
   913→			"callID": fmt.Sprintf("call-%d", i+1),
   914→			"state": map[string]interface{}{
   915→				"status": "completed",
   916→				"input":  map[string]string{"file_path": change.Path},
   917→				"output": "File written: " + change.Path,
   918→			},
   919→		})
   920→	}
   921→
   922→	asstMsg := map[string]interface{}{
   923→		"id":      fmt.Sprintf("msg-%d", s.msgCounter),
   924→		"role":    "assistant",
   925→		"content": "Done!",
   926→		"time": map[string]interface{}{
   927→			"created":   1708300000 + s.msgCounter,
   928→			"completed": 1708300000 + s.msgCounter + 5,
   929→		},
   930→		"tokens": map[string]interface{}{
   931→			"input":     150,
   932→			"output":    80,
   933→			"reasoning": 10,
   934→			"cache":     map[string]int{"read": 5, "write": 15},
   935→		},
   936→		"cost":  0.003,
   937→		"parts": parts,
   938→	}
   939→	asstJSON, _ := json.Marshal(asstMsg)
   940→	lines = append(lines, string(asstJSON))
   941→
   942→	// Ensure directory exists
   943→	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(s.TranscriptPath), 0o755); err != nil {
   944→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to create transcript dir: %v", err)
   945→	}
   946→
   947→	// Write JSONL transcript (one message per line)
   948→	content := strings.Join(lines, "\n") + "\n"
   949→	if err := os.WriteFile(s.TranscriptPath, []byte(content), 0o644); err != nil {
   950→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to write transcript: %v", err)
   951→	}
   952→
   953→	return s.TranscriptPath
   954→}
   955→

   957→func (env *TestEnv) SimulateOpenCodeSessionStart(sessionID, transcriptPath string) error {
   958→	env.T.Helper()
   959→	runner := NewOpenCodeHookRunner(env.RepoDir, env.OpenCodeProjectDir, env.T)
   960→	return runner.SimulateOpenCodeSessionStart(sessionID, transcriptPath)
   961→}
