```
     1→// Package benchutil provides test fixture helpers for CLI benchmarks.
     2→//
     3→// It creates realistic git repositories, transcripts, session states,
     4→// and checkpoint data for benchmarking the hot paths (SaveStep, PostCommit/Condense).
     5→package benchutil
     6→
     7→import (
     8→	"context"
     9→	"encoding/json"
    10→	"fmt"
    11→	"os"
    12→	"os/exec"
    13→	"path/filepath"
    14→	"strings"
    15→	"testing"
    16→	"time"
    17→
    18→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent"
    19→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint"
    20→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/id"
    21→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/jsonutil"
    22→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths"
    23→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/session"
    24→
    25→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5"
    26→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5/plumbing"
    27→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5/plumbing/object"
    28→)
    29→
    30→// BenchRepo is a fully initialized git repository with Entire configured,
    31→// ready for checkpoint benchmarks.
    32→type BenchRepo struct {
    33→	// Dir is the absolute path to the repository root.
    34→	Dir string
    35→
    36→	// Repo is the go-git repository handle.
    37→	Repo *git.Repository
    38→
    39→	// Store is the checkpoint GitStore for this repo.
    40→	Store *checkpoint.GitStore
    41→
    42→	// HeadHash is the current HEAD commit hash string.
    43→	HeadHash string
    44→
    45→	// WorktreeID is the worktree identifier (empty for main worktree).
    46→	WorktreeID string
    47→}
    48→
    49→// RepoOpts configures how NewBenchRepo creates the test repository.
    50→type RepoOpts struct {
    51→	// FileCount is the number of tracked files to create in the initial commit.
    52→	// Each file is ~100 lines of Go code. Defaults to 10.
    53→	FileCount int
    54→
    55→	// FileSizeLines is the number of lines per file. Defaults to 100.
    56→	FileSizeLines int
    57→
    58→	// CommitCount is the number of commits to create. Defaults to 1.
    59→	CommitCount int
    60→
    61→	// Strategy is the strategy name for .entire/settings.json.
    62→	// Defaults to "manual-commit".
    63→	Strategy string
    64→
    65→	// FeatureBranch, if non-empty, creates and checks out this branch
    66→	// after the initial commits.
    67→	FeatureBranch string
    68→}
    69→
    70→func (o *RepoOpts) withDefaults() RepoOpts {
    71→	out := *o
    72→	if out.FileCount == 0 {
    73→		out.FileCount = 10
    74→	}
    75→	if out.FileSizeLines == 0 {
    76→		out.FileSizeLines = 100
    77→	}
    78→	if out.CommitCount == 0 {
    79→		out.CommitCount = 1
    80→	}
    81→	if out.Strategy == "" {
    82→		out.Strategy = "manual-commit"
    83→	}
    84→	return out
    85→}
    86→
    87→// NewBenchRepo creates an isolated git repository for benchmarks.
    88→// The repo has an initial commit with the configured number of files,
    89→// a .gitignore excluding .entire/, and Entire settings initialized.
    90→//
    91→// Uses b.TempDir() so cleanup is automatic.
    92→func NewBenchRepo(b *testing.B, opts RepoOpts) *BenchRepo {
    93→	b.Helper()
    94→	opts = opts.withDefaults()
    95→
    96→	dir := b.TempDir()
    97→	// Resolve symlinks (macOS /var -> /private/var)
    98→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(dir); err == nil {
    99→		dir = resolved
   100→	}
   101→
   102→	// Init repo
   103→	repo, err := git.PlainInit(dir, false)
   104→	if err != nil {
   105→		b.Fatalf("git init: %v", err)
   106→	}
   107→
   108→	// Create .gitignore and .entire settings
   109→	writeFile(b, dir, ".gitignore", ".entire/\n")
   110→	initEntireSettings(b, dir, opts.Strategy)
   111→
   112→	// Generate initial files
   113→	wt, err := repo.Worktree()
   114→	if err != nil {
   115→		b.Fatalf("worktree: %v", err)
   116→	}
   117→
   118→	for i := range opts.FileCount {
   119→		name := fmt.Sprintf("src/file_%03d.go", i)
   120→		content := GenerateGoFile(i, opts.FileSizeLines)
   121→		writeFile(b, dir, name, content)
   122→		if _, err := wt.Add(name); err != nil {
   123→			b.Fatalf("add %s: %v", name, err)
   124→		}
   125→	}
   126→	if _, err := wt.Add(".gitignore"); err != nil {
   127→		b.Fatalf("add .gitignore: %v", err)
   128→	}
   129→
   130→	// Create commits
   131→	var headHash plumbing.Hash
   132→	for c := range opts.CommitCount {
   133→		if c > 0 {
   134→			// Modify a file for subsequent commits
   135→			name := fmt.Sprintf("src/file_%03d.go", c%opts.FileCount)
   136→			content := GenerateGoFile(c*1000, opts.FileSizeLines)
   137→			writeFile(b, dir, name, content)
   138→			if _, err := wt.Add(name); err != nil {
   139→				b.Fatalf("add %s: %v", name, err)
   140→			}
   141→		}
   142→		headHash, err = wt.Commit(fmt.Sprintf("Commit %d", c+1), &git.CommitOptions{
   143→			Author: &object.Signature{
   144→				Name:  "Bench User",
   145→				Email: "bench@example.com",
   146→				When:  time.Now(),
   147→			},
   148→		})
   149→		if err != nil {
   150→			b.Fatalf("commit %d: %v", c+1, err)
   151→		}
   152→	}
   153→
   154→	// Optionally create feature branch
   155→	if opts.FeatureBranch != "" {
   156→		ref := plumbing.NewHashReference(
   157→			plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(opts.FeatureBranch), headHash)
   158→		if err := repo.Storer.SetReference(ref); err != nil {
   159→			b.Fatalf("create branch: %v", err)
   160→		}
   161→		// Checkout via git CLI (go-git v5 checkout bug)
   162→		checkoutBranch(b, dir, opts.FeatureBranch)
   163→	}
   164→
   165→	br := &BenchRepo{
   166→		Dir:      dir,
   167→		Repo:     repo,
   168→		Store:    checkpoint.NewGitStore(repo),
   169→		HeadHash: headHash.String(),
   170→	}
   171→
   172→	// Determine worktree ID
   173→	wtID, err := paths.GetWorktreeID(dir)
   174→	if err == nil {
   175→		br.WorktreeID = wtID
   176→	}
   177→
   178→	return br
   179→}
   180→
   181→// WriteFile creates or overwrites a file relative to the repo root.
   182→func (br *BenchRepo) WriteFile(b *testing.B, relPath, content string) {
   183→	b.Helper()
   184→	writeFile(b, br.Dir, relPath, content)
   185→}
   186→
   187→// AddAndCommit stages the given files and creates a commit.
   188→// Returns the new HEAD hash.
   189→func (br *BenchRepo) AddAndCommit(b *testing.B, message string, files ...string) string {
   190→	b.Helper()
   191→	wt, err := br.Repo.Worktree()
   192→	if err != nil {
   193→		b.Fatalf("worktree: %v", err)
   194→	}
   195→	for _, f := range files {
   196→		if _, err := wt.Add(f); err != nil {
   197→			b.Fatalf("add %s: %v", f, err)
   198→		}
   199→	}
   200→	hash, err := wt.Commit(message, &git.CommitOptions{
   201→		Author: &object.Signature{
   202→			Name:  "Bench User",
   203→			Email: "bench@example.com",
   204→			When:  time.Now(),
   205→		},
   206→	})
   207→	if err != nil {
   208→		b.Fatalf("commit: %v", err)
   209→	}
   210→	br.HeadHash = hash.String()
   211→	return hash.String()
   212→}
   213→
   214→// SessionOpts configures how CreateSessionState creates a session state file.
   215→type SessionOpts struct {
   216→	// SessionID is the session identifier. Auto-generated if empty.
   217→	SessionID string
   218→
   219→	// Phase is the session phase. Defaults to session.PhaseActive.
   220→	Phase session.Phase
   221→
   222→	// StepCount is the number of prior checkpoints. Defaults to 0.
   223→	StepCount int
   224→
   225→	// FilesTouched is the list of files tracked by this session.
   226→	FilesTouched []string
   227→
   228→	// TranscriptPath is the path to the live transcript file.
   229→	TranscriptPath string
   230→
   231→	// AgentType is the agent type. Defaults to agent.AgentTypeClaudeCode.
   232→	AgentType agent.AgentType
   233→}
   234→
   235→// CreateSessionState writes a session state file to .git/entire-sessions/.
   236→// Returns the session ID used.
   237→func (br *BenchRepo) CreateSessionState(b *testing.B, opts SessionOpts) string {
   238→	b.Helper()
   239→
   240→	if opts.SessionID == "" {
   241→		cpID, err := id.Generate()
   242→		if err != nil {
   243→			b.Fatalf("generate session ID: %v", err)
   244→		}
   245→		opts.SessionID = fmt.Sprintf("bench-%s", cpID)
   246→	}
   247→	if opts.Phase == "" {
   248→		opts.Phase = session.PhaseActive
   249→	}
   250→
   251→	if opts.AgentType == "" {
   252→		opts.AgentType = agent.AgentTypeClaudeCode
   253→	}
   254→
   255→	now := time.Now()
   256→	state := &session.State{
   257→		SessionID:      opts.SessionID,
   258→		BaseCommit:     br.HeadHash,
   259→		WorktreePath:   br.Dir,
   260→		WorktreeID:     br.WorktreeID,
   261→		StartedAt:      now,
   262→		Phase:          opts.Phase,
   263→		StepCount:      opts.StepCount,
   264→		FilesTouched:   opts.FilesTouched,
   265→		TranscriptPath: opts.TranscriptPath,
   266→		AgentType:      opts.AgentType,
   267→	}
   268→
   269→	// Write to .git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json
   270→	gitDir := filepath.Join(br.Dir, ".git")
   271→	sessDir := filepath.Join(gitDir, session.SessionStateDirName)
   272→	if err := os.MkdirAll(sessDir, 0o750); err != nil {
   273→		b.Fatalf("mkdir sessions: %v", err)
   274→	}
   275→
   276→	data, err := jsonutil.MarshalIndentWithNewline(state, "", "  ")
   277→	if err != nil {
   278→		b.Fatalf("marshal state: %v", err)
   279→	}
   280→
   281→	statePath := filepath.Join(sessDir, opts.SessionID+".json")
   282→	if err := os.WriteFile(statePath, data, 0o600); err != nil {
   283→		b.Fatalf("write state: %v", err)
   284→	}
   285→
   286→	return opts.SessionID
   287→}
   288→
   289→// TranscriptOpts configures how GenerateTranscript creates JSONL data.
   290→type TranscriptOpts struct {
   291→	// MessageCount is the number of JSONL messages to generate.
   292→	MessageCount int
   293→
   294→	// AvgMessageBytes is the approximate size of each message's content field.
   295→	// Defaults to 500.
   296→	AvgMessageBytes int
   297→
   298→	// IncludeToolUse adds realistic tool_use messages (file edits, bash commands).
   299→	IncludeToolUse bool
   300→
   301→	// FilesTouched is the list of files to reference in tool_use messages.
   302→	// Only used when IncludeToolUse is true.
   303→	FilesTouched []string
   304→}
   305→
   306→// GenerateTranscript creates realistic Claude Code JSONL transcript data.
   307→// Returns the raw bytes suitable for writing to full.jsonl.
   308→func GenerateTranscript(opts TranscriptOpts) []byte {
   309→	if opts.AvgMessageBytes == 0 {
   310→		opts.AvgMessageBytes = 500
   311→	}
   312→
   313→	var buf strings.Builder
   314→	for i := range opts.MessageCount {
   315→		msg := generateTranscriptMessage(i, opts)
   316→		data, err := json.Marshal(msg)
   317→		if err != nil {
   318→			// Should never happen with map[string]any, but satisfy errcheck
   319→			continue
   320→		}
   321→		buf.Write(data)
   322→		buf.WriteByte('\n')
   323→	}
   324→	return []byte(buf.String())
   325→}
   326→
   327→// WriteTranscriptFile writes transcript data to a file and returns the path.
   328→func (br *BenchRepo) WriteTranscriptFile(b *testing.B, sessionID string, data []byte) string {
   329→	b.Helper()
   330→	// Write to .entire/metadata/<session-id>/full.jsonl (matching real layout)
   331→	relDir := filepath.Join(".entire", "metadata", sessionID)
   332→	relPath := filepath.Join(relDir, "full.jsonl")
   333→	absDir := filepath.Join(br.Dir, relDir)
   334→	if err := os.MkdirAll(absDir, 0o750); err != nil {
   335→		b.Fatalf("mkdir transcript dir: %v", err)
   336→	}
   337→	absPath := filepath.Join(br.Dir, relPath)
   338→	if err := os.WriteFile(absPath, data, 0o600); err != nil {
   339→		b.Fatalf("write transcript: %v", err)
   340→	}
   341→	return absPath
   342→}
   343→
   344→// SeedShadowBranch creates N checkpoint commits on the shadow branch
   345→// for the current HEAD. This simulates a session that already has
   346→// prior checkpoints saved.
   347→func (br *BenchRepo) SeedShadowBranch(b *testing.B, sessionID string, checkpointCount int, filesPerCheckpoint int) {
   348→	b.Helper()
   349→
   350→	for i := range checkpointCount {
   351→		var modified []string
   352→		for j := range filesPerCheckpoint {
   353→			name := fmt.Sprintf("src/file_%03d.go", j)
   354→			content := GenerateGoFile(i*1000+j, 100)
   355→			writeFile(b, br.Dir, name, content)
   356→			modified = append(modified, name)
   357→		}
   358→
   359→		metadataDir := paths.SessionMetadataDirFromSessionID(sessionID)
   360→		metadataDirAbs := filepath.Join(br.Dir, metadataDir)
   361→		if err := os.MkdirAll(metadataDirAbs, 0o750); err != nil {
   362→			b.Fatalf("mkdir metadata: %v", err)
   363→		}
   364→
   365→		// Write a minimal transcript to the metadata dir
   366→		transcriptPath := filepath.Join(metadataDirAbs, "full.jsonl")
   367→		transcript := GenerateTranscript(TranscriptOpts{MessageCount: 5, AvgMessageBytes: 200})
   368→		if err := os.WriteFile(transcriptPath, transcript, 0o600); err != nil {
   369→			b.Fatalf("write transcript: %v", err)
   370→		}
   371→
   372→		_, err := br.Store.WriteTemporary(context.Background(), checkpoint.WriteTemporaryOptions{
   373→			SessionID:         sessionID,
   374→			BaseCommit:        br.HeadHash,
   375→			WorktreeID:        br.WorktreeID,
   376→			ModifiedFiles:     modified,
   377→			MetadataDir:       metadataDir,
   378→			MetadataDirAbs:    metadataDirAbs,
   379→			CommitMessage:     fmt.Sprintf("Checkpoint %d", i+1),
   380→			AuthorName:        "Bench User",
   381→			AuthorEmail:       "bench@example.com",
   382→			IsFirstCheckpoint: i == 0,
   383→		})
   384→		if err != nil {
   385→			b.Fatalf("write temporary checkpoint %d: %v", i+1, err)
   386→		}
   387→	}
   388→}
   389→
   390→// SeedMetadataBranch creates N committed checkpoints on the entire/checkpoints/v1
   391→// branch. This simulates a repository with prior checkpoint history.
   392→func (br *BenchRepo) SeedMetadataBranch(b *testing.B, checkpointCount int) {
   393→	b.Helper()
   394→
   395→	for i := range checkpointCount {
   396→		cpID, err := id.Generate()
   397→		if err != nil {
   398→			b.Fatalf("generate checkpoint ID: %v", err)
   399→		}
   400→		sessionID := fmt.Sprintf("seed-session-%04d", i)
   401→		transcript := GenerateTranscript(TranscriptOpts{
   402→			MessageCount:    20,
   403→			AvgMessageBytes: 300,
   404→		})
   405→
   406→		files := make([]string, 0, 5)
   407→		for j := range 5 {
   408→			files = append(files, fmt.Sprintf("src/file_%03d.go", (i*5+j)%100))
   409→		}
   410→
   411→		err = br.Store.WriteCommitted(context.Background(), checkpoint.WriteCommittedOptions{
   412→			CheckpointID:     cpID,
   413→			SessionID:        sessionID,
   414→			Strategy:         "manual-commit",
   415→			Transcript:       transcript,
   416→			Prompts:          []string{fmt.Sprintf("Implement feature %d", i)},
   417→			FilesTouched:     files,
   418→			CheckpointsCount: 3,
   419→			AuthorName:       "Bench User",
   420→			AuthorEmail:      "bench@example.com",
   421→			Agent:            agent.AgentTypeClaudeCode,
   422→		})
   423→		if err != nil {
   424→			b.Fatalf("write committed checkpoint %d: %v", i+1, err)
   425→		}
   426→	}
   427→}
   428→
   429→// GenerateGoFile creates a synthetic Go source file with the given number of lines.
   430→// The seed value ensures unique content for each file.
   431→func GenerateGoFile(seed, lines int) string {
   432→	var buf strings.Builder
   433→	fmt.Fprintf(&buf, "package pkg%d\n\n", seed%100)
   434→
   435→	lineNum := 2
   436→	funcNum := 0
   437→	for lineNum < lines {
   438→		funcName := fmt.Sprintf("func%d_%d", seed, funcNum)
   439→		fmt.Fprintf(&buf, "func %s(ctx context.Context, input string) (string, error) {\n", funcName)
   440→		lineNum++
   441→
   442→		bodyLines := min(8, lines-lineNum-1)
   443→		for j := range bodyLines {
   444→			fmt.Fprintf(&buf, "\tv%d := fmt.Sprintf(\"processing %%s step %d seed %d\", input)\n", j, j, seed)
   445→			lineNum++
   446→		}
   447→		buf.WriteString("\treturn \"\", nil\n}\n\n")
   448→		lineNum += 2
   449→		funcNum++
   450→	}
   451→	return buf.String()
   452→}
   453→
   454→// GenerateFileContent creates generic file content of approximately the given byte size.
   455→func GenerateFileContent(seed, sizeBytes int) string {
   456→	var buf strings.Builder
   457→	line := fmt.Sprintf("// Line content seed=%d ", seed)
   458→	padding := strings.Repeat("x", max(1, 80-len(line)))
   459→	fullLine := line + padding + "\n"
   460→
   461→	for buf.Len() < sizeBytes {
   462→		buf.WriteString(fullLine)
   463→	}
   464→	return buf.String()
   465→}
   466→
   467→//nolint:gosec // G301/G306: benchmark fixtures use standard permissions in temp dirs
   468→func writeFile(b *testing.B, dir, relPath, content string) {
   469→	b.Helper()
   470→	abs := filepath.Join(dir, relPath)
   471→	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(abs), 0o755); err != nil {
   472→		b.Fatalf("mkdir %s: %v", filepath.Dir(relPath), err)
   473→	}
   474→	if err := os.WriteFile(abs, []byte(content), 0o644); err != nil {
   475→		b.Fatalf("write %s: %v", relPath, err)
   476→	}
   477→}
   478→
   479→//nolint:gosec // G301/G306: benchmark fixtures use standard permissions in temp dirs
   480→func initEntireSettings(b *testing.B, dir, strategy string) {
   481→	b.Helper()
   482→	entireDir := filepath.Join(dir, ".entire")
   483→	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Join(entireDir, "tmp"), 0o755); err != nil {
   484→		b.Fatalf("mkdir .entire: %v", err)

   486→
   487→	settings := map[string]any{
   488→		"strategy":  strategy,
   489→		"local_dev": true,
   490→	}
   491→	data, err := jsonutil.MarshalIndentWithNewline(settings, "", "  ")
   492→	if err != nil {
   493→		b.Fatalf("marshal settings: %v", err)

   495→	if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(entireDir, paths.SettingsFileName), data, 0o644); err != nil {
   496→		b.Fatalf("write settings: %v", err)

   499→
   500→func checkoutBranch(b *testing.B, dir, branch string) {
   501→	b.Helper()
   502→	c := exec.CommandContext(context.Background(), "git", "checkout", branch)
   503→	c.Dir = dir
   504→	if output, err := c.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   505→		b.Fatalf("git checkout %s: %v\n%s", branch, err, output)
   506→	}
   507→}
   508→
   509→// generateTranscriptMessage creates a single JSONL message for a Claude Code transcript.
   510→func generateTranscriptMessage(index int, opts TranscriptOpts) map[string]any {
   511→	msg := map[string]any{
   512→		"uuid":        fmt.Sprintf("msg_%06d", index),
   513→		"timestamp":   time.Now().Add(time.Duration(index) * time.Second).Format(time.RFC3339),
   514→		"parent_uuid": fmt.Sprintf("msg_%06d", max(0, index-1)),
   515→	}
   516→
   517→	switch {
   518→	case opts.IncludeToolUse && index%3 == 2 && len(opts.FilesTouched) > 0:
   519→		// Tool use message (every 3rd message)
   520→		file := opts.FilesTouched[index%len(opts.FilesTouched)]
   521→		msg["type"] = "tool_use"
   522→		msg["tool_name"] = "write_to_file"
   523→		msg["tool_input"] = map[string]any{
   524→			"path":    file,
   525→			"content": GenerateFileContent(index, opts.AvgMessageBytes/2),
   526→		}
   527→	case index%2 == 0:
   528→		// Assistant message
   529→		msg["type"] = "assistant"
   530→		msg["content"] = generatePadding("I'll help you implement this feature. ", opts.AvgMessageBytes)
   531→	default:
   532→		// Human message
   533→		msg["type"] = "human"
   534→		msg["content"] = generatePadding("Please update the implementation. ", opts.AvgMessageBytes/3)
   535→	}
   536→
   537→	return msg
   538→}
   539→
   540→func generatePadding(prefix string, targetBytes int) string {
   541→	if len(prefix) >= targetBytes {
   542→		return prefix[:targetBytes]
   543→	}
   544→	padding := strings.Repeat("Lorem ipsum dolor sit amet. ", (targetBytes-len(prefix))/28+1)
   545→	result := prefix + padding
   546→	if len(result) > targetBytes {
   547→		return result[:targetBytes]
   548→	}
   549→	return result
   550→}
   551→
```

```
    33→	}

    36→	code := m.Run()
    37→
```

> AGENT

Everything works. Here's a summary:

**Created:** `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/hook_bench_test.go`

**Benchmark results** (Apple M4 Pro):

| Scenario | Latency |
|---|---|
| NoSessions | ~66ms |
| 1Session | ~83ms |
| 5Sessions | ~83ms |
| 20Sessions | ~84ms |

The benchmark measures the full subprocess spawn + execution of `entire hooks claude-code session-start`, which is what Claude Code users experience on startup. The ~17ms gap between 0 sessions and 1+ sessions suggests the cost is mostly in session state directory scanning rather than per-file processing (20 sessions costs essentially the same as 1).

**Verification:**
- `mise run fmt` — clean
- `mise run lint` — 0 issues
- `mise run test:ci` — all tests pass (no regressions)

> DEVELOPER

how can i test this specifically?

> AGENT

```bash
go test -tags=integration -bench=BenchmarkHookSessionStart -benchtime=10x -run='^$' -timeout=10m ./cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/...
```

- `-bench=BenchmarkHookSessionStart` — runs only this benchmark
- `-benchtime=10x` — 10 iterations per scenario (increase for more stable numbers)
- `-run='^$'` — skips regular tests (only benchmarks)
- `-timeout=10m` — generous timeout for the binary build + runs

To run a single scenario:

```bash
go test -tags=integration -bench=BenchmarkHookSessionStart/20Sessions -benchtime=10x -run='^$' ./cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/...
```

```
Excellent! Now I have a comprehensive picture. Let me create a final summary document based on all the code I've reviewed.

## Summary: Session-Start Hook Performance Analysis

Based on my analysis of the codebase, here's a detailed breakdown of what the `session-start` hook for Claude Code does internally and where performance issues scale with large repositories:

### Hot Path - Session-Start Hook Execution Flow

The session-start hook is dispatched as follows:

**Entry Point:** `entire hooks claude-code session-start` (line 69 in hook_bench_test.go)

**Dispatch Chain** (from hook_registry.go):
1. `newAgentHookVerbCmdWithLogging()` - Validates repo, parses hook event
2. `DispatchLifecycleEvent()` (lifecycle.go:31-57)
3. `handleLifecycleSessionStart()` (lifecycle.go:61-108)

### Detailed Operations in Session-Start

In `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/lifecycle.go`, the `handleLifecycleSessionStart()` function performs:

1. **Concurrent Session Check** (lines 80-84):
   - Calls `strategy.ConcurrentSessionChecker.CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints()`
   - This is where the scaling issues begin

2. **Session State Loading** (lines 96-105):
   - Loads session state from `.git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json`
   - Performs state machine transition

### The Performance Bottleneck: `CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints()`

From `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_session.go` (lines 153-188):

```
CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints():
  1. GetWorktreePath()               // git rev-parse --show-toplevel
  2. OpenRepository()                // Opens .git repo (with go-git)
  3. repo.Head()                     // Get current HEAD commit hash
  4. listAllSessionStates()           // **EXPENSIVE - scales with session count**
     └─> store.List()
         └─> os.ReadDir(.git/entire-sessions/)  // Read all session state files
         └─> For each session state file:
             ├─> Load and unmarshal JSON
             ├─> repo.Reference(shadowBranchName, true)  // **Check if shadow branch exists**
             └─> Clean up orphaned sessions
  5. Loop through all loaded sessions and count matches
```

### Operations that Scale Poorly

**1. Session State Loading (`listAllSessionStates()` at lines 60-102):**
- **Cost scales with:** Number of session state files in `.git/entire-sessions/`
- **Operations:**
  - `os.ReadDir()` - List all `.json` files in state directory
  - For each file: `s.Load()` which does:
    - `os.ReadFile()` - Read entire session state JSON file
    - `json.Unmarshal()` - Deserialize JSON
    - `state.NormalizeAfterLoad()` - Normalize legacy fields
  - For each session state: `repo.Reference(refName, true)` - Check if shadow branch exists (git ref lookup)
  - Cleanup of orphaned sessions if branch is missing

**2. Shadow Branch Reference Lookup (`repo.Reference()` at line 91):**
- **Cost scales with:** Number of git refs in repository
- Each reference lookup may require scanning packed refs or ref files
- In large repos with many branches (main branch + feature branches + `entire/checkpoints/v1` + shadow branches), this is expensive
- Called once per active session state file

**3. Git HEAD Lookup (`repo.Head()` at line 164):**
- Relatively cheap, but happens in the hot path

### Key Scaling Factors

| Operation | Scales With | Impact |
|-----------|------------|--------|
| `os.ReadDir(.git/entire-sessions/)` | Number of session state files | Linear: O(N) |
| JSON unmarshal per state file | Size of each state file | Linear per file |
| `repo.Reference()` per session | Number of git refs + session count | O(N * M) where N=sessions, M=refs |
| State file disk I/O | Number and size of state files | Linear: O(N) |

### Concrete Example from Benchmark

From `hook_bench_test.go`:
- **0 sessions:** Fast (minimal overhead)
- **1 session:** 1x `repo.Reference()` call + 1x state file read
- **5 sessions:** 5x `repo.Reference()` calls + 5x state file reads
- **20 sessions:** 20x `repo.Reference()` calls + 20x state file reads

Each `repo.Reference()` call scans git refs, which in a large repo with:
- `~100` feature branches
- `~1` metadata branch (`entire/checkpoints/v1`)
- `~20-50` shadow branches (`entire/<hash>-<worktreeHash>`)
- Could perform expensive ref file scanning

### File I/O Operations During Session-Start

1. `repo.Head()` - Git metadata read
2. `os.ReadDir(.git/entire-sessions/)` - List state files
3. For each session: `os.ReadFile()` - Read state JSON files
4. For each session: Git ref lookup (internal go-git I/O)
5. Session state transitions (read/write to state machine)
6. Git hook installation check (`IsGitHookInstalled()`) - reads `.git/hooks/` directory

### Strategy-Specific: Manual-Commit vs Auto-Commit

**Manual-Commit** (which appears to be in use based on code):
- Uses `.git/entire-sessions/` for session state (shared across worktrees)
- Performs shadow branch existence checks per session
- More expensive in large repos due to ref scanning

**Auto-Commit**:
- Similar operations but different checkpoint storage strategy
- Also calls `listAllSessionStates()` indirectly through session initialization

### Summary of Hot Path Costs

**O(N) where N = number of active session state files:**
- Reading state directory contents
- Loading and deserializing each state file
- For each state file: Git reference lookup (which scans refs)

**O(M) where M = number of git refs (branches/tags):**
- Each `repo.Reference()` call performs a ref scan in large repos

**Combined Cost:** O(N × M) in large repos with many active sessions and many refs

### Files Involved in Session-Start

- `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/lifecycle.go` - Main dispatcher
- `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_session.go` - Session counting logic
- `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/session/state.go` - State storage I/O
- `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/common.go` - Git operations
- `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/hook_registry.go` - Hook dispatch
```

> AGENT

The benchmark is fast because the test repo is tiny. Here's what the session-start hook actually does and what scales poorly:

### The hot path

1. **`CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints()`** — this is the bottleneck
2. Lists all files in `.git/entire-sessions/`
3. For **each** session state file: reads + unmarshals JSON, then calls `repo.Reference()` to check if its shadow branch exists
4. Each `repo.Reference()` call scans git refs

### What scales poorly

| Factor | Real customer repo | Our benchmark |
|---|---|---|
| Session state files | 10s-100s (never cleaned up?) | 0-20 |
| Git refs (branches) | 100s+ (features, shadow, tags) | ~2 |
| Repo size (go-git open) | Large `.git/` | Tiny |
| Packed refs scanning | Slow per `repo.Reference()` call | Nearly free |

The cost is **O(sessions × refs)** — each session does a ref lookup that scans all refs. In a repo with 50 stale session files and 200+ branches, that's thousands of ref scans.

### To reproduce the 10s latency

We'd need to make the benchmark more realistic by:
1. Seeding many git refs (branches/tags)
2. Creating more session state files
3. Possibly seeding shadow branches with checkpoint data

Want me to beef up the benchmark to simulate a large repo, or do you want to look at optimizing the hot path first (e.g., batch the ref lookups, clean up stale sessions, or skip the concurrent session check)?

```
   501→	b.Helper()

   503→	c.Dir = dir

   509→// generateTranscriptMessage creates a single JSONL message for a Claude Code transcript.
   510→func generateTranscriptMessage(index int, opts TranscriptOpts) map[string]any {
   511→	msg := map[string]any{
   512→		"uuid":        fmt.Sprintf("msg_%06d", index),
   513→		"timestamp":   time.Now().Add(time.Duration(index) * time.Second).Format(time.RFC3339),
   514→		"parent_uuid": fmt.Sprintf("msg_%06d", max(0, index-1)),
   515→	}
   516→
   517→	switch {
   518→	case opts.IncludeToolUse && index%3 == 2 && len(opts.FilesTouched) > 0:
   519→		// Tool use message (every 3rd message)
   520→		file := opts.FilesTouched[index%len(opts.FilesTouched)]
   521→		msg["type"] = "tool_use"
   522→		msg["tool_name"] = "write_to_file"
   523→		msg["tool_input"] = map[string]any{
   524→			"path":    file,
   525→			"content": GenerateFileContent(index, opts.AvgMessageBytes/2),
   526→		}
   527→	case index%2 == 0:
   528→		// Assistant message
   529→		msg["type"] = "assistant"
   530→		msg["content"] = generatePadding("I'll help you implement this feature. ", opts.AvgMessageBytes)
   531→	default:
   532→		// Human message
   533→		msg["type"] = "human"
   534→		msg["content"] = generatePadding("Please update the implementation. ", opts.AvgMessageBytes/3)
   535→	}
   536→
   537→	return msg
   538→}
   539→
   540→func generatePadding(prefix string, targetBytes int) string {
   541→	if len(prefix) >= targetBytes {
   542→		return prefix[:targetBytes]
   543→	}
   544→	padding := strings.Repeat("Lorem ipsum dolor sit amet. ", (targetBytes-len(prefix))/28+1)
   545→	result := prefix + padding
   546→	if len(result) > targetBytes {
   547→		return result[:targetBytes]
   548→	}
   549→	return result
   550→}
   551→
```

```json
  "new_string": "// SeedBranches creates N branches pointing at the current HEAD.\n// The branches are named with the given prefix (e.g., \"feature/bench-\" → \"feature/bench-000\").\n// This simulates a repo with many refs, which affects go-git ref scanning performance.\nfunc (br *BenchRepo) SeedBranches(b *testing.B, prefix string, count int) {\n\tb.Helper()\n\theadHash := plumbing.NewHash(br.HeadHash)\n\tfor i := range count {\n\t\tname := fmt.Sprintf(\"%s%03d\", prefix, i)\n\t\tref := plumbing.NewHashReference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(name), headHash)\n\t\tif err := br.Repo.Storer.SetReference(ref); err != nil {\n\t\t\tb.Fatalf(\"create branch %s: %v\", name, err)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// PackRefs runs `git pack-refs --all` to simulate a real repo where most refs\n// are in the packed-refs file. Large repos almost always have packed refs.\nfunc (br *BenchRepo) PackRefs(b *testing.B) {\n\tb.Helper()\n\tcmd := exec.CommandContext(context.Background(), \"git\", \"pack-refs\", \"--all\")\n\tcmd.Dir = br.Dir\n\tif output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {\n\t\tb.Fatalf(\"git pack-refs: %v\\n%s\", err, output)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc generatePadding(prefix string, targetBytes int) string {"
}
```

> AGENT

Interesting — everything is flat at ~80ms regardless of dimension. None of these repo-size factors explain a 10s startup. The bottleneck is likely **not** in the data at all — it's the fixed cost. ~60-80ms is dominated by subprocess spawn + Go binary startup + cobra init.

This means the customer's 10s is probably coming from something else entirely. A few possibilities:

1. **Slow disk** (network filesystem, spinning disk, encrypted FS)
2. **Large `.git/` directory** — we're not simulating a real `.git/objects` pack file. A repo with 100k+ objects in a multi-GB packfile makes `go-git` `PlainOpen` slow
3. **The hook is being called multiple times** per startup
4. **Something outside this hook** — maybe the overall Claude Code hook chain (session-start + other hooks combined)

Want me to:
- **Add a dimension for `.git/` pack size** (seed thousands of objects to bloat the packfile)?
- **Profile the binary** to see where the 80ms is actually spent (subprocess overhead vs actual hook logic)?
- **Investigate if the customer issue is about a different hook** (e.g., `user-prompt-submit` or `stop`)?

> DEVELOPER

list the dimensions which could affect how fast claude code starts up given the entire hooks.

```
Perfect! Now I have enough information to create a comprehensive report. Let me compile all the findings:

## Claude Code Hook Startup Analysis - Complete Report

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here's every hook that fires during Claude Code startup and all I/O operations in each one:

### Registered Hooks (7 total)

Claude Code registers 7 hooks that are installed in `.claude/settings.json`:

1. **session-start** - Lifecycle: `SessionStart` event
2. **user-prompt-submit** - Lifecycle: `TurnStart` event
3. **stop** - Lifecycle: `TurnEnd` event
4. **session-end** - Lifecycle: `SessionEnd` event
5. **pre-task** - Lifecycle: `SubagentStart` event
6. **post-task** - Lifecycle: `SubagentEnd` event
7. **post-todo** - Claude-specific: Incremental checkpoints (special handling, no lifecycle event)

---

## Full Hook Execution Chain & I/O Operations

### 0. HOOK INITIALIZATION (Happens before any hook runs)

**File:** `/cmd/entire/cli/hook_registry.go` (lines 54-63, 111)

When any Claude Code hook command is invoked:
```
entire hooks claude-code <hook-name>
```

**I/O Operations:**
- **PersistentPreRunE:**
  1. Call `initHookLogging()` (from `hooks_git_cmd.go`)
     - `logging.SetLogLevelGetter(GetLogLevel)` - Registers callback
     - `strategy.FindMostRecentSession()` - **READS** `.git/entire-sessions/` directory
       - Lists all session state files from `.git/entire-sessions/*.json`
       - Filters by current worktree (via `GetWorktreePath()`)
       - Returns most recent session ID
     - `logging.Init(sessionID)` - **CREATES/OPENS**:
       - `paths.RepoRoot()` - Git config read (via go-git)
       - `.entire/logs/` directory
       - `.entire/logs/entire.log` file (APPEND mode, 8KB buffer)
     - Returns cleanup function

**Early Exit Checks** (lines 97-106 in hook_registry.go):
  2. `paths.RepoRoot()` - **READS** `.git/` directory (go-git library)
  3. `IsEnabled()` - **READS**:
     - `.entire/settings.json`
     - `.entire/settings.local.json` (if exists)
  4. If not enabled, hook exits silently

---

### Hook Execution: Common Pattern (All Hooks)

**File:** `hook_registry.go` (lines 108-160)

For every hook:

**I/O Before Handler:**
1. `GetStrategy()` - **READS**:
   - `.entire/settings.json`
   - `.entire/settings.local.json` (if exists)
   - Returns strategy instance (default: `manual-commit`)
   
2. Log setup: writes DEBUG log to `.entire/logs/entire.log`

3. `ParseHookEvent(hookName, stdin)` - **READS from stdin**, returns normalized Event

4. `DispatchLifecycleEvent(ag, event)` - Routes to specific handler

**Logging on completion:**
- Writes to `.entire/logs/entire.log` with duration

---

### 1. SESSION-START HOOK

**Files:**
- `agent/claudecode/lifecycle.go` (lines 142-152) - ParseHookEvent
- `lifecycle.go` (lines 61-108) - handleLifecycleSessionStart

**Event Flow:**
```
ParseHookEvent → parseSessionStart()
  ↓
DispatchLifecycleEvent → handleLifecycleSessionStart()
```

**I/O Operations:**

1. **Parse input from stdin:**
   - `ParseHookEvent` reads JSON from stdin

2. **Session validation:**
   - `validation.ValidateSessionID()` - basic format check

3. **Get strategy:**
   - `GetStrategy()` - Reads settings (see above)

4. **Check concurrent sessions:**
   - `strategy.LoadSessionState(event.SessionID)` - **READS** `.git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json`
   - If strategy has `ConcurrentSessionChecker`:
     - `CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints()` - Scans session state files

5. **Output hook response:**
   - `outputHookResponse(message)` - **WRITES to stdout** (JSON)

6. **Transition session phase:**
   - `strategy.LoadSessionState()` - **READS** `.git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json`
   - `strategy.TransitionAndLog()` - **WRITES** `.git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json`
   - `strategy.SaveSessionState()` - **WRITES** `.git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json`

7. **Log output:**
   - Writes to stderr (status messages)
   - Writes to `.entire/logs/entire.log`

---

### 2. USER-PROMPT-SUBMIT HOOK

**Files:**
- `agent/claudecode/lifecycle.go` (lines 155-166) - ParseHookEvent
- `lifecycle.go` (lines 112-147) - handleLifecycleTurnStart

**Event Flow:**
```
ParseHookEvent → parseTurnStart()
  ↓
DispatchLifecycleEvent → handleLifecycleTurnStart()
```

**I/O Operations:**

1. **Parse input from stdin:**
   - Reads JSON from stdin (session_id, transcript_path, prompt)

2. **Capture pre-prompt state:**
   - `CapturePrePromptState(ag, sessionID, event.SessionRef)` (from `state.go`, lines 92-146):
     - `paths.AbsPath()` - Uses repo root
     - **CREATES** `.entire/tmp/` directory
     - Get untracked files:
       - `openRepository()` - Opens `.git/` directory
       - `worktree.Status()` - **READS** working tree and index (git status)
       - **WRITES** `.entire/tmp/pre-prompt-<sessionid>.json`
     - If agent has `TranscriptAnalyzer`:
       - `analyzer.GetTranscriptPosition(sessionRef)` - **READS** transcript file
       - Parses transcript to find position

3. **Ensure strategy setup:**
   - `strat.EnsureSetup()` - Strategy-specific initialization (may create branches)

4. **Initialize session:**
   - `strat.InitializeSession()` - Creates/updates session state

5. **Log output:**
   - Writes to stderr
   - Writes to `.entire/logs/entire.log`

**Key I/O Summary:**
- Reads: `.git/`, working tree status, transcript file
- Writes: `.entire/tmp/pre-prompt-*.json`, git branches/objects, `.git/entire-sessions/`, `.entire/logs/entire.log`

### 3. STOP HOOK (Turn End)

**Files:**
- `agent/claudecode/lifecycle.go` (lines 169-179) - ParseHookEvent
- `lifecycle.go` (lines 150-424) - handleLifecycleTurnEnd (COMPLEX: 8 major steps)

**Event Flow:**
```
ParseHookEvent → parseTurnEnd()
  ↓
DispatchLifecycleEvent → handleLifecycleTurnEnd()
```

**I/O Operations (this is the most I/O-heavy hook):**

1. **Parse input from stdin:**
   - Reads JSON from stdin

2. **Early checks:**
   - `fileExists(transcriptRef)` - **READS** transcript file to verify it exists
   - `strategy.OpenRepository()` - Opens `.git/`
   - `IsEmptyRepository()` - Checks if repo has commits

3. **Create session metadata directory:**
   - `paths.SessionMetadataDirFromSessionID()` - Derives path
   - `os.MkdirAll()` - **CREATES** `.entire/metadata/<session-id>/` directory

4. **Wait for transcript flush (Claude Code specific):**
   - If agent has `TranscriptPreparer`:
     - `PrepareTranscript()` - Polls transcript file for "stop hook sentinel" entry
       - Reads transcript file multiple times (up to 3 second timeout, 50ms intervals)
       - Looks for `hook_progress` entry with sentinel string

5. **Copy transcript to session metadata:**
   - `ag.ReadTranscript(transcriptRef)` - **READS** transcript file
   - `os.WriteFile()` - **WRITES** `.entire/metadata/<session-id>/full.jsonl`
   - Check for export data:
     - `os.ReadFile(exportSrc)` - **READS** `.export.json` (OpenCode plugin data)
     - `os.WriteFile()` - **WRITES** `.entire/metadata/<session-id>/export.json`

6. **Load pre-prompt state:**
   - `LoadPrePromptState()` - **READS** `.entire/tmp/pre-prompt-<sessionid>.json`

7. **Extract metadata via agent interface:**
   - `ExtractPrompts()` - **READS** transcript file, parses JSONL
   - `ExtractSummary()` - **READS** transcript file, parses JSONL
   - `ExtractAllModifiedFiles()` or `ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset()` - **READS** transcript file
   - `CalculateTotalTokenUsage()` or `CalculateTokenUsage()` - **READS** transcript file + subagent transcripts

8. **Write prompt file:**
   - `os.WriteFile()` - **WRITES** `.entire/metadata/<session-id>/prompt.txt`

9. **Write summary file:**
   - `os.WriteFile()` - **WRITES** `.entire/metadata/<session-id>/summary.txt`

10. **Generate commit message:**
    - Uses last prompt from extracted prompts

11. **Get repo root:**
    - `paths.RepoRoot()` - Uses `.git/` location

12. **Detect file changes:**
    - `DetectFileChanges(previouslyUntracked)` - **READS**:
      - `.git/` directory (via go-git)
      - Working tree status
      - Filters out pre-existing untracked files

13. **Filter and normalize paths:**
    - `FilterAndNormalizePaths()` - Path transformations (no I/O)

14. **Filter to uncommitted files:**
    - `openRepository()` - Opens `.git/`
    - `repo.Head()` - **READS** HEAD reference
    - `repo.CommitObject()` - **READS** HEAD commit object
    - `commit.Tree()` - **READS** HEAD tree
    - `headTree.File()` - **READS** files from HEAD tree
    - `os.ReadFile()` - **READS** working tree files (for content comparison)

15. **Check if there are changes:**
    - If no changes: **READS/WRITES** `.git/entire-sessions/<sessionid>.json` for state transition

16. **Create context file:**
    - `os.WriteFile()` - **WRITES** `.entire/metadata/<session-id>/context.md`

17. **Get git author:**
    - `GetGitAuthor()` - **READS**:
      - `.git/config` (via go-git)
      - Falls back to `git config user.name` and `git config user.email` commands

18. **Get strategy and calculate token usage:**
    - Strategy-specific operations
    - May read subagent transcript files

19. **Save step checkpoint:**
    - `strat.SaveStep(ctx)` - Strategy-specific:
      - **CREATES/WRITES** git objects
      - **CREATES/WRITES** metadata on branches
      - **WRITES** `.git/entire-sessions/<sessionid>.json`

20. **Update transcript position (auto-commit only):**
    - **READS/WRITES** `.git/entire-sessions/<sessionid>.json`

21. **Transition session phase:**
    - `TransitionAndLog()` + `SaveSessionState()` - **READS/WRITES** `.git/entire-sessions/<sessionid>.json`

22. **Cleanup pre-prompt state:**
    - `os.Remove()` - **DELETES** `.entire/tmp/pre-prompt-*.json`

23. **Log output:**
    - Multiple `fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, ...)` calls
    - Writes to `.entire/logs/entire.log`

**Total I/O for STOP Hook: ~30 distinct operations**

## Summary: I/O Operations by Hook

| Hook | Type | Reads | Writes | Key Files |
|------|------|-------|--------|-----------|
| **session-start** | Lifecycle | `.git/`, settings, session state | `.git/entire-sessions/`, settings (state) | ~3-4 ops |
| **user-prompt-submit** | Lifecycle | `.git/`, settings, transcript, session state | `.entire/tmp/`, `.git/entire-sessions/`, git objects | ~8-10 ops |
| **stop** | Lifecycle | transcript, `.git/`, settings, session state, HEAD tree, working tree | `.entire/metadata/`, `.entire/logs/`, `.git/entire-sessions/`, git objects | **~30+ ops** |
| **session-end** | Lifecycle | session state | `.git/entire-sessions/` | ~2 ops |
| **pre-task** | Lifecycle | `.git/`, working tree | `.entire/tmp/` | ~3 ops |
| **post-task** | Lifecycle | `.git/`, transcript, session state, `.git/config` | git objects, metadata | ~8 ops |
| **post-todo** | Claude-specific | `.entire/tmp/`, `.git/`, session state, settings | git objects, metadata, `.entire/logs/` | ~7 ops |

---

## Startup Latency Sources (Ordered by Impact)

### CRITICAL PATH (Before Claude Code sees "Ready"):

1. **Session-Start Hook** (~50-150ms):
   - Settings load (2 files)
   - Session state directory scan
   - Logging initialization + directory creation
   - Output banner message

2. **User-Prompt-Submit Hook** (~100-500ms):
   - Untracked file scan (full `git status`)
   - Transcript position detection (parses existing transcript)
   - Session initialization
   - Create `.entire/tmp/` directory

3. **All Subsequent Hooks** (on Turn End, ~1-10s):
   - **STOP hook is the bottleneck:**
     - Full transcript read + JSON parsing (100KB-10MB+)
     - Prompt extraction from all messages
     - Summary extraction from last message
     - Modified files detection (full git status + HEAD tree traversal)
     - Commit object creation

### NOT on Critical Path (Fire asynchronously):

- **Session-End** - Fires after user closes session
- **Pre-Task/Post-Task** - Fire during subagent execution
- **Post-Todo** - Incremental, only if in subagent and files changed

---

## File System Impact Summary

### Reads (No caching, happens every hook):
- `.entire/settings.json` (small, ~1KB)
- `.entire/settings.local.json` (if exists, small)
- `.git/` directory (go-git library handles)
- `.git/entire-sessions/` (all session state files)
- Working tree status (git status operation)
- `.entire/logs/entire.log` (append)

### Writes (Always):
- `.entire/logs/entire.log` (buffered, 8KB buffer)
- `.git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json` (on state transitions)

### Writes (On STOP hook):
- `.entire/metadata/<session-id>/` (full session capture)
- Git objects (commits, trees, blobs)
- `.entire/tmp/pre-*.json` files (intermediate, cleaned up)

### Temp Files (Created & Deleted):
- `.entire/tmp/pre-prompt-<sessionid>.json` (created on TurnStart, deleted on TurnEnd)
- `.entire/tmp/pre-task-<tooluseid>.json` (created on PreTask, deleted on PostTask)

---

## Network Operations

**None.** All hooks are purely local filesystem and git repository operations. No network calls are made during hook execution.

---

## Key Finding: Why Startup Might Feel Slow

1. **Session-Start** fires immediately but must scan `.git/entire-sessions/` for all sessions
2. **User-Prompt-Submit** must run `git status` (expensive on large repos)
3. **Logging Init** creates `.entire/logs/` directory and opens log file on first hook
4. **Settings load** happens on every hook (no caching between hooks)
5. **STOP hook** is where the real work happens (transcript parsing, file detection, git tree traversal)

**Optimization opportunities identified:**
- Cache settings within a single hook invocation
- Skip untracked file scan if `.entire/metadata/` hasn't changed
- Pre-compute transcript position instead of re-parsing on each hook
- Batch git operations (currently: status called multiple times)
```

> AGENT

Based on that analysis, here are all the dimensions that affect Claude Code startup latency when Entire hooks are involved:

### Hooks that fire on startup

Two hooks fire before the user can interact:
1. **`session-start`** — fires immediately
2. **`user-prompt-submit`** — fires on first prompt

Each one is a **separate subprocess spawn** of the `entire` binary, and each one independently:
- Opens the git repo (go-git `PlainOpen`)
- Reads `.entire/settings.json` + `.entire/settings.local.json`
- Scans `.git/entire-sessions/` (lists + reads all session state files)
- Initializes logging (creates/opens `.entire/logs/`)

### Dimensions to benchmark

| # | Dimension | What it affects | Why it could be slow |
|---|-----------|----------------|---------------------|
| 1 | **Session state files** | `ReadDir` + JSON unmarshal per file + ref lookup per file | O(N) file reads + O(N) ref lookups |
| 2 | **Git refs (branches/tags)** | `repo.Reference()` scanning in concurrent session check | O(refs) per session state lookup |
| 3 | **Repo file count** | `git status` in `user-prompt-submit` (worktree scan) | O(files) tree walk |
| 4 | **Commit history depth** | go-git `PlainOpen`, `repo.Head()`, tree traversal | Pack file parsing |
| 5 | **`.git/` pack size** | go-git opening the repo, any object lookup | Large packfiles slow all git ops |
| 6 | **Shadow branches with checkpoints** | Concurrent session checker reads shadow branch refs | More refs to scan + checkpoint data |
| 7 | **Settings file size/complexity** | Loaded on every hook invocation | Unlikely bottleneck but worth ruling out |
| 8 | **Transcript file size** | `user-prompt-submit` reads transcript for position | Large transcripts from long sessions |
| 9 | **Working tree dirty files** | `git status` in `user-prompt-submit` | Many uncommitted changes slow status |

Dimensions 1-2 we already benchmarked and they were flat. That suggests the **subprocess spawn + Go binary init + cobra + go-git PlainOpen** dominates at ~80ms and the actual hook logic is negligible in our synthetic repos.

The most likely culprits for a 10s startup are **5 (`.git/` pack size)** and **3+9 (worktree scan with many files/dirty state)** since those affect the fixed costs we can't avoid.

Want me to update the benchmark to cover these additional dimensions?

```
     1→// hook_registry.go provides hook command registration for agents.
     2→// The lifecycle dispatcher (DispatchLifecycleEvent) handles all lifecycle events.
     3→// PostTodo is the only hook that's handled directly (not via lifecycle dispatcher).
     4→package cli
     5→
     6→import (
     7→	"context"
     8→	"errors"
     9→	"fmt"
    10→	"log/slog"
    11→	"time"
    12→
    13→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent"
    14→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode"
    15→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/geminicli"
    16→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/logging"
    17→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths"
    18→
    19→	"github.com/spf13/cobra"
    20→)
    21→
    22→// agentHookLogCleanup stores the cleanup function for agent hook logging.
    23→// Set by PersistentPreRunE, called by PersistentPostRunE.
    24→var agentHookLogCleanup func()
    25→
    26→// currentHookAgentName stores the agent name for the currently executing hook.
    27→// Set by newAgentHookVerbCmdWithLogging before calling the handler.
    28→// This allows handlers to know which agent invoked the hook without guessing.
    29→var currentHookAgentName agent.AgentName
    30→
    31→// GetCurrentHookAgent returns the agent for the currently executing hook.
    32→// Returns the agent based on the hook command structure (e.g., "entire hooks claude-code ...")
    33→// rather than guessing from directory presence.
    34→// Falls back to GetAgent() if not in a hook context.
    35→func GetCurrentHookAgent() (agent.Agent, error) {
    36→	if currentHookAgentName == "" {
    37→		return nil, errors.New("not in a hook context: agent name not set")
    38→	}
    39→
    40→	ag, err := agent.Get(currentHookAgentName)
    41→	if err != nil {
    42→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("getting hook agent %q: %w", currentHookAgentName, err)
    43→	}
    44→	return ag, nil
    45→}
    46→
    47→// newAgentHooksCmd creates a hooks subcommand for an agent.
    48→// It dynamically creates subcommands for each hook the agent supports.
    49→func newAgentHooksCmd(agentName agent.AgentName, handler agent.Agent) *cobra.Command {
    50→	cmd := &cobra.Command{
    51→		Use:    string(agentName),
    52→		Short:  handler.Description() + " hook handlers",
    53→		Hidden: true,
    54→		PersistentPreRunE: func(_ *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
    55→			agentHookLogCleanup = initHookLogging()
    56→			return nil
    57→		},
    58→		PersistentPostRunE: func(_ *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
    59→			if agentHookLogCleanup != nil {
    60→				agentHookLogCleanup()
    61→			}
    62→			return nil
    63→		},
    64→	}
    65→
    66→	for _, hookName := range handler.HookNames() {
    67→		cmd.AddCommand(newAgentHookVerbCmdWithLogging(agentName, hookName))
    68→	}
    69→
    70→	return cmd
    71→}
    72→
    73→// getHookType returns the hook type based on the hook name.
    74→// Returns "subagent" for task-related hooks (pre-task, post-task, post-todo),
    75→// "tool" for tool-related hooks (before-tool, after-tool),
    76→// "agent" for all other agent hooks.
    77→func getHookType(hookName string) string {
    78→	switch hookName {
    79→	case claudecode.HookNamePreTask, claudecode.HookNamePostTask, claudecode.HookNamePostTodo:
    80→		return "subagent"
    81→	case geminicli.HookNameBeforeTool, geminicli.HookNameAfterTool:
    82→		return "tool"
    83→	default:
    84→		return "agent"
    85→	}
    86→}
    87→
    88→// newAgentHookVerbCmdWithLogging creates a command for a specific hook verb with structured logging.
    89→// It uses the lifecycle dispatcher (ParseHookEvent → DispatchLifecycleEvent) as the primary path.
    90→// PostTodo is handled directly as it's Claude-specific and not part of the lifecycle dispatcher.
    91→func newAgentHookVerbCmdWithLogging(agentName agent.AgentName, hookName string) *cobra.Command {
    92→	return &cobra.Command{
    93→		Use:    hookName,
    94→		Hidden: true,
    95→		Short:  "Called on " + hookName,
    96→		RunE: func(cmd *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
    97→			// Skip silently if not in a git repository - hooks shouldn't prevent the agent from working
    98→			if _, err := paths.RepoRoot(); err != nil {
    99→				return nil
   100→			}
   101→
   102→			// Skip if Entire is not enabled
   103→			enabled, err := IsEnabled()
   104→			if err == nil && !enabled {
   105→				return nil
   106→			}
   107→
   108→			start := time.Now()
   109→
   110→			// Initialize logging context with agent name
   111→			ctx := logging.WithAgent(logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "hooks"), agentName)
   112→
   113→			// Get strategy name for logging
   114→			strategyName := GetStrategy().Name()
   115→
   116→			hookType := getHookType(hookName)
   117→
   118→			logging.Debug(ctx, "hook invoked",
   119→				slog.String("hook", hookName),
   120→				slog.String("hook_type", hookType),
   121→				slog.String("strategy", strategyName),
   122→			)
   123→
   124→			// Set the current hook agent so handlers can retrieve it
   125→			currentHookAgentName = agentName
   126→			defer func() { currentHookAgentName = "" }()
   127→
   128→			// Use the lifecycle dispatcher for all hooks
   129→			var hookErr error
   130→			ag, agentErr := agent.Get(agentName)
   131→			if agentErr != nil {
   132→				return fmt.Errorf("failed to get agent %q: %w", agentName, agentErr)
   133→			}
   134→
   135→			// Use cmd.InOrStdin() to support testing with cmd.SetIn()
   136→			event, parseErr := ag.ParseHookEvent(hookName, cmd.InOrStdin())
   137→			if parseErr != nil {
   138→				return fmt.Errorf("failed to parse hook event: %w", parseErr)
   139→			}
   140→
   141→			if event != nil {
   142→				// Lifecycle event — use the generic dispatcher
   143→				hookErr = DispatchLifecycleEvent(ag, event)
   144→			} else if agentName == agent.AgentNameClaudeCode && hookName == claudecode.HookNamePostTodo {
   145→				// PostTodo is Claude-specific: creates incremental checkpoints during subagent execution
   146→				hookErr = handleClaudeCodePostTodo()
   147→			}
   148→			// Other pass-through hooks (nil event, no special handling) are no-ops
   149→
   150→			logging.LogDuration(ctx, slog.LevelDebug, "hook completed", start,
   151→				slog.String("hook", hookName),
   152→				slog.String("hook_type", hookType),
   153→				slog.String("strategy", strategyName),
   154→				slog.Bool("success", hookErr == nil),
   155→			)
   156→
   157→			return hookErr
   158→		},
   159→	}
   160→}
   161→
```

```
     1→// lifecycle.go implements the generic lifecycle event dispatcher.
     2→// It routes normalized events from any agent to the appropriate framework actions.
     3→//
     4→// The dispatcher inverts the current flow from "agent handler calls framework functions"
     5→// to "framework dispatcher calls agent methods." Agents are passive data providers;
     6→// the dispatcher handles all orchestration: state transitions, strategy calls,
     7→// file change detection, metadata generation.
     8→package cli
     9→
    10→import (
    11→	"context"
    12→	"errors"
    13→	"fmt"
    14→	"log/slog"
    15→	"os"
    16→	"path/filepath"
    17→	"strings"
    18→	"time"
    19→
    20→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent"
    21→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/logging"
    22→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths"
    23→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/session"
    24→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy"
    25→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/transcript"
    26→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/validation"
    27→)
    28→
    29→// DispatchLifecycleEvent routes a normalized lifecycle event to the appropriate handler.
    30→// Returns nil if the event was handled successfully.
    31→func DispatchLifecycleEvent(ag agent.Agent, event *agent.Event) error {
    32→	if ag == nil {
    33→		return errors.New("agent cannot be nil")
    34→	}
    35→	if event == nil {
    36→		return errors.New("event cannot be nil")
    37→	}
    38→
    39→	switch event.Type {
    40→	case agent.SessionStart:
    41→		return handleLifecycleSessionStart(ag, event)
    42→	case agent.TurnStart:
    43→		return handleLifecycleTurnStart(ag, event)
    44→	case agent.TurnEnd:
    45→		return handleLifecycleTurnEnd(ag, event)
    46→	case agent.Compaction:
    47→		return handleLifecycleCompaction(ag, event)
    48→	case agent.SessionEnd:
    49→		return handleLifecycleSessionEnd(ag, event)
    50→	case agent.SubagentStart:
    51→		return handleLifecycleSubagentStart(ag, event)
    52→	case agent.SubagentEnd:
    53→		return handleLifecycleSubagentEnd(ag, event)
    54→	default:
    55→		return fmt.Errorf("unknown lifecycle event type: %d", event.Type)
    56→	}
    57→}
    58→
    59→// handleLifecycleSessionStart handles session start: shows banner, checks concurrent sessions,
    60→// fires state machine transition.
    61→func handleLifecycleSessionStart(ag agent.Agent, event *agent.Event) error {
    62→	logCtx := logging.WithAgent(logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "lifecycle"), ag.Name())
    63→	logging.Info(logCtx, "session-start",
    64→		slog.String("event", event.Type.String()),
    65→		slog.String("session_id", event.SessionID),
    66→		slog.String("session_ref", event.SessionRef),
    67→	)
    68→
    69→	if event.SessionID == "" {
    70→		return fmt.Errorf("no session_id in %s event", event.Type)
    71→	}
    72→	if err := validation.ValidateSessionID(event.SessionID); err != nil {
    73→		return fmt.Errorf("invalid %s event: %w", event.Type, err)
    74→	}
    75→
    76→	// Build informational message
    77→	message := "\n\nPowered by Entire:\n  This conversation will be linked to your next commit."
    78→
    79→	// Check for concurrent sessions and append count if any
    80→	strat := GetStrategy()
    81→	if concurrentChecker, ok := strat.(strategy.ConcurrentSessionChecker); ok {
    82→		if count, err := concurrentChecker.CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints(event.SessionID); err == nil && count > 0 {
    83→			message += fmt.Sprintf("\n  %d other active conversation(s) in this workspace will also be included.\n  Use 'entire status' for more information.", count)
    84→		}
    85→	}
    86→
    87→	// Output informational message
    88→	if event.ResponseMessage != "" {
    89→		message = event.ResponseMessage
    90→	}
    91→	if err := outputHookResponse(message); err != nil {
    92→		return err
    93→	}
    94→
    95→	// Fire EventSessionStart for the current session (if state exists).
    96→	if state, loadErr := strategy.LoadSessionState(event.SessionID); loadErr != nil {
    97→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: failed to load session state on start: %v\n", loadErr)
    98→	} else if state != nil {
    99→		if transErr := strategy.TransitionAndLog(state, session.EventSessionStart, session.TransitionContext{}, session.NoOpActionHandler{}); transErr != nil {
   100→			fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: session start transition failed: %v\n", transErr)
   101→		}
   102→		if saveErr := strategy.SaveSessionState(state); saveErr != nil {
   103→			fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: failed to update session state on start: %v\n", saveErr)
   104→		}
   105→	}
   106→
   107→	return nil
   108→}
   109→
   110→// handleLifecycleTurnStart handles turn start: captures pre-prompt state,
```

```
     1→package cli
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"log/slog"
     6→	"time"
     7→
     8→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/logging"

    13→
    14→const unknownStrategyName = "unknown"
    15→
    16→// gitHookContext holds common state for git hook logging.
    17→type gitHookContext struct {
    18→	hookName     string
    19→	ctx          context.Context
    20→	start        time.Time
    21→	strategy     strategy.Strategy
    22→	strategyName string
    23→}
    24→
    25→// newGitHookContext creates a new git hook context with logging initialized.
    26→func newGitHookContext(hookName string) *gitHookContext {
    27→	g := &gitHookContext{
    28→		hookName:     hookName,
    29→		start:        time.Now(),
    30→		ctx:          logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "hooks"),
    31→		strategyName: unknownStrategyName,
    32→	}
    33→	g.strategy = GetStrategy()
    34→	g.strategyName = g.strategy.Name()
    35→	return g
    36→}
    37→
    38→// logInvoked logs that the hook was invoked.
    39→func (g *gitHookContext) logInvoked(extraAttrs ...any) {
    40→	attrs := []any{
    41→		slog.String("hook", g.hookName),
    42→		slog.String("hook_type", "git"),
    43→		slog.String("strategy", g.strategyName),
    44→	}
    45→	logging.Debug(g.ctx, g.hookName+" hook invoked", append(attrs, extraAttrs...)...)
    46→}
    47→
    48→// logCompleted logs hook completion with duration at DEBUG level.
    49→// The actual work logging (checkpoint operations) happens at INFO level in the handlers.
    50→func (g *gitHookContext) logCompleted(err error, extraAttrs ...any) {
    51→	attrs := []any{
    52→		slog.String("hook", g.hookName),
    53→		slog.String("hook_type", "git"),
    54→		slog.String("strategy", g.strategyName),
    55→		slog.Bool("success", err == nil),
    56→	}
    57→	logging.LogDuration(g.ctx, slog.LevelDebug, g.hookName+" hook completed", g.start, append(attrs, extraAttrs...)...)
    58→}
    59→
    60→// initHookLogging initializes logging for hooks by finding the most recent session.
    61→// Returns a cleanup function that should be deferred.
    62→func initHookLogging() func() {
    63→	// Set up log level getter so logging can read from settings
    64→	logging.SetLogLevelGetter(GetLogLevel)
    65→
    66→	// Read session ID for the slog attribute (empty string is fine - log file is fixed)
    67→	sessionID := strategy.FindMostRecentSession()
    68→	if err := logging.Init(sessionID); err != nil {
    69→		// Init failed - logging will use stderr fallback
    70→		return func() {}
    71→	}
    72→	return logging.Close
    73→}
    74→
    75→// hookLogCleanup stores the cleanup function for hook logging.
    76→// Set by PersistentPreRunE, called by PersistentPostRunE.
    77→var hookLogCleanup func()
    78→
    79→func newHooksGitCmd() *cobra.Command {
    80→	cmd := &cobra.Command{
    81→		Use:    "git",
    82→		Short:  "Git hook handlers",
    83→		Long:   "Commands called by git hooks. These delegate to the current strategy.",
    84→		Hidden: true, // Internal command, not for direct user use
    85→		PersistentPreRunE: func(_ *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
    86→			hookLogCleanup = initHookLogging()
    87→			return nil
    88→		},
    89→		PersistentPostRunE: func(_ *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
    90→			if hookLogCleanup != nil {
    91→				hookLogCleanup()
    92→			}
    93→			return nil
    94→		},
    95→	}
    96→
    97→	cmd.AddCommand(newHooksGitPrepareCommitMsgCmd())
    98→	cmd.AddCommand(newHooksGitCommitMsgCmd())
    99→	cmd.AddCommand(newHooksGitPostCommitCmd())
   100→	cmd.AddCommand(newHooksGitPrePushCmd())
   101→
   102→	return cmd
   103→}
   104→
   105→func newHooksGitPrepareCommitMsgCmd() *cobra.Command {
   106→	return &cobra.Command{
   107→		Use:   "prepare-commit-msg <commit-msg-file> [source]",
   108→		Short: "Handle prepare-commit-msg git hook",
   109→		Args:  cobra.RangeArgs(1, 2),
   110→		RunE: func(_ *cobra.Command, args []string) error {
   111→			commitMsgFile := args[0]
   112→			var source string
   113→			if len(args) > 1 {
   114→				source = args[1]
   115→			}
   116→
   117→			g := newGitHookContext("prepare-commit-msg")
   118→			g.logInvoked(slog.String("source", source))
   119→
   120→			if handler, ok := g.strategy.(strategy.PrepareCommitMsgHandler); ok {
   121→				hookErr := handler.PrepareCommitMsg(commitMsgFile, source)
   122→				g.logCompleted(hookErr, slog.String("source", source))
   123→			}
   124→
   125→			return nil
   126→		},
   127→	}
   128→}
   129→
   130→func newHooksGitCommitMsgCmd() *cobra.Command {
   131→	return &cobra.Command{
   132→		Use:   "commit-msg <commit-msg-file>",
   133→		Short: "Handle commit-msg git hook",
   134→		Args:  cobra.ExactArgs(1),
   135→		RunE: func(_ *cobra.Command, args []string) error {
   136→			commitMsgFile := args[0]
   137→
   138→			g := newGitHookContext("commit-msg")
   139→			g.logInvoked()
   140→
   141→			if handler, ok := g.strategy.(strategy.CommitMsgHandler); ok {
   142→				hookErr := handler.CommitMsg(commitMsgFile)
   143→				g.logCompleted(hookErr)
   144→				return hookErr //nolint:wrapcheck // Thin delegation layer - wrapping adds no value
   145→			}
   146→
   147→			return nil
   148→		},
   149→	}
   150→}

   152→func newHooksGitPostCommitCmd() *cobra.Command {
   153→	return &cobra.Command{
   154→		Use:   "post-commit",
   155→		Short: "Handle post-commit git hook",
   156→		Args:  cobra.NoArgs,
   157→		RunE: func(_ *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
   158→			g := newGitHookContext("post-commit")
   159→			g.logInvoked()
   160→
   161→			if handler, ok := g.strategy.(strategy.PostCommitHandler); ok {
   162→				hookErr := handler.PostCommit()
   163→				g.logCompleted(hookErr)
   164→			}
   165→
   166→			return nil
   167→		},
   168→	}
   169→}
   170→
   171→func newHooksGitPrePushCmd() *cobra.Command {
   172→	return &cobra.Command{
   173→		Use:   "pre-push <remote>",
   174→		Short: "Handle pre-push git hook",
   175→		Args:  cobra.ExactArgs(1),
   176→		RunE: func(_ *cobra.Command, args []string) error {
   177→			remote := args[0]
   178→
   179→			g := newGitHookContext("pre-push")
   180→			g.logInvoked(slog.String("remote", remote))
   181→
   182→			if handler, ok := g.strategy.(strategy.PrePushHandler); ok {
   183→				hookErr := handler.PrePush(remote)
   184→				g.logCompleted(hookErr, slog.String("remote", remote))
   185→			}
   186→
   187→			return nil
   188→		},
   189→	}
   190→}
```

```
   101→// GetAgentsWithHooksInstalled returns names of agents that have hooks installed.
   102→func GetAgentsWithHooksInstalled() []agent.AgentName {
   103→	var installed []agent.AgentName
   104→	for _, name := range agent.List() {
   105→		ag, err := agent.Get(name)
   106→		if err != nil {
   107→			continue
   108→		}
   109→		if hs, ok := ag.(agent.HookSupport); ok && hs.AreHooksInstalled() {
   110→			installed = append(installed, name)
   111→		}
   112→	}
   113→	return installed
   114→}
   115→
   116→// JoinAgentNames joins agent names into a comma-separated string.
   117→func JoinAgentNames(names []agent.AgentName) string {
   118→	strs := make([]string, len(names))
   119→	for i, n := range names {
   120→		strs[i] = string(n)
   121→	}
   122→	return strings.Join(strs, ",")
   123→}
```

```
     1→package strategy
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"encoding/json"
     6→	"fmt"
     7→	"log/slog"
     8→	"os"
     9→	"path/filepath"
    10→
    11→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/jsonutil"
    12→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/logging"
    13→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/session"
    14→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/validation"
    15→)
    16→
    17→// Session state management functions shared across all strategies.
    18→// SessionState is stored in .git/entire-sessions/{session_id}.json
    19→
    20→// getSessionStateDir returns the path to the session state directory.
    21→// This is stored in the git common dir so it's shared across all worktrees.
    22→func getSessionStateDir() (string, error) {
    23→	commonDir, err := GetGitCommonDir()
    24→	if err != nil {
    25→		return "", err
    26→	}
    27→	return filepath.Join(commonDir, session.SessionStateDirName), nil
    28→}
    29→
    30→// sessionStateFile returns the path to a session state file.
    31→func sessionStateFile(sessionID string) (string, error) {
    32→	stateDir, err := getSessionStateDir()
    33→	if err != nil {
    34→		return "", err
    35→	}
    36→	return filepath.Join(stateDir, sessionID+".json"), nil
    37→}
    38→
    39→// LoadSessionState loads the session state for the given session ID.
    40→// Returns (nil, nil) when session file doesn't exist (not an error condition).
    41→func LoadSessionState(sessionID string) (*SessionState, error) {
    42→	// Validate session ID to prevent path traversal
    43→	if err := validation.ValidateSessionID(sessionID); err != nil {
    44→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("invalid session ID: %w", err)
    45→	}
    46→
    47→	stateFile, err := sessionStateFile(sessionID)
    48→	if err != nil {
    49→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get session state file path: %w", err)
    50→	}
    51→
    52→	data, err := os.ReadFile(stateFile) //nolint:gosec // stateFile is derived from sessionID, not user input
    53→	if os.IsNotExist(err) {
    54→		return nil, nil //nolint:nilnil // nil,nil indicates session not found (expected case)
    55→	}
    56→	if err != nil {
    57→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read session state: %w", err)
    58→	}
    59→
    60→	var state SessionState
    61→	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &state); err != nil {
    62→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to unmarshal session state: %w", err)
    63→	}
    64→	state.NormalizeAfterLoad()
    65→	return &state, nil
    66→}
    67→
    68→// SaveSessionState saves the session state atomically.
    69→func SaveSessionState(state *SessionState) error {
    70→	// Validate session ID to prevent path traversal
    71→	if err := validation.ValidateSessionID(state.SessionID); err != nil {
    72→		return fmt.Errorf("invalid session ID: %w", err)
    73→	}
    74→
    75→	stateDir, err := getSessionStateDir()
    76→	if err != nil {
    77→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to get session state directory: %w", err)
    78→	}
    79→
    80→	if err := os.MkdirAll(stateDir, 0o750); err != nil {
    81→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create session state directory: %w", err)
    82→	}
    83→
    84→	data, err := jsonutil.MarshalIndentWithNewline(state, "", "  ")
    85→	if err != nil {
    86→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal session state: %w", err)
    87→	}
    88→
    89→	stateFile, err := sessionStateFile(state.SessionID)
    90→	if err != nil {
    91→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to get session state file path: %w", err)
    92→	}
    93→
    94→	// Atomic write: write to temp file, then rename
    95→	tmpFile := stateFile + ".tmp"
    96→	if err := os.WriteFile(tmpFile, data, 0o600); err != nil {
    97→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to write session state: %w", err)
    98→	}
    99→	if err := os.Rename(tmpFile, stateFile); err != nil {
   100→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to rename session state file: %w", err)
   101→	}
   102→	return nil
   103→}
   104→
   105→// ListSessionStates returns all session states from the state directory.
   106→// This is a package-level function that doesn't require a specific strategy instance.
   107→func ListSessionStates() ([]*SessionState, error) {
   108→	store, err := session.NewStateStore()
   109→	if err != nil {
   110→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to create state store: %w", err)
   111→	}
   112→
   113→	states, err := store.List(context.Background())
   114→	if err != nil {
   115→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to list session states: %w", err)
   116→	}
   117→	return states, nil
   118→}
   119→
   120→// FindMostRecentSession returns the session ID of the most recently interacted session
   121→// (by LastInteractionTime) in the current worktree. Returns empty string if no sessions exist.
   122→// Scoping to the current worktree prevents cross-worktree pollution in log routing.
   123→// Falls back to unfiltered search if the worktree path can't be determined.
   124→func FindMostRecentSession() string {
   125→	states, err := ListSessionStates()
   126→	if err != nil || len(states) == 0 {
   127→		return ""
   128→	}
   129→
   130→	// Scope to current worktree to prevent cross-worktree pollution.
   131→	worktreePath, wpErr := GetWorktreePath()
   132→	if wpErr == nil && worktreePath != "" {
   133→		var filtered []*SessionState
   134→		for _, s := range states {
   135→			if s.WorktreePath == worktreePath {
   136→				filtered = append(filtered, s)
   137→			}
   138→		}
   139→		if len(filtered) > 0 {
   140→			states = filtered
   141→		}
   142→		// If no sessions match the worktree, fall back to all sessions
   143→	}
   144→
   145→	var best *SessionState
   146→	for _, s := range states {
   147→		if s.LastInteractionTime == nil {
   148→			continue
   149→		}
   150→		if best == nil || s.LastInteractionTime.After(*best.LastInteractionTime) {
   151→			best = s
   152→		}
   153→	}
   154→	if best != nil {
   155→		return best.SessionID
   156→	}
   157→
   158→	// Fallback: return most recently started session
   159→	for _, s := range states {
   160→		if best == nil || s.StartedAt.After(best.StartedAt) {
   161→			best = s
   162→		}
   163→	}
   164→	if best != nil {
   165→		return best.SessionID
   166→	}
   167→	return ""
   168→}
   169→
   170→// TransitionAndLog runs a session phase transition, applies actions via the
   171→// handler, and logs the transition. Returns the first handler error from
   172→// ApplyTransition (if any) so callers can surface it. The error is also
   173→// logged internally for diagnostics.
   174→// This is the single entry point for all state machine transitions to ensure
   175→// consistent logging of phase changes.
   176→func TransitionAndLog(state *SessionState, event session.Event, ctx session.TransitionContext, handler session.ActionHandler) error {
   177→	oldPhase := state.Phase
   178→	result := session.Transition(oldPhase, event, ctx)
   179→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "session")
   180→
   181→	handlerErr := session.ApplyTransition(state, result, handler)
   182→	if handlerErr != nil {
   183→		logging.Error(logCtx, "action handler error during transition",
   184→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   185→			slog.String("event", event.String()),
   186→			slog.Any("error", handlerErr),
   187→		)
   188→	}
   189→
   190→	if result.NewPhase != oldPhase {
   191→		logging.Info(logCtx, "phase transition",
   192→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   193→			slog.String("event", event.String()),
   194→			slog.String("from", string(oldPhase)),
   195→			slog.String("to", string(result.NewPhase)),
   196→		)
   197→	} else {
   198→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "phase unchanged",
   199→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   200→			slog.String("event", event.String()),
   201→			slog.String("phase", string(result.NewPhase)),
   202→			slog.Any("result", result),
   203→		)
   204→	}
   205→
   206→	if handlerErr != nil {
   207→		return fmt.Errorf("transition %s: %w", event, handlerErr)
   208→	}
   209→	return nil
   210→}
   211→
   212→// ClearSessionState removes the session state file for the given session ID.
   213→func ClearSessionState(sessionID string) error {
   214→	// Validate session ID to prevent path traversal
   215→	if err := validation.ValidateSessionID(sessionID); err != nil {
   216→		return fmt.Errorf("invalid session ID: %w", err)
   217→	}
   218→
   219→	stateFile, err := sessionStateFile(sessionID)
   220→	if err != nil {
   221→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to get session state file path: %w", err)
   222→	}
   223→
   224→	if err := os.Remove(stateFile); err != nil {
   225→		if os.IsNotExist(err) {
   226→			return nil // Already gone, not an error
   227→		}
   228→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to remove session state file: %w", err)
   229→	}
   230→	return nil
   231→}
   232→
```
