     1→package strategy
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"fmt"
     6→	"time"
     7→
     8→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent"
     9→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/buildinfo"
    10→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint"
    11→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/id"
    12→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths"
    13→
    14→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5"
    15→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5/plumbing"
    16→)
    17→
    18→// Shadow strategy session state methods.
    19→// Uses session.StateStore for persistence.
    20→
    21→// loadSessionState loads session state using the StateStore.
    22→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) loadSessionState(sessionID string) (*SessionState, error) {
    23→	store, err := s.getStateStore()
    24→	if err != nil {
    25→		return nil, err
    26→	}
    27→	state, err := store.Load(context.Background(), sessionID)
    28→	if err != nil {
    29→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to load session state: %w", err)
    30→	}
    31→	return state, nil
    32→}
    33→
    34→// saveSessionState saves session state using the StateStore.
    35→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) saveSessionState(state *SessionState) error {
    36→	store, err := s.getStateStore()
    37→	if err != nil {
    38→		return err
    39→	}
    40→	if err := store.Save(context.Background(), state); err != nil {
    41→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to save session state: %w", err)
    42→	}
    43→	return nil
    44→}
    45→
    46→// clearSessionState clears session state using the StateStore.
    47→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) clearSessionState(sessionID string) error {
    48→	store, err := s.getStateStore()
    49→	if err != nil {
    50→		return err
    51→	}
    52→	if err := store.Clear(context.Background(), sessionID); err != nil {
    53→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to clear session state: %w", err)
    54→	}
    55→	return nil
    56→}
    57→
    58→// listAllSessionStates returns all active session states.
    59→// It filters out orphaned sessions whose shadow branch no longer exists.
    60→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) listAllSessionStates() ([]*SessionState, error) {
    61→	store, err := s.getStateStore()
    62→	if err != nil {
    63→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get state store: %w", err)
    64→	}
    65→
    66→	sessionStates, err := store.List(context.Background())
    67→	if err != nil {
    68→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to list session states: %w", err)
    69→	}
    70→
    71→	if len(sessionStates) == 0 {
    72→		return nil, nil
    73→	}
    74→
    75→	repo, err := OpenRepository()
    76→	if err != nil {
    77→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to open git repository: %w", err)
    78→	}
    79→
    80→	var states []*SessionState
    81→	for _, sessionState := range sessionStates {
    82→		state := sessionState
    83→
    84→		// Skip and cleanup orphaned sessions whose shadow branch no longer exists.
    85→		// Keep active sessions (shadow branch may not be created yet) and sessions
    86→		// with LastCheckpointID (needed for checkpoint ID reuse on subsequent commits).
    87→		// Clean up everything else: stale pre-state-machine sessions (empty phase),
    88→		// IDLE/ENDED sessions that were never condensed, etc.
    89→		shadowBranch := getShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit, state.WorktreeID)
    90→		refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranch)
    91→		if _, err := repo.Reference(refName, true); err != nil {
    92→			if !state.Phase.IsActive() && state.LastCheckpointID.IsEmpty() {
    93→				//nolint:errcheck,gosec // G104: Cleanup is best-effort, shouldn't fail the list operation
    94→				store.Clear(context.Background(), state.SessionID)
    95→				continue
    96→			}
    97→		}
    98→
    99→		states = append(states, state)
   100→	}
   101→	return states, nil
   102→}
   103→
   104→// findSessionsForWorktree finds all sessions for the given worktree path.
   105→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) findSessionsForWorktree(worktreePath string) ([]*SessionState, error) {
   106→	allStates, err := s.listAllSessionStates()
   107→	if err != nil {
   108→		return nil, err
   109→	}
   110→
   111→	var matching []*SessionState
   112→	for _, state := range allStates {
   113→		if state.WorktreePath == worktreePath {
   114→			matching = append(matching, state)
   115→		}
   116→	}
   117→	return matching, nil
   118→}
   119→
   120→// findSessionsForCommit finds all sessions where base_commit matches the given SHA.
   121→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) findSessionsForCommit(baseCommitSHA string) ([]*SessionState, error) {
   122→	allStates, err := s.listAllSessionStates()
   123→	if err != nil {
   124→		return nil, err
   125→	}
   126→
   127→	var matching []*SessionState
   128→	for _, state := range allStates {
   129→		if state.BaseCommit == baseCommitSHA {
   130→			matching = append(matching, state)
   131→		}
   132→	}
   133→	return matching, nil
   134→}
   135→
   136→// FindSessionsForCommit is the exported version of findSessionsForCommit.
   137→// Used by the rewind reset command to find sessions to clean up.
   138→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) FindSessionsForCommit(baseCommitSHA string) ([]*SessionState, error) {
   139→	return s.findSessionsForCommit(baseCommitSHA)
   140→}
   141→
   142→// ClearSessionState is the exported version of clearSessionState.
   143→// Used by the rewind reset command to clean up session state files.
   144→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) ClearSessionState(sessionID string) error {
   145→	return s.clearSessionState(sessionID)
   146→}
   147→
   148→// CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints counts how many other active sessions
   149→// from the SAME worktree (different from currentSessionID) have created checkpoints
   150→// on the SAME base commit (current HEAD). This is used to show an informational message
   151→// about concurrent sessions that will be included in the next commit.
   152→// Returns 0, nil if no such sessions exist.
   153→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints(currentSessionID string) (int, error) {
   154→	currentWorktree, err := GetWorktreePath()
   155→	if err != nil {
   156→		return 0, err
   157→	}
   158→
   159→	// Get current HEAD to compare with session base commits
   160→	repo, err := OpenRepository()
   161→	if err != nil {
   162→		return 0, err
   163→	}
   164→	head, err := repo.Head()
   165→	if err != nil {
   166→		return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to get HEAD: %w", err)
   167→	}
   168→	currentHead := head.Hash().String()
   169→
   170→	allStates, err := s.listAllSessionStates()
   171→	if err != nil {
   172→		return 0, err
   173→	}
   174→
   175→	count := 0
   176→	for _, state := range allStates {
   177→		// Only consider sessions from the same worktree with checkpoints
   178→		// AND based on the same commit (current HEAD)
   179→		// Sessions from different base commits are independent and shouldn't be counted
   180→		if state.SessionID != currentSessionID &&
   181→			state.WorktreePath == currentWorktree &&
   182→			state.StepCount > 0 &&
   183→			state.BaseCommit == currentHead {
   184→			count++
   185→		}
   186→	}
   187→	return count, nil
   188→}
   189→
   190→// initializeSession creates a new session state or updates a partial one.
   191→// A partial state may exist if the concurrent session warning was shown.
   192→// agentType is the human-readable name of the agent (e.g., "Claude Code").
   193→// transcriptPath is the path to the live transcript file (for mid-session commit detection).
   194→// userPrompt is the user's prompt text (stored truncated as FirstPrompt for display).
   195→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) initializeSession(repo *git.Repository, sessionID string, agentType agent.AgentType, transcriptPath string, userPrompt string) (*SessionState, error) {
   196→	head, err := repo.Head()
   197→	if err != nil {
   198→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get HEAD: %w", err)
   199→	}
   200→
   201→	worktreePath, err := GetWorktreePath()
   202→	if err != nil {
   203→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get worktree path: %w", err)
   204→	}
   205→
   206→	// Get worktree ID for shadow branch naming
   207→	worktreeID, err := paths.GetWorktreeID(worktreePath)
   208→	if err != nil {
   209→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get worktree ID: %w", err)
   210→	}
   211→
   212→	// Capture untracked files at session start to preserve them during rewind
   213→	untrackedFiles, err := collectUntrackedFiles()
   214→	if err != nil {
   215→		// Non-fatal: continue even if we can't collect untracked files
   216→		untrackedFiles = nil
   217→	}
   218→
   219→	// Generate TurnID for the first turn
   220→	turnID, err := id.Generate()
   221→	if err != nil {
   222→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to generate turn ID: %w", err)
   223→	}
   224→
   225→	now := time.Now()
   226→	headHash := head.Hash().String()
   227→	state := &SessionState{
   228→		SessionID:             sessionID,
   229→		CLIVersion:            buildinfo.Version,
   230→		BaseCommit:            headHash,
   231→		AttributionBaseCommit: headHash,
   232→		WorktreePath:          worktreePath,
   233→		WorktreeID:            worktreeID,
   234→		StartedAt:             now,
   235→		LastInteractionTime:   &now,
   236→		TurnID:                turnID.String(),
   237→		StepCount:             0,
   238→		UntrackedFilesAtStart: untrackedFiles,
   239→		AgentType:             agentType,
   240→		TranscriptPath:        transcriptPath,
   241→		FirstPrompt:           truncatePromptForStorage(userPrompt),
   242→	}
   243→
   244→	if err := s.saveSessionState(state); err != nil {
   245→		return nil, err
   246→	}
   247→
   248→	return state, nil
   249→}
   250→
   251→// getShadowBranchNameForCommit returns the shadow branch name for the given base commit and worktree ID.
   252→// worktreeID should be empty for the main worktree or the internal git worktree name for linked worktrees.
   253→func getShadowBranchNameForCommit(baseCommit, worktreeID string) string {
   254→	return checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(baseCommit, worktreeID)
   255→}
   256→

    26→// State represents the state of an active session.
    27→// This is stored in .git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json
    28→type State struct {
    29→	// SessionID is the unique session identifier
    30→	SessionID string `json:"session_id"`
    31→
    32→	// CLIVersion is the version of the CLI that created this session
    33→	CLIVersion string `json:"cli_version,omitempty"`
    34→
    35→	// BaseCommit tracks the current shadow branch base. Initially set to HEAD when the
    36→	// session starts, but updated on migration (pull/rebase) and after condensation.
    37→	// Used for shadow branch naming and checkpoint storage — NOT for attribution.
    38→	BaseCommit string `json:"base_commit"`
    39→
    40→	// AttributionBaseCommit is the commit used as the reference point for attribution calculations.
    41→	// Unlike BaseCommit (which tracks the shadow branch and moves with migration), this field
    42→	// preserves the original base commit so deferred condensation can correctly calculate
    43→	// agent vs human line attribution. Updated only after successful condensation.
    44→	AttributionBaseCommit string `json:"attribution_base_commit,omitempty"`
    45→
    46→	// WorktreePath is the absolute path to the worktree root
    47→	WorktreePath string `json:"worktree_path,omitempty"`
    48→
    49→	// WorktreeID is the internal git worktree identifier (empty for main worktree)
    50→	// Derived from .git/worktrees/<name>/, stable across git worktree move
    51→	WorktreeID string `json:"worktree_id,omitempty"`
    52→
    53→	// StartedAt is when the session was started
    54→	StartedAt time.Time `json:"started_at"`
    55→
    56→	// EndedAt is when the session was explicitly closed by the user.
    57→	// nil means the session is still active or was not cleanly closed.
    58→	EndedAt *time.Time `json:"ended_at,omitempty"`
    59→
    60→	// Phase is the lifecycle stage of this session (see phase.go).

    62→	Phase Phase `json:"phase,omitempty"`
    63→
    64→	// TurnID is a unique identifier for the current agent turn.

    67→	//   - Shared across all checkpoints within the same turn
    68→	//   - Used to correlate related checkpoints when a turn's work spans multiple commits
    69→	//   - Persists until the next InitializeSession call generates a new one
    70→	TurnID string `json:"turn_id,omitempty"`
    71→
    72→	// TurnCheckpointIDs tracks all checkpoint IDs condensed during the current turn.

    76→	//   - Cleared in HandleTurnEnd after finalization completes
    77→	//   - Cleared in InitializeSession when a new prompt starts

    85→	// StepCount is the number of checkpoints/steps created in this session.

    89→	// CheckpointTranscriptStart is the transcript line offset where the current

    99→

   121→	// TranscriptIdentifierAtStart is the last transcript identifier when the session started.

   126→	TranscriptPath string `json:"transcript_path,omitempty"`
   127→

   132→	// This enables accurate attribution by capturing user edits between checkpoints.

   134→
   135→	// PendingPromptAttribution holds attribution calculated at prompt start (before agent runs).

   139→
   140→// PromptAttribution captures line-level attribution data at the start of each prompt.
   141→// By recording what changed since the last checkpoint BEFORE the agent works,
   142→// we can accurately separate user edits from agent contributions.
   143→type PromptAttribution struct {
   144→	// CheckpointNumber is which checkpoint this was recorded before (1-indexed)
   145→	CheckpointNumber int `json:"checkpoint_number"`
   146→
   147→	// UserLinesAdded is lines added by user since the last checkpoint
   148→	UserLinesAdded int `json:"user_lines_added"`
   149→
   150→	// UserLinesRemoved is lines removed by user since the last checkpoint
   151→	UserLinesRemoved int `json:"user_lines_removed"`
   152→
   153→	// AgentLinesAdded is total agent lines added so far (base → last checkpoint).
   154→	// Always 0 for checkpoint 1 since there's no previous checkpoint to measure against.
   155→	AgentLinesAdded int `json:"agent_lines_added"`
   156→
   157→	// AgentLinesRemoved is total agent lines removed so far (base → last checkpoint).
   158→	// Always 0 for checkpoint 1 since there's no previous checkpoint to measure against.
   159→	AgentLinesRemoved int `json:"agent_lines_removed"`
   160→
   161→	// UserAddedPerFile tracks per-file user additions for accurate modification tracking.
   162→	// This enables distinguishing user self-modifications from agent modifications.
   163→	// See docs/architecture/attribution.md for details.
   164→	UserAddedPerFile map[string]int `json:"user_added_per_file,omitempty"`
   165→}
   166→
   167→// NormalizeAfterLoad applies backward-compatible migrations to state loaded from disk.
   168→// Call this after deserializing a State from JSON.
   169→func (s *State) NormalizeAfterLoad() {
   170→	// Normalize legacy phase values. "active_committed" was removed with the
   171→	// 1:1 checkpoint model in favor of the state machine handling commits
   172→	// during ACTIVE phase with immediate condensation.
   173→	if s.Phase == "active_committed" {
   174→		logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "session")
   175→		logging.Info(logCtx, "migrating legacy active_committed phase to active",
   176→			slog.String("session_id", s.SessionID),
   177→		)
   178→		s.Phase = PhaseActive
   179→	}
   180→	// Also normalize via PhaseFromString to handle any other legacy/unknown values.
   181→	s.Phase = PhaseFromString(string(s.Phase))
   182→
   183→	// Migrate transcript fields: CheckpointTranscriptStart replaces both
   184→	// CondensedTranscriptLines and TranscriptLinesAtStart from older state files.
   185→	if s.CheckpointTranscriptStart == 0 {
   186→		if s.CondensedTranscriptLines > 0 {
   187→			s.CheckpointTranscriptStart = s.CondensedTranscriptLines
   188→		} else if s.TranscriptLinesAtStart > 0 {
   189→			s.CheckpointTranscriptStart = s.TranscriptLinesAtStart
   190→		}
   191→	}
   192→	// Clear deprecated fields so they aren't re-persisted.
   193→	// Note: this is a one-way migration. If the state is re-saved, older CLI versions
   194→	// will see 0 for these fields and fall back to scoping from the transcript start.
   195→	// This is acceptable since CLI upgrades are monotonic and the worst case is
   196→	// redundant transcript content in a condensation, not data loss.
   197→	s.CondensedTranscriptLines = 0
   198→	s.TranscriptLinesAtStart = 0
   199→
   200→	// Backfill AttributionBaseCommit for sessions created before this field existed.
   201→	// Without this, a mid-turn commit would migrate BaseCommit and the fallback in
   202→	// calculateSessionAttributions would use the migrated value, producing zero attribution.
   203→	if s.AttributionBaseCommit == "" && s.BaseCommit != "" {
   204→		s.AttributionBaseCommit = s.BaseCommit
   205→	}
   206→}
   207→
   208→// StateStore provides low-level operations for managing session state files.
   209→//
   210→// StateStore is a primitive for session state persistence. It is NOT the same as
   211→// the Sessions interface - it only handles state files in .git/entire-sessions/,
   212→// not the full session data which includes checkpoint content.
   213→//
   214→// Use StateStore directly in strategies for performance-critical state operations.
   215→// Use the Sessions interface (when implemented) for high-level session management.
   216→type StateStore struct {
   217→	// stateDir is the directory where session state files are stored
   218→	stateDir string
   219→}
   220→
   221→// NewStateStore creates a new state store.
   222→// Uses the git common dir to store session state (shared across worktrees).
   223→func NewStateStore() (*StateStore, error) {
   224→	commonDir, err := getGitCommonDir()
   225→	if err != nil {
   226→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get git common dir: %w", err)
   227→	}
   228→	return &StateStore{
   229→		stateDir: filepath.Join(commonDir, SessionStateDirName),
   230→	}, nil
   231→}
   232→
   233→// NewStateStoreWithDir creates a new state store with a custom directory.
   234→// This is useful for testing.
   235→func NewStateStoreWithDir(stateDir string) *StateStore {
   236→	return &StateStore{stateDir: stateDir}
   237→}
   238→
   239→// Load loads the session state for the given session ID.
   240→// Returns (nil, nil) when session file doesn't exist (not an error condition).
   241→func (s *StateStore) Load(ctx context.Context, sessionID string) (*State, error) {
   242→	_ = ctx // Reserved for future use
   243→
   244→	// Validate session ID to prevent path traversal
   245→	if err := validation.ValidateSessionID(sessionID); err != nil {
   246→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("invalid session ID: %w", err)
   247→	}
   248→
   249→	stateFile := s.stateFilePath(sessionID)
   250→
   251→	data, err := os.ReadFile(stateFile) //nolint:gosec // stateFile is derived from sessionID
   252→	if os.IsNotExist(err) {
   253→		return nil, nil //nolint:nilnil // nil,nil indicates session not found (expected case)
   254→	}
   255→	if err != nil {
   256→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read session state: %w", err)
   257→	}
   258→
   259→	var state State
   260→	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &state); err != nil {
   261→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to unmarshal session state: %w", err)
   262→	}
   263→	state.NormalizeAfterLoad()
   264→	return &state, nil
   265→}
   266→
   267→// Save saves the session state atomically.
   268→func (s *StateStore) Save(ctx context.Context, state *State) error {
   269→	_ = ctx // Reserved for future use
   270→
   271→	// Validate session ID to prevent path traversal
   272→	if err := validation.ValidateSessionID(state.SessionID); err != nil {
   273→		return fmt.Errorf("invalid session ID: %w", err)
   274→	}
   275→
   276→	if err := os.MkdirAll(s.stateDir, 0o750); err != nil {
   277→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create session state directory: %w", err)
   278→	}
   279→
   280→	data, err := jsonutil.MarshalIndentWithNewline(state, "", "  ")
   281→	if err != nil {
   282→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal session state: %w", err)
   283→	}
   284→
   285→	stateFile := s.stateFilePath(state.SessionID)
   286→
   287→	// Atomic write: write to temp file, then rename
   288→	tmpFile := stateFile + ".tmp"
   289→	if err := os.WriteFile(tmpFile, data, 0o600); err != nil {
   290→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to write session state: %w", err)
   291→	}
   292→	if err := os.Rename(tmpFile, stateFile); err != nil {
   293→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to rename session state file: %w", err)
   294→	}
   295→	return nil
   296→}
   297→
   298→// Clear removes the session state file for the given session ID.
   299→func (s *StateStore) Clear(ctx context.Context, sessionID string) error {
   300→	_ = ctx // Reserved for future use
   301→
   302→	// Validate session ID to prevent path traversal
   303→	if err := validation.ValidateSessionID(sessionID); err != nil {
   304→		return fmt.Errorf("invalid session ID: %w", err)
   305→	}
   306→
   307→	stateFile := s.stateFilePath(sessionID)
   308→
   309→	if err := os.Remove(stateFile); err != nil {
   310→		if os.IsNotExist(err) {
   311→			return nil // Already gone, not an error
   312→		}
   313→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to remove session state file: %w", err)
   314→	}
   315→	return nil
   316→}
   317→
   318→// RemoveAll removes the entire session state directory.
   319→// This is used during uninstall to completely remove all session state.
   320→func (s *StateStore) RemoveAll() error {
   321→	if err := os.RemoveAll(s.stateDir); err != nil {
   322→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to remove session state directory: %w", err)
   323→	}
   324→	return nil
   325→}
   326→
   327→// List returns all session states.
   328→func (s *StateStore) List(ctx context.Context) ([]*State, error) {
   329→	_ = ctx // Reserved for future use
   330→
   331→	entries, err := os.ReadDir(s.stateDir)
   332→	if os.IsNotExist(err) {
   333→		return nil, nil
   334→	}
   335→	if err != nil {
   336→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read session state directory: %w", err)
   337→	}
   338→
   339→	var states []*State
   340→	for _, entry := range entries {
   341→		if entry.IsDir() || !strings.HasSuffix(entry.Name(), ".json") {
   342→			continue
   343→		}
   344→		if strings.HasSuffix(entry.Name(), ".tmp") {
   345→			continue // Skip temp files
   346→		}
   347→
   348→		sessionID := strings.TrimSuffix(entry.Name(), ".json")
   349→		state, err := s.Load(ctx, sessionID)
   350→		if err != nil {
   351→			continue // Skip corrupted state files
   352→		}
   353→		if state == nil {
   354→			continue
   355→		}
   356→
   357→		states = append(states, state)
   358→	}
   359→	return states, nil
   360→}
   361→
   362→// stateFilePath returns the path to a session state file.
   363→func (s *StateStore) stateFilePath(sessionID string) string {
   364→	return filepath.Join(s.stateDir, sessionID+".json")
   365→}
   366→
   367→// getGitCommonDir returns the path to the shared git directory.
   368→// In a regular checkout, this is .git/
   369→// In a worktree, this is the main repo's .git/ (not .git/worktrees/<name>/)
   370→func getGitCommonDir() (string, error) {
   371→	ctx := context.Background()
   372→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "rev-parse", "--git-common-dir")
   373→	cmd.Dir = "."
   374→	output, err := cmd.Output()
   375→	if err != nil {
   376→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to get git common dir: %w", err)
   377→	}
   378→
   379→	commonDir := strings.TrimSpace(string(output))
   380→
   381→	// git rev-parse --git-common-dir returns relative paths from the working directory,
   382→	// so we need to make it absolute if it isn't already
   383→	if !filepath.IsAbs(commonDir) {
   384→		commonDir = filepath.Join(".", commonDir)
   385→	}
   386→
   387→	return filepath.Clean(commonDir), nil
   388→}
   389→
```

Now I have the full picture. Here's the complete call chain for `session-start`, with every I/O operation:

**`PersistentPreRunE` (hook_registry.go:54)**
1. `initHookLogging()` → `FindMostRecentSession()` → `ListSessionStates()` → `NewStateStore()` → **`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`** (subprocess) → `store.List()` → **`ReadDir` + `ReadFile` × N session files** → `GetWorktreePath()` → **`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`** (subprocess)
2. `logging.Init()` → **`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`** (cached) → **mkdir + open** `.entire/logs/entire.log`

**`RunE` (hook_registry.go:96)**
3. `paths.RepoRoot()` → **`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`** (cached)
4. `IsEnabled()` → `settings.Load()` → `paths.AbsPath()` → **`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`** (cached) → **`ReadFile`** `.entire/settings.json` + **`ReadFile`** `.entire/settings.local.json`
5. `GetStrategy()` → `settings.Load()` → (**same as #4, repeated**)
6. `ag.ParseHookEvent()` → **read stdin**
7. `handleLifecycleSessionStart()`:
   - `GetStrategy()` → `settings.Load()` → (**same as #4, repeated again**)
   - `CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints()`:
     - `GetWorktreePath()` → **`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`** (subprocess, NOT cached — different package)
     - `OpenRepository()` → `GetWorktreePath()` → **`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`** (subprocess again) → **`go-git PlainOpen`** (reads `.git/` directory, packed-refs, etc.)
     - `repo.Head()` → **read `.git/HEAD`** + **ref resolve**
     - `listAllSessionStates()`:
       - `NewStateStore()` → **`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`** (subprocess)
       - `store.List()` → **`ReadDir` + `ReadFile` × N** session files
       - `OpenRepository()` → **`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`** (subprocess) + **`go-git PlainOpen`**
       - For each session: `repo.Reference(shadowBranch)` → **scan packed-refs**
   - `LoadSessionState()` → **`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`** (subprocess) → **`ReadFile`** session file
   - `SaveSessionState()` → **`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`** (subprocess) → **`WriteFile`** session file

Here are the dimensions, most likely to least likely:

**1. Git subprocess spawns** — There are **~7-8 `git rev-parse` subprocess spawns** per invocation. `paths.RepoRoot()` caches but `GetWorktreePath()` and `GetGitCommonDir()` don't. Each is ~5-15ms. On a slow system this alone is 50-100ms+.

**2. `go-git PlainOpen` calls** — Called **2-3 times** (no caching). Reads `.git/HEAD`, `.git/config`, `.git/packed-refs`, ref directories. On a large repo with a multi-GB `.git/`, this is expensive.

**3. Session state file count** — `store.List()` is called **twice** (once in `FindMostRecentSession`, once in `CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints`). Each call does `ReadDir` + `ReadFile` + JSON unmarshal per file.

**4. `repo.Reference()` per session × packed-refs size** — For each session state file, `listAllSessionStates` checks if the shadow branch exists. In a repo with hundreds of packed refs, each lookup scans the packed-refs file.

**5. `settings.Load()` calls** — Called **3 times** (no caching). Each reads 2 files from disk + does `paths.AbsPath()` which calls `RepoRoot()`.

**6. Go binary startup + cobra init** — The process spawn itself + Go runtime init + cobra command tree registration. Fixed ~30-40ms cost.

**7. Logging initialization** — `mkdir` + file open for `.entire/logs/entire.log`.

**8. Packed-refs file size** — Affects every `repo.Reference()` call and every `go-git PlainOpen`. Large repos with thousands of branches/tags have multi-MB packed-refs.

**9. Filesystem latency** — Network filesystems (NFS, SSHFS), encrypted volumes, or spinning disks multiply every I/O operation above.

> DEVELOPER

which dimensions should we add in our benchmarking to test against?

> AGENT

Based on the code trace, the ones worth testing are:

1. **Git subprocess spawns** — 7-8 uncached `git rev-parse` calls per invocation. This is fixed cost but we should measure it in isolation to know the floor.

2. **Session state files** — `store.List()` called twice, each doing `ReadDir` + `ReadFile` + unmarshal + `repo.Reference()` per file. Already benchmarked but the range was too small to see scaling.

3. **Packed-refs size (git refs)** — Every `repo.Reference()` and `PlainOpen` scans packed-refs. Our current Refs benchmark showed flat results, but we were only creating branches without checking if the *sessions × refs interaction* matters. We should test sessions × refs together.

4. **`go-git PlainOpen` cost** — Called 2-3 times with no caching. Scales with `.git/` size (objects, packed-refs, config). We can approximate by seeding objects/packfiles.

5. **`settings.Load()` redundancy** — Called 3 times. Unlikely to be the bottleneck but easy to confirm by testing with large settings files or deep directory nesting (affects `AbsPath` → `RepoRoot`).

I'd skip repo file count, commit history, and transcript size — those don't appear anywhere in the session-start path.

Want me to update the benchmark with these dimensions?

   124→// runSessionStartHook is the shared benchmark loop that invokes the session-start
   125→// hook as a subprocess and reports latency in ms/op.
   126→func runSessionStartHook(b *testing.B, repo *benchutil.BenchRepo) {
   127→	b.Helper()
   128→
   129→	stdinPayload, err := json.Marshal(map[string]string{
   130→		"session_id":      "bench-session",
   131→		"transcript_path": "",
   132→	})
   133→	if err != nil {
   134→		b.Fatalf("marshal stdin: %v", err)
   135→	}
   136→
   137→	binary := getTestBinary()
   138→	claudeProjectDir := b.TempDir()
   139→
   140→	b.ResetTimer()
   141→	for range b.N {
   142→		start := time.Now()
   143→
   144→		cmd := exec.Command(binary, "hooks", "claude-code", "session-start")
   145→		cmd.Dir = repo.Dir
   146→		cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(stdinPayload)
   147→		cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   148→			"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+claudeProjectDir,
   149→		)
   150→
   151→		output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   152→		if err != nil {
   153→			b.Fatalf("session-start hook failed: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   154→		}
   155→
   156→		b.ReportMetric(float64(time.Since(start).Milliseconds()), "ms/op")
   157→	}
   158→}

    30→// BenchRepo is a fully initialized git repository with Entire configured,
    31→// ready for checkpoint benchmarks.
    32→type BenchRepo struct {
    33→	// Dir is the absolute path to the repository root.
    34→	Dir string
    35→
    36→	// Repo is the go-git repository handle.

    38→
    39→	// Store is the checkpoint GitStore for this repo.

    41→
    42→	// HeadHash is the current HEAD commit hash string.

    46→	WorktreeID string
    47→}
    48→
    49→// RepoOpts configures how NewBenchRepo creates the test repository.
    50→type RepoOpts struct {
    51→	// FileCount is the number of tracked files to create in the initial commit.
    52→	// Each file is ~100 lines of Go code. Defaults to 10.
    53→	FileCount int
    54→
    55→	// FileSizeLines is the number of lines per file. Defaults to 100.
    56→	FileSizeLines int
    57→
    58→	// CommitCount is the number of commits to create. Defaults to 1.
    59→	CommitCount int
    60→
    61→	// Strategy is the strategy name for .entire/settings.json.
    62→	// Defaults to "manual-commit".
    63→	Strategy string
    64→
    65→	// FeatureBranch, if non-empty, creates and checks out this branch
    66→	// after the initial commits.
    67→	FeatureBranch string
    68→}
    69→
    70→func (o *RepoOpts) withDefaults() RepoOpts {
    71→	out := *o
    72→	if out.FileCount == 0 {
    73→		out.FileCount = 10
    74→	}
    75→	if out.FileSizeLines == 0 {
    76→		out.FileSizeLines = 100
    77→	}
    78→	if out.CommitCount == 0 {
    79→		out.CommitCount = 1
    80→	}
    81→	if out.Strategy == "" {
    82→		out.Strategy = "manual-commit"
    83→	}
    84→	return out
    85→}
    86→
    87→// NewBenchRepo creates an isolated git repository for benchmarks.
    88→// The repo has an initial commit with the configured number of files,
    89→// a .gitignore excluding .entire/, and Entire settings initialized.
    90→//
    91→// Uses b.TempDir() so cleanup is automatic.
    92→func NewBenchRepo(b *testing.B, opts RepoOpts) *BenchRepo {
    93→	b.Helper()
    94→	opts = opts.withDefaults()
    95→
    96→	dir := b.TempDir()
    97→	// Resolve symlinks (macOS /var -> /private/var)
    98→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(dir); err == nil {
    99→		dir = resolved
   100→	}
   101→
   102→	// Init repo
   103→	repo, err := git.PlainInit(dir, false)
   104→	if err != nil {
   105→		b.Fatalf("git init: %v", err)
   106→	}
   107→
   108→	// Create .gitignore and .entire settings
   109→	writeFile(b, dir, ".gitignore", ".entire/\n")
   110→	initEntireSettings(b, dir, opts.Strategy)
   111→
   112→	// Generate initial files
   113→	wt, err := repo.Worktree()
   114→	if err != nil {
   115→		b.Fatalf("worktree: %v", err)
   116→	}
   117→
   118→	for i := range opts.FileCount {
   119→		name := fmt.Sprintf("src/file_%03d.go", i)
   120→		content := GenerateGoFile(i, opts.FileSizeLines)
   121→		writeFile(b, dir, name, content)
   122→		if _, err := wt.Add(name); err != nil {
   123→			b.Fatalf("add %s: %v", name, err)
   124→		}
   125→	}
   126→	if _, err := wt.Add(".gitignore"); err != nil {
   127→		b.Fatalf("add .gitignore: %v", err)
   128→	}
   129→
   130→	// Create commits
   131→	var headHash plumbing.Hash
   132→	for c := range opts.CommitCount {
   133→		if c > 0 {
   134→			// Modify a file for subsequent commits
   135→			name := fmt.Sprintf("src/file_%03d.go", c%opts.FileCount)
   136→			content := GenerateGoFile(c*1000, opts.FileSizeLines)
   137→			writeFile(b, dir, name, content)
   138→			if _, err := wt.Add(name); err != nil {
   139→				b.Fatalf("add %s: %v", name, err)
   140→			}
   141→		}
   142→		headHash, err = wt.Commit(fmt.Sprintf("Commit %d", c+1), &git.CommitOptions{
   143→			Author: &object.Signature{
   144→				Name:  "Bench User",
   145→				Email: "bench@example.com",
   146→				When:  time.Now(),
   147→			},
   148→		})
   149→		if err != nil {
   150→			b.Fatalf("commit %d: %v", c+1, err)
   151→		}
   152→	}
   153→
   154→	// Optionally create feature branch
   155→	if opts.FeatureBranch != "" {
   156→		ref := plumbing.NewHashReference(
   157→			plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(opts.FeatureBranch), headHash)
   158→		if err := repo.Storer.SetReference(ref); err != nil {
   159→			b.Fatalf("create branch: %v", err)
   160→		}
   161→		// Checkout via git CLI (go-git v5 checkout bug)
   162→		checkoutBranch(b, dir, opts.FeatureBranch)
   163→	}
   164→
   165→	br := &BenchRepo{
   166→		Dir:      dir,
   167→		Repo:     repo,
   168→		Store:    checkpoint.NewGitStore(repo),
   169→		HeadHash: headHash.String(),
   170→	}
   171→
   172→	// Determine worktree ID
   173→	wtID, err := paths.GetWorktreeID(dir)
   174→	if err == nil {
   175→		br.WorktreeID = wtID
   176→	}
   177→
   178→	return br
   179→}
   180→
   181→// WriteFile creates or overwrites a file relative to the repo root.
   182→func (br *BenchRepo) WriteFile(b *testing.B, relPath, content string) {
   183→	b.Helper()
   184→	writeFile(b, br.Dir, relPath, content)
   185→}
   186→
   187→// AddAndCommit stages the given files and creates a commit.
   188→// Returns the new HEAD hash.
   189→func (br *BenchRepo) AddAndCommit(b *testing.B, message string, files ...string) string {
   190→	b.Helper()
   191→	wt, err := br.Repo.Worktree()
   192→	if err != nil {
   193→		b.Fatalf("worktree: %v", err)
   194→	}
   195→	for _, f := range files {
   196→		if _, err := wt.Add(f); err != nil {
   197→			b.Fatalf("add %s: %v", f, err)
   198→		}
   199→	}
   200→	hash, err := wt.Commit(message, &git.CommitOptions{
   201→		Author: &object.Signature{
   202→			Name:  "Bench User",
   203→			Email: "bench@example.com",
   204→			When:  time.Now(),
   205→		},
   206→	})
   207→	if err != nil {
   208→		b.Fatalf("commit: %v", err)
   209→	}
   210→	br.HeadHash = hash.String()
   211→	return hash.String()
   212→}
   213→
   214→// SessionOpts configures how CreateSessionState creates a session state file.
   215→type SessionOpts struct {
   216→	// SessionID is the session identifier. Auto-generated if empty.
   217→	SessionID string
   218→
   219→	// Phase is the session phase. Defaults to session.PhaseActive.
   220→	Phase session.Phase
   221→
   222→	// StepCount is the number of prior checkpoints. Defaults to 0.
   223→	StepCount int
   224→
   225→	// FilesTouched is the list of files tracked by this session.
   226→	FilesTouched []string
   227→
   228→	// TranscriptPath is the path to the live transcript file.
   229→	TranscriptPath string
   230→
   231→	// AgentType is the agent type. Defaults to agent.AgentTypeClaudeCode.
   232→	AgentType agent.AgentType
   233→}
   234→
   235→// CreateSessionState writes a session state file to .git/entire-sessions/.
   236→// Returns the session ID used.
   237→func (br *BenchRepo) CreateSessionState(b *testing.B, opts SessionOpts) string {
   238→	b.Helper()
   239→
   240→	if opts.SessionID == "" {
   241→		cpID, err := id.Generate()
   242→		if err != nil {
   243→			b.Fatalf("generate session ID: %v", err)
   244→		}
   245→		opts.SessionID = fmt.Sprintf("bench-%s", cpID)
   246→	}
   247→	if opts.Phase == "" {
   248→		opts.Phase = session.PhaseActive
   249→	}
   250→
   251→	if opts.AgentType == "" {
   252→		opts.AgentType = agent.AgentTypeClaudeCode
   253→	}
   254→
   255→	now := time.Now()
   256→	state := &session.State{
   257→		SessionID:      opts.SessionID,
   258→		BaseCommit:     br.HeadHash,
   259→		WorktreePath:   br.Dir,
   260→		WorktreeID:     br.WorktreeID,
   261→		StartedAt:      now,
   262→		Phase:          opts.Phase,
   263→		StepCount:      opts.StepCount,
   264→		FilesTouched:   opts.FilesTouched,
   265→		TranscriptPath: opts.TranscriptPath,
   266→		AgentType:      opts.AgentType,
   267→	}
   268→
   269→	// Write to .git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json
   270→	gitDir := filepath.Join(br.Dir, ".git")
   271→	sessDir := filepath.Join(gitDir, session.SessionStateDirName)
   272→	if err := os.MkdirAll(sessDir, 0o750); err != nil {
   273→		b.Fatalf("mkdir sessions: %v", err)
   274→	}
   275→
   276→	data, err := jsonutil.MarshalIndentWithNewline(state, "", "  ")
   277→	if err != nil {
   278→		b.Fatalf("marshal state: %v", err)
   279→	}
   280→
   281→	statePath := filepath.Join(sessDir, opts.SessionID+".json")
   282→	if err := os.WriteFile(statePath, data, 0o600); err != nil {
   283→		b.Fatalf("write state: %v", err)
   284→	}
   285→
   286→	return opts.SessionID
   287→}

   289→// TranscriptOpts configures how GenerateTranscript creates JSONL data.
   290→type TranscriptOpts struct {
   291→	// MessageCount is the number of JSONL messages to generate.
   292→	MessageCount int
   293→
   294→	// AvgMessageBytes is the approximate size of each message's content field.
   295→	// Defaults to 500.
   296→	AvgMessageBytes int
   297→
   298→	// IncludeToolUse adds realistic tool_use messages (file edits, bash commands).
   299→	IncludeToolUse bool
   300→
   301→	// FilesTouched is the list of files to reference in tool_use messages.
   302→	// Only used when IncludeToolUse is true.
   303→	FilesTouched []string
   304→}
   305→
   306→// GenerateTranscript creates realistic Claude Code JSONL transcript data.
   307→// Returns the raw bytes suitable for writing to full.jsonl.
   308→func GenerateTranscript(opts TranscriptOpts) []byte {
   309→	if opts.AvgMessageBytes == 0 {
   310→		opts.AvgMessageBytes = 500
   311→	}
   312→
   313→	var buf strings.Builder
   314→	for i := range opts.MessageCount {
   315→		msg := generateTranscriptMessage(i, opts)
   316→		data, err := json.Marshal(msg)
   317→		if err != nil {
   318→			// Should never happen with map[string]any, but satisfy errcheck
   319→			continue
   320→		}
   321→		buf.Write(data)
   322→		buf.WriteByte('\n')
   323→	}
   324→	return []byte(buf.String())
   325→}
   326→
   327→// WriteTranscriptFile writes transcript data to a file and returns the path.
   328→func (br *BenchRepo) WriteTranscriptFile(b *testing.B, sessionID string, data []byte) string {
   329→	b.Helper()
   330→	// Write to .entire/metadata/<session-id>/full.jsonl (matching real layout)
   331→	relDir := filepath.Join(".entire", "metadata", sessionID)
   332→	relPath := filepath.Join(relDir, "full.jsonl")
   333→	absDir := filepath.Join(br.Dir, relDir)
   334→	if err := os.MkdirAll(absDir, 0o750); err != nil {
   335→		b.Fatalf("mkdir transcript dir: %v", err)
   336→	}
   337→	absPath := filepath.Join(br.Dir, relPath)
   338→	if err := os.WriteFile(absPath, data, 0o600); err != nil {
   339→		b.Fatalf("write transcript: %v", err)
   340→	}
   341→	return absPath
   342→}
   343→
   344→// SeedShadowBranch creates N checkpoint commits on the shadow branch
   345→// for the current HEAD. This simulates a session that already has
   346→// prior checkpoints saved.
   347→func (br *BenchRepo) SeedShadowBranch(b *testing.B, sessionID string, checkpointCount int, filesPerCheckpoint int) {
   348→	b.Helper()
   349→
   350→	for i := range checkpointCount {
   351→		var modified []string
   352→		for j := range filesPerCheckpoint {
   353→			name := fmt.Sprintf("src/file_%03d.go", j)
   354→			content := GenerateGoFile(i*1000+j, 100)
   355→			writeFile(b, br.Dir, name, content)
   356→			modified = append(modified, name)
   357→		}
   358→
   359→		metadataDir := paths.SessionMetadataDirFromSessionID(sessionID)
   360→		metadataDirAbs := filepath.Join(br.Dir, metadataDir)
   361→		if err := os.MkdirAll(metadataDirAbs, 0o750); err != nil {
   362→			b.Fatalf("mkdir metadata: %v", err)
   363→		}
   364→
   365→		// Write a minimal transcript to the metadata dir
   366→		transcriptPath := filepath.Join(metadataDirAbs, "full.jsonl")
   367→		transcript := GenerateTranscript(TranscriptOpts{MessageCount: 5, AvgMessageBytes: 200})
   368→		if err := os.WriteFile(transcriptPath, transcript, 0o600); err != nil {
   369→			b.Fatalf("write transcript: %v", err)
   370→		}
   371→
   372→		_, err := br.Store.WriteTemporary(context.Background(), checkpoint.WriteTemporaryOptions{
   373→			SessionID:         sessionID,
   374→			BaseCommit:        br.HeadHash,
   375→			WorktreeID:        br.WorktreeID,
   376→			ModifiedFiles:     modified,
   377→			MetadataDir:       metadataDir,
   378→			MetadataDirAbs:    metadataDirAbs,
   379→			CommitMessage:     fmt.Sprintf("Checkpoint %d", i+1),
   380→			AuthorName:        "Bench User",
   381→			AuthorEmail:       "bench@example.com",
   382→			IsFirstCheckpoint: i == 0,
   383→		})
   384→		if err != nil {
   385→			b.Fatalf("write temporary checkpoint %d: %v", i+1, err)
   386→		}
   387→	}
   388→}
   389→
   390→// SeedMetadataBranch creates N committed checkpoints on the entire/checkpoints/v1
   391→// branch. This simulates a repository with prior checkpoint history.
   392→func (br *BenchRepo) SeedMetadataBranch(b *testing.B, checkpointCount int) {
   393→	b.Helper()
   394→
   395→	for i := range checkpointCount {
   396→		cpID, err := id.Generate()
   397→		if err != nil {
   398→			b.Fatalf("generate checkpoint ID: %v", err)
   399→		}
   400→		sessionID := fmt.Sprintf("seed-session-%04d", i)
   401→		transcript := GenerateTranscript(TranscriptOpts{
   402→			MessageCount:    20,
   403→			AvgMessageBytes: 300,
   404→		})
   405→
   406→		files := make([]string, 0, 5)
   407→		for j := range 5 {
   408→			files = append(files, fmt.Sprintf("src/file_%03d.go", (i*5+j)%100))
   409→		}
   410→
   411→		err = br.Store.WriteCommitted(context.Background(), checkpoint.WriteCommittedOptions{
   412→			CheckpointID:     cpID,
   413→			SessionID:        sessionID,
   414→			Strategy:         "manual-commit",
   415→			Transcript:       transcript,
   416→			Prompts:          []string{fmt.Sprintf("Implement feature %d", i)},
   417→			FilesTouched:     files,
   418→			CheckpointsCount: 3,
   419→			AuthorName:       "Bench User",
   420→			AuthorEmail:      "bench@example.com",
   421→			Agent:            agent.AgentTypeClaudeCode,
   422→		})
   423→		if err != nil {
   424→			b.Fatalf("write committed checkpoint %d: %v", i+1, err)
   425→		}
   426→	}
   427→}
   428→
   429→// GenerateGoFile creates a synthetic Go source file with the given number of lines.
   430→// The seed value ensures unique content for each file.
   431→func GenerateGoFile(seed, lines int) string {
   432→	var buf strings.Builder
   433→	fmt.Fprintf(&buf, "package pkg%d\n\n", seed%100)
   434→
   435→	lineNum := 2
   436→	funcNum := 0
   437→	for lineNum < lines {
   438→		funcName := fmt.Sprintf("func%d_%d", seed, funcNum)
   439→		fmt.Fprintf(&buf, "func %s(ctx context.Context, input string) (string, error) {\n", funcName)
   440→		lineNum++
   441→
   442→		bodyLines := min(8, lines-lineNum-1)
   443→		for j := range bodyLines {
   444→			fmt.Fprintf(&buf, "\tv%d := fmt.Sprintf(\"processing %%s step %d seed %d\", input)\n", j, j, seed)
   445→			lineNum++
   446→		}
   447→		buf.WriteString("\treturn \"\", nil\n}\n\n")
   448→		lineNum += 2
   449→		funcNum++
   450→	}
   451→	return buf.String()
   452→}
   453→
   454→// GenerateFileContent creates generic file content of approximately the given byte size.
   455→func GenerateFileContent(seed, sizeBytes int) string {
   456→	var buf strings.Builder
   457→	line := fmt.Sprintf("// Line content seed=%d ", seed)
   458→	padding := strings.Repeat("x", max(1, 80-len(line)))
   459→	fullLine := line + padding + "\n"
   460→
   461→	for buf.Len() < sizeBytes {
   462→		buf.WriteString(fullLine)
   463→	}
   464→	return buf.String()
   465→}
   466→
   467→//nolint:gosec // G301/G306: benchmark fixtures use standard permissions in temp dirs
   468→func writeFile(b *testing.B, dir, relPath, content string) {
   469→	b.Helper()
   470→	abs := filepath.Join(dir, relPath)
   471→	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(abs), 0o755); err != nil {
   472→		b.Fatalf("mkdir %s: %v", filepath.Dir(relPath), err)
   473→	}
   474→	if err := os.WriteFile(abs, []byte(content), 0o644); err != nil {
   475→		b.Fatalf("write %s: %v", relPath, err)
   476→	}
   477→}
   478→
   479→//nolint:gosec // G301/G306: benchmark fixtures use standard permissions in temp dirs
   480→func initEntireSettings(b *testing.B, dir, strategy string) {
   481→	b.Helper()
   482→	entireDir := filepath.Join(dir, ".entire")
   483→	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Join(entireDir, "tmp"), 0o755); err != nil {
   484→		b.Fatalf("mkdir .entire: %v", err)
   485→	}
   486→
   487→	settings := map[string]any{
   488→		"strategy":  strategy,
   489→		"local_dev": true,
   490→	}
   491→	data, err := jsonutil.MarshalIndentWithNewline(settings, "", "  ")
   492→	if err != nil {

   496→		b.Fatalf("write settings: %v", err)

   500→func checkoutBranch(b *testing.B, dir, branch string) {
   501→	b.Helper()
   502→	c := exec.CommandContext(context.Background(), "git", "checkout", branch)
   503→	c.Dir = dir
   504→	if output, err := c.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   505→		b.Fatalf("git checkout %s: %v\n%s", branch, err, output)
   506→	}
   507→}
   508→
   509→// generateTranscriptMessage creates a single JSONL message for a Claude Code transcript.
   510→func generateTranscriptMessage(index int, opts TranscriptOpts) map[string]any {
   511→	msg := map[string]any{
   512→		"uuid":        fmt.Sprintf("msg_%06d", index),
   513→		"timestamp":   time.Now().Add(time.Duration(index) * time.Second).Format(time.RFC3339),
   514→		"parent_uuid": fmt.Sprintf("msg_%06d", max(0, index-1)),
   515→	}
   516→
   517→	switch {
   518→	case opts.IncludeToolUse && index%3 == 2 && len(opts.FilesTouched) > 0:
   519→		// Tool use message (every 3rd message)
   520→		file := opts.FilesTouched[index%len(opts.FilesTouched)]
   521→		msg["type"] = "tool_use"
   522→		msg["tool_name"] = "write_to_file"
   523→		msg["tool_input"] = map[string]any{
   524→			"path":    file,
   525→			"content": GenerateFileContent(index, opts.AvgMessageBytes/2),
   526→		}
   527→	case index%2 == 0:
   528→		// Assistant message
   529→		msg["type"] = "assistant"
   530→		msg["content"] = generatePadding("I'll help you implement this feature. ", opts.AvgMessageBytes)
   531→	default:
   532→		// Human message
   533→		msg["type"] = "human"
   534→		msg["content"] = generatePadding("Please update the implementation. ", opts.AvgMessageBytes/3)
   535→	}
   536→
   537→	return msg
   538→}
   539→
   540→// SeedBranches creates N branches pointing at the current HEAD.
   541→// The branches are named with the given prefix (e.g., "feature/bench-" → "feature/bench-000").
   542→// This simulates a repo with many refs, which affects go-git ref scanning performance.
   543→func (br *BenchRepo) SeedBranches(b *testing.B, prefix string, count int) {
   544→	b.Helper()
   545→	headHash := plumbing.NewHash(br.HeadHash)
   546→	for i := range count {
   547→		name := fmt.Sprintf("%s%03d", prefix, i)
   548→		ref := plumbing.NewHashReference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(name), headHash)
   549→		if err := br.Repo.Storer.SetReference(ref); err != nil {
   550→			b.Fatalf("create branch %s: %v", name, err)
   551→		}
   552→	}
   553→}
   554→
   555→// PackRefs runs `git pack-refs --all` to simulate a real repo where most refs
   556→// are in the packed-refs file. Large repos almost always have packed refs.
   557→func (br *BenchRepo) PackRefs(b *testing.B) {
   558→	b.Helper()
   559→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(context.Background(), "git", "pack-refs", "--all")
   560→	cmd.Dir = br.Dir
   561→	if output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   562→		b.Fatalf("git pack-refs: %v\n%s", err, output)
   563→	}
   564→}
   565→
   566→func generatePadding(prefix string, targetBytes int) string {
   567→	if len(prefix) >= targetBytes {
   568→		return prefix[:targetBytes]
   569→	}
   570→	padding := strings.Repeat("Lorem ipsum dolor sit amet. ", (targetBytes-len(prefix))/28+1)
   571→	result := prefix + padding
   572→	if len(result) > targetBytes {
   573→		return result[:targetBytes]
   574→	}
   575→	return result
   576→}
   577→

Large repos almost always have packed refs.\nfunc (br *BenchRepo) PackRefs(b *testing.B) {\n\tb.Helper()\n\tcmd := exec.CommandContext(context.Background(), \"git\", \"pack-refs\", \"--all\")\n\tcmd.Dir = br.Dir\n\tif output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {\n\t\tb.Fatalf(\"git pack-refs: %v\\n%s\", err, output)\n\t}\n}",
  "new_string": "// PackRefs runs `git pack-refs --all` to simulate a real repo where most refs\n// are in the packed-refs file. Large repos almost always have packed refs.\nfunc (br *BenchRepo) PackRefs(b *testing.B) {\n\tb.Helper()\n\tcmd := exec.CommandContext(context.Background(), \"git\", \"pack-refs\", \"--all\")\n\tcmd.Dir = br.Dir\n\tif output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {\n\t\tb.Fatalf(\"git pack-refs: %v\\n%s\", err, output)\n\t}\n}\n\n// SeedGitObjects creates loose git objects to bloat .git/objects/.\n// Each call creates N blob objects via `git hash-object -w`.\n// After seeding, runs `git gc` to pack them into a packfile (realistic).\nfunc (br *BenchRepo) SeedGitObjects(b *testing.B, count int) {\n\tb.Helper()\n\n\tfor i := range count {\n\t\tcontent := GenerateFileContent(i, 4096)\n\t\tcmd := exec.CommandContext(context.Background(), \"git\", \"hash-object\", \"-w\", \"--stdin\")\n\t\tcmd.Dir = br.Dir\n\t\tcmd.Stdin = strings.NewReader(content)\n\t\tif output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {\n\t\t\tb.Fatalf(\"git hash-object %d: %v\\n%s\", i, err, output)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Pack into a packfile like a real repo\n\tgc := exec.CommandContext(context.Background(), \"git\", \"gc\", \"--quiet\")\n\tgc.Dir = br.Dir\n\tif output, err := gc.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {\n\t\tb.Fatalf(\"git gc: %v\\n%s\", err, output)\n\t}\n}"
}

  "content": "//go:build integration\n\npackage integration\n\nimport (\n\t\"bytes\"\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"os\"\n\t\"os/exec\"\n\t\"testing\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/benchutil\"\n)\n\n// BenchmarkHookSessionStart measures the end-to-end latency of the\n// \"entire hooks claude-code session-start\" subprocess.\n//\n// Each sub-benchmark isolates a single scaling dimension that appears\n// in the session-start hot path (see hook_registry.go → lifecycle.go):\n//\n//   - Sessions:     store.List() called 2x, ReadDir + ReadFile + unmarshal + repo.Reference() per file\n//   - SessionsXRefs: sessions × refs interaction (repo.Reference scans packed-refs per session)\n//   - PackedRefs:   go-git PlainOpen + repo.Reference cost with many packed refs\n//   - GitObjects:   go-git PlainOpen cost with large .git/objects packfile\n//\n// Run all:\n//\n//\tgo test -tags=integration -bench=BenchmarkHookSessionStart -benchtime=3x -run='^$' -timeout=10m ./cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/...\n//\n// Run one dimension:\n//\n//\tgo test -tags=integration -bench=BenchmarkHookSessionStart/Sessions/ -benchtime=5x -run='^$' ./cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/...\nfunc BenchmarkHookSessionStart(b *testing.B) {\n\tb.Run(\"Sessions\", benchSessions)\n\tb.Run(\"SessionsXRefs\", benchSessionsXRefs)\n\tb.Run(\"PackedRefs\", benchPackedRefs)\n\tb.Run(\"GitObjects\", benchGitObjects)\n}\n\n// benchSessions scales session state files in .git/entire-sessions/.\n// listAllSessionStates() is called twice: once in FindMostRecentSession (logging init),\n// once in CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints. Each call does\n// ReadDir + (ReadFile + JSON unmarshal + repo.Reference) per file.\nfunc benchSessions(b *testing.B) {\n\tfor _, n := range []int{0, 1, 5, 10, 25, 50, 100, 200} {\n\t\tb.Run(fmt.Sprintf(\"%d\", n), func(b *testing.B) {\n\t\t\trepo := benchutil.NewBenchRepo(b, benchutil.RepoOpts{\n\t\t\t\tFileCount:     10,\n\t\t\t\tFeatureBranch: \"feature/bench\",\n\t\t\t})\n\t\t\tfor range n {\n\t\t\t\trepo.CreateSessionState(b, benchutil.SessionOpts{\n\t\t\t\t\tStepCount:    3,\n\t\t\t\t\tFilesTouched: []string{\"src/file_000.go\", \"src/file_001.go\"},\n\t\t\t\t})\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\trunSessionStartHook(b, repo)\n\t\t})\n\t}\n}\n\n// benchSessionsXRefs tests the interaction between session count and ref count.\n// For each session, listAllSessionStates calls repo.Reference() which scans packed-refs.\n// Cost should be O(sessions × packed-refs-size).\nfunc benchSessionsXRefs(b *testing.B) {\n\ttype scenario struct {\n\t\tsessions int\n\t\trefs     int\n\t}\n\tscenarios := []scenario{\n\t\t{5, 10},\n\t\t{5, 500},\n\t\t{50, 10},\n\t\t{50, 500},\n\t\t{100, 500},\n\t}\n\tfor _, sc := range scenarios {\n\t\tb.Run(fmt.Sprintf(\"s%d_r%d\", sc.sessions, sc.refs), func(b *testing.B) {\n\t\t\trepo := benchutil.NewBenchRepo(b, benchutil.RepoOpts{\n\t\t\t\tFileCount:     10,\n\t\t\t\tFeatureBranch: \"feature/bench\",\n\t\t\t})\n\t\t\tfor range sc.sessions {\n\t\t\t\trepo.CreateSessionState(b, benchutil.SessionOpts{\n\t\t\t\t\tStepCount:    3,\n\t\t\t\t\tFilesTouched: []string{\"src/file_000.go\"},\n\t\t\t\t})\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\trepo.SeedBranches(b, \"feature/team-\", sc.refs)\n\t\t\trepo.PackRefs(b)\n\t\t\trunSessionStartHook(b, repo)\n\t\t})\n\t}\n}\n\n// benchPackedRefs scales the number of packed git refs (branches).\n// go-git PlainOpen reads packed-refs, and every repo.Reference() call\n// scans it. Session count held constant at 5.\nfunc benchPackedRefs(b *testing.B) {\n\tfor _, n := range []int{0, 50, 200, 500, 1000, 2000} {\n\t\tb.Run(fmt.Sprintf(\"%d\", n), func(b *testing.B) {\n\t\t\trepo := benchutil.NewBenchRepo(b, benchutil.RepoOpts{\n\t\t\t\tFileCount:     10,\n\t\t\t\tFeatureBranch: \"feature/bench\",\n\t\t\t})\n\t\t\tfor range 5 {\n\t\t\t\trepo.CreateSessionState(b, benchutil.SessionOpts{\n\t\t\t\t\tStepCount:    3,\n\t\t\t\t\tFilesTouched: []string{\"src/file_000.go\"},\n\t\t\t\t})\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif n > 0 {\n\t\t\t\trepo.SeedBranches(b, \"feature/team-\", n)\n\t\t\t\trepo.PackRefs(b)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\trunSessionStartHook(b, repo)\n\t\t})\n\t}\n}\n\n// benchGitObjects scales the .git/objects packfile size.\n// go-git PlainOpen parses pack indexes; large packs may slow it down.\n// This also affects repo.Head() and repo.Reference() indirectly.\nfunc benchGitObjects(b *testing.B) {\n\tfor _, n := range []int{0, 1000, 5000, 10000} {\n\t\tb.Run(fmt.Sprintf(\"%d\", n), func(b *testing.B) {\n\t\t\trepo := benchutil.NewBenchRepo(b, benchutil.RepoOpts{\n\t\t\t\tFileCount:     10,\n\t\t\t\tFeatureBranch: \"feature/bench\",\n\t\t\t})\n\t\t\tfor range 5 {\n\t\t\t\trepo.CreateSessionState(b, benchutil.SessionOpts{\n\t\t\t\t\tStepCount:    3,\n\t\t\t\t\tFilesTouched: []string{\"src/file_000.go\"},\n\t\t\t\t})\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif n > 0 {\n\t\t\t\trepo.SeedGitObjects(b, n)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\trunSessionStartHook(b, repo)\n\t\t})\n\t}\n}\n\n// runSessionStartHook is the shared benchmark loop that invokes the session-start\n// hook as a subprocess and reports latency in ms/op.\nfunc runSessionStartHook(b *testing.B, repo *benchutil.BenchRepo) {\n\tb.Helper()\n\n\tstdinPayload, err := json.Marshal(map[string]string{\n\t\t\"session_id\":      \"bench-session\",\n\t\t\"transcript_path\": \"\",\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tb.Fatalf(\"marshal stdin: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tbinary := getTestBinary()\n\tclaudeProjectDir := b.TempDir()\n\n\tb.ResetTimer()\n\tfor range b.N {\n\t\tstart := time.Now()\n\n\t\tcmd := exec.Command(binary, \"hooks\", \"claude-code\", \"session-start\")\n\t\tcmd.Dir = repo.Dir\n\t\tcmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(stdinPayload)\n\t\tcmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),\n\t\t\t\"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR=\"+claudeProjectDir,\n\t\t)\n\n\t\toutput, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tb.Fatalf(\"session-start hook failed: %v\\nOutput: %s\", err, output)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tb.ReportMetric(float64(time.Since(start).Milliseconds()), \"ms/op\")\n\t}\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

Results are in. Here's the summary:

| Dimension | Range | Latency Range | Trend |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Sessions** | 0 → 200 | 59ms → 91ms | +32ms (+54%) across 200 files |
| **Sessions×Refs** | 5s/10r → 100s/500r | 79ms → 86ms | Barely moves |
| **PackedRefs** | 0 → 2000 | 75ms → 80ms | Flat |
| **GitObjects** | 0 → 10k | 75ms → 86ms | Slight uptick at 10k |

**Key takeaway: nothing here explains 10s.** Even at extreme values (200 session files, 2000 refs, 10k objects), we're still under 100ms. The session-start hook itself is not the bottleneck.

This strongly suggests the customer's 10s latency is coming from one of:
1. **A different hook** — `user-prompt-submit` does `git status` on the worktree, which on a huge monorepo with 100k+ files could take seconds
2. **Multiple hooks firing sequentially** — Claude Code runs `session-start` then `user-prompt-submit` back-to-back, each spawning a subprocess
3. **Filesystem latency** — network mounts, encrypted FS, or spinning disk multiplying every I/O
4. **Git itself being slow** — `git rev-parse` taking seconds on a massive repo (our benchmarks use tiny repos where `git` returns in <5ms)

Want me to add a `user-prompt-submit` benchmark, or investigate what the actual customer environment looks like?

> DEVELOPER

how can our repo be tiny if have have 10k git objects? what am i missing there?

> AGENT

10k git *objects* (blobs) doesn't mean 10k *files in the worktree*. Our benchmark creates 10k anonymous blobs via `git hash-object -w` that are just loose data in `.git/objects/` — they aren't referenced by any tree or commit. The actual working tree still has only 10 files.

So go-git's `PlainOpen` doesn't care about those orphaned blobs. It reads `.git/HEAD`, `.git/config`, `.git/packed-refs`, and the pack index — but it doesn't enumerate every object on open. The pack index is a binary lookup table, so even a large packfile has O(1) lookups.

What actually makes a "large repo" slow for go-git is:
- **Large trees** — `repo.Head()` → resolve ref → commit object → tree object. A tree with 100k entries (like a monorepo root) is expensive to parse
- **Many commits on the ref path** — walking commit parents
- **Large pack index in memory** — go-git loads the full `.idx` file

To simulate a genuinely large repo, we'd need to create 10k+ *tracked files committed to a tree*, not just orphaned blobs. Our `RepoFiles` dimension from the earlier benchmark did this, but only went to 1000 files — and session-start doesn't walk the tree anyway.

The real question is whether `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (called 7-8 times as a subprocess) is slow on the customer's repo. That's a `git` CLI cost, not a go-git cost, and our benchmark doesn't distinguish between them because our `.git/` is always on a fast local SSD in a small temp dir.

Want me to add a dimension that measures pure `git rev-parse` subprocess cost in isolation, so we can see what fraction of the 80ms floor comes from those spawns?

> DEVELOPER

i mean how likely is that in a customer environment that they would have 10k untracked files? thinking about the normal development process, few developers are working on 10k files at a time? or am i thikning about this wrong?

> AGENT

You're right — 10k untracked files is unrealistic. But the dimension wasn't about *untracked* files. Let me clarify what I was trying to test and why it missed.

The `GitObjects` benchmark created 10k orphaned blobs in `.git/objects/` to bloat the packfile. The idea was to simulate a repo with a long history (thousands of commits over months/years = millions of objects). But as I just explained, go-git doesn't scan objects on `PlainOpen`, so this didn't test anything useful.

What a real customer repo looks like:
- **Tracked files in the tree**: 5k-50k (monorepo), but session-start doesn't walk the tree
- **Commits**: 10k-500k over years of history, but session-start doesn't walk history
- **Refs**: 100-2000 (feature branches, tags, release branches) — we tested this, it's flat

So none of the "repo size" dimensions matter for session-start because **session-start doesn't touch the worktree, the tree objects, or the commit history**. It only does:
1. Subprocess spawns (`git rev-parse` × 7-8)
2. Read session state files
3. Ref lookups for shadow branches

We've benchmarked 2 and 3 and they're fast. The only thing left that we haven't isolated is **#1 — the `git rev-parse` subprocess spawns**. Those 7-8 spawns account for most of the ~80ms floor, and on a customer machine they could be slower due to:
- Slow shell startup (heavy `.bashrc`/`.zshrc` that `git` inherits)
- Antivirus scanning each subprocess
- Slow disk for `/usr/bin/git` loading

Want me to add a benchmark that measures just `git rev-parse` round-trip time in isolation? That would tell us exactly how much of the 80ms is subprocess overhead vs actual hook logic.

> DEVELOPER

yes let's do that

    35→func BenchmarkHookSessionStart(b *testing.B) {
    36→	b.Run("Sessions", benchSessions)
    37→	b.Run("SessionsXRefs", benchSessionsXRefs)
    38→	b.Run("PackedRefs", benchPackedRefs)

    41→
    42→// benchSessions scales session state files in .git/entire-sessions/.
    43→// listAllSessionStates() is called twice: once in FindMostRecentSession (logging init),
    44→// once in CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints. Each call does
    45→// ReadDir + (ReadFile + JSON unmarshal + repo.Reference) per file.
    46→func benchSessions(b *testing.B) {
    47→	for _, n := range []int{0, 1, 5, 10, 25, 50, 100, 200} {
    48→		b.Run(fmt.Sprintf("%d", n), func(b *testing.B) {
    49→			repo := benchutil.NewBenchRepo(b, benchutil.RepoOpts{
    50→				FileCount:     10,
    51→				FeatureBranch: "feature/bench",
    52→			})
    53→			for range n {
    54→				repo.CreateSessionState(b, benchutil.SessionOpts{
    55→					StepCount:    3,
    56→					FilesTouched: []string{"src/file_000.go", "src/file_001.go"},
    57→				})
    58→			}
    59→			runSessionStartHook(b, repo)
    60→		})
    61→	}
    62→}
    63→
    64→// benchSessionsXRefs tests the interaction between session count and ref count.
    65→// For each session, listAllSessionStates calls repo.Reference() which scans packed-refs.
    66→// Cost should be O(sessions × packed-refs-size).
    67→func benchSessionsXRefs(b *testing.B) {

    70→		refs     int
    71→	}

    77→		{100, 500},
    78→	}
    79→	for _, sc := range scenarios {

    81→			repo := benchutil.NewBenchRepo(b, benchutil.RepoOpts{
    82→				FileCount:     10,
    83→				FeatureBranch: "feature/bench",
    84→			})

    87→					StepCount:    3,
    88→					FilesTouched: []string{"src/file_000.go"},
    89→				})
    90→			}
    91→			repo.SeedBranches(b, "feature/team-", sc.refs)
    92→			repo.PackRefs(b)
    93→			runSessionStartHook(b, repo)
    94→		})
    95→	}
    96→}
    97→
    98→// benchPackedRefs scales the number of packed git refs (branches).
    99→// go-git PlainOpen reads packed-refs, and every repo.Reference() call
   100→// scans it. Session count held constant at 5.
   101→func benchPackedRefs(b *testing.B) {
   102→	for _, n := range []int{0, 50, 200, 500, 1000, 2000} {
   103→		b.Run(fmt.Sprintf("%d", n), func(b *testing.B) {

   105→				FileCount:     10,
   106→				FeatureBranch: "feature/bench",
   107→			})
   108→			for range 5 {

   110→					StepCount:    3,
   111→					FilesTouched: []string{"src/file_000.go"},
   112→				})
   113→			}
   114→			if n > 0 {
   115→				repo.SeedBranches(b, "feature/team-", n)
   116→				repo.PackRefs(b)
   117→			}
   118→			runSessionStartHook(b, repo)
   119→		})
   120→	}
   121→}
   122→
   123→// benchGitObjects scales the .git/objects packfile size.
   124→// go-git PlainOpen parses pack indexes; large packs may slow it down.
   125→// This also affects repo.Head() and repo.Reference() indirectly.
   126→func benchGitObjects(b *testing.B) {
   127→	for _, n := range []int{0, 1000, 5000, 10000} {
   128→		b.Run(fmt.Sprintf("%d", n), func(b *testing.B) {
   129→			repo := benchutil.NewBenchRepo(b, benchutil.RepoOpts{
   130→				FileCount:     10,

   132→			})
   133→			for range 5 {
   134→				repo.CreateSessionState(b, benchutil.SessionOpts{
   135→					StepCount:    3,
   136→					FilesTouched: []string{"src/file_000.go"},
   137→				})
   138→			}
   139→			if n > 0 {
   140→				repo.SeedGitObjects(b, n)
   141→			}
   142→			runSessionStartHook(b, repo)
   143→		})
   144→	}
   145→}
   146→
   147→// runSessionStartHook is the shared benchmark loop that invokes the session-start
   148→// hook as a subprocess and reports latency in ms/op.
   149→func runSessionStartHook(b *testing.B, repo *benchutil.BenchRepo) {
   150→	b.Helper()
   151→
   152→	stdinPayload, err := json.Marshal(map[string]string{
   153→		"session_id":      "bench-session",
   154→		"transcript_path": "",
   155→	})
   156→	if err != nil {
   157→		b.Fatalf("marshal stdin: %v", err)
   158→	}
   159→
   160→	binary := getTestBinary()
   161→	claudeProjectDir := b.TempDir()
   162→
   163→	b.ResetTimer()
   164→	for range b.N {
   165→		start := time.Now()
   166→
   167→		cmd := exec.Command(binary, "hooks", "claude-code", "session-start")
   168→		cmd.Dir = repo.Dir
   169→		cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(stdinPayload)
   170→		cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   171→			"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+claudeProjectDir,
   172→		)
   173→
   174→		output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   175→		if err != nil {
   176→			b.Fatalf("session-start hook failed: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   177→		}
   178→
   179→		b.ReportMetric(float64(time.Since(start).Milliseconds()), "ms/op")
   180→	}
   181→}
   182→

| `entire` binary spawn (version) | **14ms** | 15% |
| **Full hook** | **91ms** | 100% |

**Two thirds of the session-start latency is `git rev-parse` subprocess spawns.** The actual hook logic (reading session files, checking refs, etc.) is only ~16ms (91 - 61 - 14).

On a customer machine where `git rev-parse` takes 50-100ms per call (large repo, slow FS, antivirus), those 7 calls alone would be **350-700ms**. Stack that with a heavier `entire` binary init and the `user-prompt-submit` hook doing the same thing again, and you're easily in multi-second territory.

The optimization path is clear: **cache or eliminate redundant `git rev-parse` calls**. `GetWorktreePath()` and `GetGitCommonDir()` are called repeatedly without caching (unlike `paths.RepoRoot()` which does cache). Consolidating those 7 calls down to 1-2 would cut ~50ms on fast machines and potentially seconds on slow ones.

  └─ settings.Load() → paths.RepoRoot()               (CACHED — no spawn)

CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints()              (manual_commit_session.go:153)
  ├─ GetWorktreePath()                                 (strategy/common.go:661)
  │    └─ ④ git rev-parse --show-toplevel              (NOT cached — different from paths.RepoRoot)
  ├─ OpenRepository()                                  (strategy/common.go:560)
  │    └─ GetWorktreePath()                            (strategy/common.go:661)
  │         └─ ⑤ git rev-parse --show-toplevel
  ├─ listAllSessionStates()                            (manual_commit_session.go:60)
  │    ├─ getStateStore() → NewStateStore()
  │    │    └─ GetGitCommonDir()                       (strategy/common.go:639)
  │    │         └─ ⑥ git rev-parse --git-common-dir
  │    └─ OpenRepository()                             (strategy/common.go:560)
  │         └─ GetWorktreePath()
  │              └─ ⑦ git rev-parse --show-toplevel

LoadSessionState()                                     (session_state.go:41)
  └─ getSessionStateDir() → GetGitCommonDir()          (strategy/common.go:639)
       └─ ⑧ git rev-parse --git-common-dir

SaveSessionState()                                     (session_state.go:69)
  └─ getSessionStateDir() → GetGitCommonDir()
       └─ ⑨ git rev-parse --git-common-dir
```

**Summary:**

| # | Call | Location | Cacheable? |
|---|---|---|---|
| ① | `--git-common-dir` | `session/state.go:372` | Yes — same per process |
| ② | `--show-toplevel` | `strategy/common.go:663` | Yes — same per process |
| ③ | `--show-toplevel` | `paths/paths.go:79` | Already cached |
| ④ | `--show-toplevel` | `strategy/common.go:663` | Yes — duplicate of ② |
| ⑤ | `--show-toplevel` | `strategy/common.go:663` | Yes — duplicate of ② |
| ⑥ | `--git-common-dir` | `strategy/common.go:641` | Yes — duplicate of ① |
| ⑦ | `--show-toplevel` | `strategy/common.go:663` | Yes — duplicate of ② |
| ⑧ | `--git-common-dir` | `session/state.go:372` | Yes — duplicate of ① |
| ⑨ | `--git-common-dir` | `session/state.go:372` | Yes — duplicate of ① |

There are two distinct functions that need caching:
- **`strategy.GetWorktreePath()`** at `strategy/common.go:661` — called 4x, never cached
- **`session.getGitCommonDir()`** at `session/state.go:370` + **`strategy.GetGitCommonDir()`** at `strategy/common.go:639` — called 4x total across two packages, never cached

With caching, the 9 spawns collapse to **3** (one `--show-toplevel` via `paths.RepoRoot`, one via `strategy.GetWorktreePath`, one `--git-common-dir`). Or **2** if `GetWorktreePath` delegates to `paths.RepoRoot`.

   531→	default:

   535→	}
   536→

   538→}

   540→func generatePadding(prefix string, targetBytes int) string {
   541→	if len(prefix) >= targetBytes {

   544→	padding := strings.Repeat("Lorem ipsum dolor sit amet. ", (targetBytes-len(prefix))/28+1)

   547→		return result[:targetBytes]
   548→	}
   549→	return result

  "new_string": "// SeedBranches creates N branches pointing at the current HEAD.\n// The branches are named with the given prefix (e.g., \"feature/bench-\" → \"feature/bench-000\").\n// This simulates a repo with many refs, which affects go-git ref scanning performance.\nfunc (br *BenchRepo) SeedBranches(b *testing.B, prefix string, count int) {\n\tb.Helper()\n\theadHash := plumbing.NewHash(br.HeadHash)\n\tfor i := range count {\n\t\tname := fmt.Sprintf(\"%s%03d\", prefix, i)\n\t\tref := plumbing.NewHashReference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(name), headHash)\n\t\tif err := br.Repo.Storer.SetReference(ref); err != nil {\n\t\t\tb.Fatalf(\"create branch %s: %v\", name, err)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// PackRefs runs `git pack-refs --all` to simulate a real repo where most refs\n// are in the packed-refs file. Large repos almost always have packed refs.\nfunc (br *BenchRepo) PackRefs(b *testing.B) {\n\tb.Helper()\n\tcmd := exec.CommandContext(context.Background(), \"git\", \"pack-refs\", \"--all\")\n\tcmd.Dir = br.Dir\n\tif output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {\n\t\tb.Fatalf(\"git pack-refs: %v\\n%s\", err, output)\n\t}\n}\n\n// SeedGitObjects creates loose git objects to bloat .git/objects/.\n// Each call creates N blob objects via `git hash-object -w`.\n// After seeding, runs `git gc` to pack them into a packfile (realistic).\nfunc (br *BenchRepo) SeedGitObjects(b *testing.B, count int) {\n\tb.Helper()\n\n\tfor i := range count {\n\t\tcontent := GenerateFileContent(i, 4096)\n\t\tcmd := exec.CommandContext(context.Background(), \"git\", \"hash-object\", \"-w\", \"--stdin\")\n\t\tcmd.Dir = br.Dir\n\t\tcmd.Stdin = strings.NewReader(content)\n\t\tif output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {\n\t\t\tb.Fatalf(\"git hash-object %d: %v\\n%s\", i, err, output)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Pack into a packfile like a real repo\n\tgc := exec.CommandContext(context.Background(), \"git\", \"gc\", \"--quiet\")\n\tgc.Dir = br.Dir\n\tif output, err := gc.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {\n\t\tb.Fatalf(\"git gc: %v\\n%s\", err, output)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc generatePadding(prefix string, targetBytes int) string {"
